# Changelog

This file records user-visible changes before they are committed. New work should
be added here first, then marked `Committed` or `Pushed` after the git action.

## Unreleased

- 2026-07-07 23:21 PST +0800 - Outreach tasks fail closed with receipts or blockers
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/types/kanban.ts`, `src/lib/services/kanban/kanban-failure-classification.ts`, `src/lib/services/kanban/local-kanban-store.ts`, `src/lib/services/kanban/outreach-safeguards.ts`, `scripts/test-outreach-runtime-safeguards.mjs`, `package.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: Website Outreach Agency and other company outreach revenue tasks now have runtime safeguards against silent failure. HTTP 429 / rate-limit / usage-limit errors classify as `rate-limit` and use the normal retry path while attempts remain. Completing or patching an outreach task to `done` now requires a final response that explicitly declares `Status: sent` with prospect, receipt, and verification fields, or `Status: blocked` with a concrete blocker/action-needed field and evidence. Empty/no-final responses and raw runtime-failure text fail closed into Needs You instead of closing the card as done, and the board records a `task.outreach-evidence-blocked` event receipt.
  - Verification: `pnpm test:outreach-runtime-safeguards` passed, covering 429 retry to Ready, empty final-response guard to Needs You, patch-to-done rejection without a sent receipt, successful sent completion with receipt, blocked completion requiring blocker evidence, and the Work Board guard event. Focused ESLint passed for the changed kanban services and regression script. Focused `git diff --check` passed for the outreach safeguard files, package script, and changelog.
  - Intended commit message: `kanban: require outreach receipts or blockers`

- 2026-07-07 23:07 PST +0800 - Shared env self-heals keys that were written without a sync timestamp
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `scripts/hive-env-add`, `scripts/test-env-sync-queue.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: A shared-hive-env key could land in `~/.hivemindos/.env` with no per-key `updatedAt` entry in `.env.meta.json` whenever something wrote it outside the sanctioned `apply_update` path (a raw file edit, an out-of-repo tool, an older writer). `pull_reconcile` then reads that key's age as `0.0`, treats it as `UnknownAge`, and refuses to overwrite it (`hive-env-add:1591-1594`), while `export_json` serves it with `updatedAt=0` (`:1719`) so peers won't adopt it either — the key is frozen out of sync in BOTH directions and silently, permanently diverges the moment anyone edits it on one machine. It only looks fine while every machine holds the identical value (the equal-value short-circuit at `:1587-1588`). New self-healing step: `backfill_missing_meta()` stamps any key present in the env file but absent from meta, run FIRST inside `--sync-maintenance` (so the stamped keys take part in the same cycle's pull/push) and exposed as a standalone `--backfill-meta [--dry-run]`. It only fills gaps — never rewrites an existing stamp, never resurrects a tombstone (removed keys keep their meta entry and are absent from the values), never touches values. Kill switch `HIVE_ENV_BACKFILL_META=0`. This is the durable root fix so no key can strand however it was written, replacing a one-off manual re-stamp. Live This Mac audit found 22 real unstamped keys (AGENTMAIL_API_KEY, CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN, GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY, CAL_API_KEY, …) all in this freeze trap; a fleet hash-compare confirmed their values are byte-identical on This Mac, NYC, and hel1-2, so the backfill converges the missing stamps with zero value churn.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-env-sync-queue.mjs` passes, including new scenario 9 which proves the before/after — with `HIVE_ENV_BACKFILL_META=0` an unstamped key IS reported in `pull.skippedUnknownAge` and stays unstamped; with the backfill on it is stamped, is NO LONGER in `skippedUnknownAge`, and gains a real numeric meta timestamp; a second `--backfill-meta` is idempotent (keeps the original stamp). `python3 -c ast.parse` confirms the script parses; `--help` shows the new flag. A read-only `--backfill-meta --dry-run` against the real env reported the 22 gap keys (OUTREACH_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS is NOT among them — it has been stamped since 2026-07-02). Not yet applied to the live env: This Mac's collector will run the new backfill on its next `--sync-maintenance` cycle (the `~/.local/bin/hive-env-add` wrapper execs this checkout, so the change is live locally); hel1-2/NYC get it after they `git pull`. Kill switch and dry-run verified; no value was read, printed, or mutated.
  - Intended commit message: `hive-env: self-heal keys written without a sync timestamp`

- 2026-07-07 23:01 PST +0800 - Alerts rebuilt as the two-column Desktop design + a shared approve/reject queue
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/features/approvals/` (new shared module), `src/features/notifications/NotificationsPanel.tsx`, `src/app/notifications.module.css`, `src/lib/services/wallet/spend-approvals.ts`, `src/app/api/wallet/approvals/route.ts`, `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/{types,mappers,Cockpit,ZeroHumanCompanies,ZeroHumanCompaniesView}.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: The Alerts view is rebuilt as the two-column Desktop design from the redesign handoff: a header ("What needs attention" / "Alerts" with Refresh + Mark-all-read — no Dark/Light toggle, per prior request), a left column with the filter tabs + the full-height alert list, and a right rail with three panels — Overview (unread / need-attention / decisions), "Review first" (the real pending spend-approval queue), and Autonomy (the review-mode cards + urgent-messaging toggle, moved out of the old settings drawer). "Review first" and the company approvals section now share one module: `src/features/approvals/` — a `useSpendApprovals` hook (fetch pending + decide), an `ApprovalCard`, a shared `ApproveRejectModal` that takes an optional note/change, and an `ApprovalReviewCard` that wires them together. Approve/Reject open that modal and post the decision — with the note — to the real `/api/wallet/approvals` backend (extended to persist a `decisionNote` so a change request / caveat rides back to the agent). Zero Human Companies now renders the same shared card + modal (its `Approval` type and `mapApproval` delegate to the shared model), so both surfaces are one code path (DRY). Also fixed: the list previously stopped after the first page because 38 look-alike escalations collapse into one `+N` cluster, leaving the tall list underfilled with nothing to scroll — it now auto-loads the next page until the viewport fills (plus the existing scroll-to-load).
  - Verification: full-project `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` reports 0 errors; `eslint` clean across all 10 changed files + the 6 new module files; `node scripts/test-notification-{actions,clustering,resolution}.mjs` all pass. Rendered the two-column layout and the approve/reject modal with the real stylesheets (both notifications + approvals CSS modules) against JSX-mirroring markup — header, tabs + "N total", the full-height list, the Overview / Review-first (real ApprovalCards) / Autonomy rail, and the modal (title, requester/kind/risk/amount, reason, note textarea, Reject/Approve) all render correctly. File sizes all under 1500 (Cockpit shrank after removing its local ApprovalCard). Not verified through the authed live dashboard (that's the user's session); the approval decide path is exercised against the real `/api/wallet/approvals` route by the shared hook. Pre-existing/concurrent gate failures unrelated to this change: `check-sizes` (three legacy files), `guard-browser-durable-state` (`WalletsView.tsx`, byte-identical to HEAD), and `guard-hive-action-route-drift` (7 unrelated routes) — none are files this change touched; the edited `wallet/approvals` route is not flagged. Follow-up fixes: (1) the alert list capped its scroll (couldn't reach past what fit) because the new two-column `.twoCol` grid only set `grid-template-columns` — its implicit row was `auto` (content-sized), so `.listScroll` never got a bounded height; added `grid-template-rows: minmax(0, 1fr)` so the list is a real bounded scroll region — verified headlessly at 1400×800 with 30 rows (`.listScroll` clientHeight 584px, scrollHeight 1909px, scrollable). (2) Rows expanded on `:hover` as the pointer passed over them mid-scroll (janky) — the panel now toggles a `scrolling` class on the scroll container (ref-based, no re-render) that sets `pointer-events: none` on rows while scrolling and clears it 160ms after the scroll goes idle; keyboard focus-within expansion is unaffected. (3) The look-alike clustering was over-collapsing: 160 loaded notifications showed as ~18 rows because the cluster key was title+source+priority+kind (ignoring body), so distinct escalations for different tasks all merged. Per owner preference, `notificationClusterKey` now includes the body, so only byte-identical cards dedup and every distinct alert gets its own row; updated `scripts/test-notification-clustering.mjs` to assert the new intent (18 different-task escalations → 18 rows; 5 identical repeats → one paged stack) — the suite passes. `notification-clustering.ts` is only consumed by the alerts panel, so the change is contained.
  - Intended commit message: `alerts: two-column desktop redesign + shared spend-approval queue`

  - Intended commit message: `miroshark: expect $1.20 x402 simulation price`

- 2026-07-07 22:53 PST +0800 - Companies auto-pause autonomy on approval backpressure
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/types/company.ts`, `src/lib/services/companies-store.ts`, `src/lib/services/company-autonomy-driver.ts`, `src/app/api/companies/route.ts`, `scripts/test-company-autonomy.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: A Zero Human Company can now auto-pause its own autonomy driver when too many items are already waiting on a human, so the Needs You lane can't silently pile up to hundreds of unreviewed approvals. New optional per-company config `autonomyPause` (`maxWaitingOnHuman`, `countMode` "all" | "deliverable-kinds", `deliverableKinds`): once the count of the company's `needs-human` tasks reaches the threshold, the driver stops planning NEW work and fires one low-severity, in-app, per-company "N waiting on you" nudge. It is fully reversible and self-resuming — in-flight work still finishes, and dispatch restarts on the next tick once the human clears enough to drop back under the threshold (no Stop button, no `frozen` kill switch). Default `countMode` is "all" (every waiting item); "deliverable-kinds" counts only `needs-human` tasks carrying a deliverable of a chosen kind (e.g. just website approvals), and an empty kind list safely falls back to counting all so a mode can never silently count zero. Config is persisted through the replicated company definition and settable via `POST /api/companies` (`action: "upsert"`, `autonomyPause`). Re-notify cadence is `HIVEMINDOS_COMPANY_PAUSE_NUDGE_TTL_MS` (default 6h).
  - Verification: Backend verified in isolation against a clean-HEAD `git worktree` (the live working tree carries unrelated concurrent-session changes to the kanban/deliverable path that make one pre-existing deliverable-extraction assertion fail — reproduced by baselining clean HEAD: `company autonomy suite passed`, EXIT 0). My source + my new tests on that clean worktree: `company autonomy suite passed`, EXIT 0. New hermetic coverage in `scripts/test-company-autonomy.mjs` exercises `countCompanyWaitingOnHuman` for all-mode counting, deliverable-kinds filtering, empty-kinds fallback-to-all, status exclusion (only `needs-human` counts), and cross-company isolation — all pass. Filtered `pnpm typecheck` reports no diagnostics in any touched file (`types/company.ts`, `companies-store.ts`, `company-autonomy-driver.ts`, `api/companies/route.ts`). NOT done: the ZHC dashboard edit-modal control was deliberately deferred — the ZHC view files (`types.ts`, `Modals.tsx`, `mappers.ts`, `ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx`) are under active concurrent-session editing right now, so touching them would risk clobbering that uncommitted work; the setting is reachable through the companies API in the meantime. No company definition was written (WEBS threshold not set) — that is a fleet-replicated vault change awaiting the user's go-ahead. Driver pause behavior was not exercised end-to-end against a live running driver.
  - Intended commit message: `companies: auto-pause autonomy on approval backpressure`

- 2026-07-07 21:29 PST +0800 - Messaging Channels discovers Hermes-delivered chat targets
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/types/messaging-channels.ts`, `src/lib/services/messaging/channels.ts`, `src/app/api/messaging-channels/route.ts`, `src/features/dashboard/views/MessagingChannelsPanel.tsx`, `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`, `scripts/lib/collector-messaging-channels.mjs`, `scripts/test-messaging-channels-bridge.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: Messaging Channels no longer depends only on the shared-vault registry. The dashboard now asks the runtime roster for Hermes messaging targets and merges discovered Telegram/Discord/Slack destinations into the same channel list as vault-managed channels. Hermes-discovered channels are marked live/read-only, sent through `hermes send`, and kept out of the vault registry so runtime state does not overwrite durable user-managed channels. Collectors also expose `/messaging-channels` so remote machines can list and send through their own local Hermes targets once their collector process is updated.
  - Verification: `pnpm exec tsx scripts/test-messaging-channels-bridge.mjs` passed; `node --check scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs && node --check scripts/lib/collector-messaging-channels.mjs` passed; focused ESLint on the messaging service, API route, panel, bridge module, and regression script passed; focused `tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` diagnostic filter reported no touched-file errors; `git diff --check -- ...` passed for the touched messaging files. A direct service smoke against this Mac's Hermes target returned 1 read-only Telegram channel with Hermes delivery, and a temporary collector on `127.0.0.1:5899` returned the same bridged Telegram channel from `/messaging-channels`. The dashboard UI was not live-clicked, and NYC/Ubuntu collectors were not updated or restarted in this change.
  - Intended commit message: `messaging: bridge Hermes runtime channels into dashboard`

- 2026-07-07 21:25 PST +0800 - Tauri OAuth launches leave the dashboard in place
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/features/integrations/IntegrationsView.tsx`, `src/features/integrations/integrations-view-helpers.ts`, `src/features/integrations/ConnectionsPanel.tsx`, `src/app/api/integrations/google/oauth/start/route.ts`, `src/app/api/integrations/x-managed/desktop-return/route.ts`, `src/app/api/integrations/x-managed/desktop-return-pending/route.ts`, `src/lib/services/managed-x-api-client.ts`, `src/lib/services/managed-x-return.ts`, `src/lib/services/managed-x-desktop-return-store.ts`, `src/proxy.ts`, `src-tauri/src/desktop_navigation.rs`, `scripts/test-x-mcp-integration.mjs`, `scripts/test-tauri-oauth-external-browser.mjs`
  - Summary: MCP managed X sign-in and Google app sign-in now open the provider OAuth page in the system browser instead of replacing the Tauri desktop window. Google sign-in starts from an authenticated dashboard request that returns the provider authorization URL, and the Google callback is allowed to complete through signed OAuth state even when the external browser does not carry the dashboard session cookie. Managed X first-connect OAuth now requests only `tweet.read users.read tweet.write offline.access` by default, avoiding the broad DM/media/bookmark/list/follow/mute/block scopes that can make X reject the grant before the account is connected. Managed X now returns through a browser-safe local receipt page that records the OAuth result without navigating Safari to a custom `hivemindos://` address; the authenticated Tauri MCP panel polls for that receipt, refreshes the connected-account state, and keeps the desktop dashboard in place instead of landing on the locked browser dashboard.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-tauri-oauth-external-browser.mjs && node scripts/test-x-mcp-integration.mjs` passed after replacing the custom-scheme bounce with the browser-safe receipt and polling route; focused OAuth ESLint passed for the touched TS/TSX/API/test files; filtered `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` reported no touched-file diagnostics, with the run exiting 2 only on the unrelated concurrent `src/features/notifications/NotificationsPanel.tsx(255,63)` optional amount error; `cargo check --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml` passed; `git diff --check -- ...` passed for the touched OAuth files, native handler, tests, and changelog. A real unauthenticated local probe of `http://127.0.0.1:5021/api/integrations/x-managed/desktop-return?...` returned HTTP 200 with no locked dashboard, no `hivemindos://` deep link, no `window.location.href` auto-redirect, and the receipt copy present. A token-authenticated probe of `/api/integrations/x-managed/desktop-return-pending` returned the stored `codex_probe` receipt, while the same pending route without auth returned HTTP 401. Earlier real local API probe against `/api/integrations/x-managed` returned an X authorization URL with scope `tweet.read users.read tweet.write offline.access` and hosted callback `hivemindos-x-api-gateway.hivemindos.workers.dev`. `cargo fmt --check --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml` could not run because the active stable Rust toolchain is missing `rustfmt`.

Full-project `tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` reported 0 errors. `node scripts/test-notification-actions.mjs`, `node scripts/test-notification-clustering.mjs`, and `node scripts/test-notification-resolution.mjs` all passed (the pure action/cluster/resolution modules the panel consumes are unchanged). `node scripts/guard-browser-durable-state.mjs` passed (the panel uses only React state — no browser durable storage). File sizes: `NotificationsPanel.tsx` 559 lines, `notifications.module.css` 846 lines (both well under 1500); `node scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs` remains red only on pre-existing/concurrent oversized files outside this change (`scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`, `src/app/fleet.module.css`, `src/app/kanban-board.module.css`, all byte-identical to HEAD). Visual: rendered the real `notifications.module.css` against markup mirroring the component's JSX (via a static harness) and screenshotted both themes — header/eyebrow/theme-toggle, count summary with the Mark-all-read action, the two-icon header (refresh + settings), filter tabs with counts, mode indicator, open settings drawer, sticky day headers, priority-colored glyphs, unread dots, `+N` cluster badge, an expanded row's Discuss/Open/Read actions, priority/resolved pills, and the footer all render correctly in dark and hive-light. The React data-flow itself is covered by the clean typecheck + passing unit suites rather than an authed live-data render. A 16-agent adversarial review (4 dimensions — wiring / fidelity / a11y+regression / theme+runtime — each finding independently verified) confirmed the data wiring and theme plumbing are correct and rejected several false positives; it surfaced 4 real defects, all addressed: (1) an undesigned third header icon — "Mark all read" moved into the count summary so the header matches the mockup's two buttons; (2) the cluster pager position was keyed on the mutable title-derived cluster key — re-keyed on the stable anchor notification id so a refresh no longer snaps the reader back to 1/N; (3) the expand ceiling was 520px vs the mockup's 460px — set to 460px after measuring the tallest real card (a Hermes sign-out alert with a code block + pager + full action row) at ~268px via headless Playwright, so expanded content never clips; (4) the collapsed-row action buttons keep `visibility:hidden` intentionally — it removes them from the tab order so keyboard users don't wade through a wall of invisible buttons, and focusing the row reveals them. eslint (clean) and the full typecheck (0 errors) were re-run after the fixes. Follow-up per user request: removed the in-view Dark/Light toggle entirely (the view still follows the app's global theme via the CSS `[data-theme="hive-light"]` mirror) and reverted the `theme`/`onToggleTheme` prop plumbing from `NotificationsPanel`/`UtilityPanels`/`DashboardApp`; added `32px` top padding to the panel so it no longer sits flush against the top; and removed the in-panel "HivemindOS / Alerts" eyebrow+title header (redundant with the dashboard's own chrome) so the card is now the top element — the section keeps `aria-label="Alerts"` for its accessible name. eslint clean and full typecheck 0 errors after this follow-up; verified via a real-CSS render that the panel has no toggle and no eyebrow/title header, and the panel top padding is 32px. Second follow-up (layout felt "off"): the panel was centering a narrow 680px column (`margin: 0 auto`) inside the ~1560px, left-hugging `.commandMain`, so it floated mid-content with large voids on both sides; changed it to left-hug the nav rail (`margin: 0`) at `max-width: 860px` with a 24px side gutter, matching the dashboard's flush-left convention — compared A/B/C/D width+alignment variants rendered at the real 1500px content width and picked the left-aligned 860px column, which reads as a proper panel and keeps rows compact/readable (full titles no longer truncate). CSS-only; eslint clean. (The 2 `tsc` errors now present are in `src/app/api/messaging-channels/route.ts`, a concurrently-edited file outside this change — my files typecheck clean.) Third follow-up: made the list a full-height infinite scroller instead of a short card that stopped at the first page. The panel now fills the content height (`height: 100%`; overrides the global `.tabPanel` `overflow:auto` with a `.notificationsPanel:global(.tabPanel){overflow:hidden}` rule — the height chain resolves because `.commandShell` is `100dvh` and the route error boundary returns children directly, so the section is a definite-height child of `.commandMain`), the card fills it (`flex:1; min-height:0`), and a new inner `.listScroll` region (`flex:1; min-height:0; overflow-y:auto`) owns scrolling: the header, filter tabs, status, and footer stay fixed while the alert list scrolls. Restored infinite-load — an `onScroll` handler on `.listScroll` calls `onRefresh({append:true})` within 320px of the bottom; the "Load more" button is kept as an explicit/keyboard fallback. Verified headlessly: panel fills the 860px viewport, the list region is a real scroll container (1424px content in a 588px window, `scrollable:true`), and a scrolled render confirmed the header/tabs/footer stay pinned while only the list moves. eslint clean; my files typecheck clean.
  - Intended commit message: `alerts: rebuild notifications view as a compact list`

- 2026-07-07 19:07 PST +0800 - OpenAI voice Preview no longer fails with "Invalid URL (POST /v1/audio/speech)"
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/phone/cloud-voice-transports.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: The Calls-panel voice Preview button hit `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/audio/speech` with whatever model the selected runtime transport used. When the selected runtime was the OpenAI realtime hybrid (the default for legacy/unknown voice profiles), that model was `gpt-realtime` — a realtime model the TTS-only speech endpoint rejects with the misleading `Invalid URL (POST /v1/audio/speech)` error. `synthesizeVoicePreview` only fell back to a TTS model when the passed model was empty, so the non-empty realtime id sailed through and failed. The preview now coerces any model outside the TTS-capable set (`gpt-4o-mini-tts`, `tts-1`, `tts-1-hd`) to `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, so previewing an OpenAI voice works regardless of which runtime kind (realtime hybrid or cloud TTS) is selected. Explicit valid TTS model overrides are preserved.
  - Verification: Reproduced the exact user error against the live OpenAI API by POSTing `model:"gpt-realtime"` to `/v1/audio/speech` — returned HTTP 404 with the byte-identical `{ "detail": {...}, "error": {...} }` "Invalid URL" body. After the fix, a script mirroring the edited coercion + fetch returned HTTP 200 with real PCM audio for the previously-failing inputs (`gpt-realtime`→`gpt-4o-mini-tts` with voices `alloy`/`verse`), and preserved explicit `tts-1`. Confirmed the OpenAI realtime-only voices `cedar`/`marin` render fine on `gpt-4o-mini-tts`. `pnpm typecheck` (tsc --noEmit) exited 0. Not verified through the running dashboard UI (the /api surface is auth-gated and the change was proven against the real upstream endpoint instead).
  - Intended commit message: `calls: fix OpenAI voice preview using realtime model on TTS endpoint`

  - Status: Uncommitted

  - Summary: Schedules are now machine-scoped. A schedule is pinned to its designated machine by default; a new **Run on all machines** toggle (off by default) in the create/edit modal opts into fleet-wide fan-out. Phase 1 (in-repo, tested): `runOnAllMachines` field round-tripped through the vault snapshot; machine-aware duplicate detection collapses a fan-out's per-machine copies into one (so an intended all-machines loop isn't flagged as duplicates) while accidental cross-machine same-name loops still surface; `dropOrphanScheduleRows` prunes rows whose owning identity is gone from the fleet (kills the `resume-anima` dead-hostname ghost); `reconcileReachedOwners` prunes deleted crons for machines whose collector was reached this import (kills the emerson "Daily Hive Pulse" ghost — its cron `be00d36360d6` was verified absent from the Ubuntu box's live jobs.json, which holds 9 unrelated Obsidian crons, so it was a stale row, not a live duplicate). Phase 2: a cron-write primitive (`hermes-cron-doc` pure mutations + `cron-write` local-fs/remote-linkd-shell transport + `/api/scheduler/cron-write`) that edits a machine's `~/.hermes/cron/jobs.json` with backup + atomic write. Phase 3: `/api/scheduler/replicate` fans a schedule's cron out to every machine (or strips stray copies off non-designated machines), wired to fire from create/edit only when replication is on.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` = 100/103 — three new hermetic suites green (`test:schedule-health` +orphan-purge/machine-aware/reconcile, `test:hermes-cron-doc`, `test:schedule-replication`) plus typecheck; the 3 red steps (`check-sizes`, `guard:hive-action-route-drift`, `test:agent-providers`) are pre-existing concurrent-session failures (baselined at clean HEAD 8d43de69), none in my files. `use-scheduler-controller.tsx` = 1495 lines (< 1500). Cron-write LOCAL path verified end-to-end through the HTTP route against a temp home (set-enabled flipped one job, other untouched, backup written). Remote path: the linkd-shell READ verified against the live Ubuntu box (clean parse of its 9-job jobs.json after fixing a marker-newline bug); the remote WRITE shares the verified read + a defensive backup/atomic write but is NOT yet exercised against a real remote machine — validate before relying on fleet fan-out. NOT live-clicked in the browser (auth-gated Tauri).
  - Intended commit message: `schedules: machine-scoping + run-on-all-machines replication + dead-cron reconciliation`

- 2026-07-07 15:43 PST +0800 - Platform-wide deliverable-acceptance gate stops any company shipping placeholder outward work
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/deliverables/acceptance-contracts.ts` (new), `src/lib/services/deliverables/deliverable-acceptance.ts` (new), `src/lib/services/deliverables/content-fetcher.ts` (new), `src/lib/services/loops/loop-runner.ts`, `src/lib/services/queen-bee/autonomous-worker.ts`, `src/lib/services/company-email-qa.ts`, `scripts/test-deliverable-acceptance.mjs` (new), `package.json`, `scripts/test-gate.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Summary: New typed "Deliverable Acceptance Contracts" registry — a capability matrix keyed by deliverable KIND (restaurant website preview, generic web page, outreach email) — defines, platform-wide, what makes an outward deliverable real vs. a placeholder/wireframe skeleton, generalizing the 2026-07-07 failure where a restaurant preview shipped to a live prospect with fake "menu" items ("Menu / offer clarity"), no prices, an empty menu category, and a single stock hero. A pure, dependency-injected orchestrator fetches the customer-facing URL(s) a worker/email presents and runs the matching contract; an affirmative rejection is a HARD-fail receipt that blocks loop completion (→ needs-human), exactly like the existing live-URL integrity gate, so this covers EVERY company that completes work through the Queen Bee autonomous-worker path — not just one company. A second enforcement point in the company email-QA scan flags any live preview/offer link in sent outreach whose page fails its acceptance contract. Fail-closed on affirmative rejection ONLY: unfetchable, reserved/mock, transactional (/paid,/checkout,/book), or ambiguous content abstains and never blocks, so the gate never cries wolf. Kill-switch `QUEEN_BEE_DELIVERABLE_ACCEPTANCE=0`. Hardened after an adversarial multi-agent review of the diff (14 findings triaged, 7 acted on): dropped a standalone "too-thin menu" rule and a "missing-imagery" rule (both false-positive vectors on legit small/plain menus); the wireframe-label rule now needs a MAJORITY of items (one oddly-named real dish can't fail a good menu); the generic near-empty check defers to the restaurant contract and requires no media; and the content-URL shape was unified with the live-URL gate's deploy hosts (.onrender/.fly/.firebaseapp) so coverage doesn't diverge, with deliverables fetched concurrently under a cap of 6.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-deliverable-acceptance.mjs` passed (13 groups: contracts reject Giuseppe HTML + JSON config; pass a real priced menu, a small/tasting menu, an 8-item menu with one wireframe-ish dish name, and an image-driven splash; don't false-fail a real non-restaurant page; catch lorem-ipsum/near-empty/unfilled-merge-field; orchestrator fails closed on affirmative rejection only, doesn't fetch reserved/transactional URLs, and DOES catch a deploy-host (fly.dev) preview; and the loop-runner emits a hard-fail receipt that `loopCompletionBlock` blocks while a real menu passes). Full gate `pnpm test`: baseline 97/100 (3 pre-existing red from concurrent uncommitted work: `check-sizes`, `guard:hive-action-route-drift`, `test:agent-providers`) → after 98/101 with the SAME 3 red and the new `test:deliverable-acceptance` passing (no regressions; typecheck + eslint green). `eslint --max-warnings=0` on all touched files passed. NOT runtime-verified against a live fleet pickup or a real send — blocking behavior is proven via the `runLoopGates`+`loopCompletionBlock` unit path using Giuseppe's real config/markup shapes, not observed in a running company driver.
  - Intended commit message: `deliverables: platform-wide acceptance gate blocks placeholder outward work`

- 2026-07-07 12:05 PST +0800 - Schedules panel: dedupe machine-identity forks, stop false "silently dead" warnings, and add per-warning banner dismiss
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/features/dashboard/schedule-health.ts`, `src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-scheduler-controller.tsx`, `src/lib/services/runtime-adapters/hermes.ts`, `src/features/dashboard/views/SchedulerPanel.tsx`, `scripts/test-schedule-health.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`
  - Banner dismiss: the loop-health banner now has a per-warning dismiss (X) button plus an "N dismissed · Show" restore affordance. Dismissals persist through the dashboard state service (`hivemindos.scheduleHealthDismissed.v1`, never localStorage), keyed by `scheduleHealthWarningKey` (kind + scheduleIds), so an acknowledged warning stays hidden while a genuinely new one still surfaces; stale keys prune to live warnings on each dismiss. Pure `scheduleHealthWarningKey`/`visibleScheduleHealthWarnings` are single-sourced in schedule-health.ts and covered by the hermetic suite.
  - Operational cleanup (not committed code): removed two ghost schedule rows pointing at deleted Hermes crons (verified absent from live collector `127.0.0.1:8787/schedules`, which reports only the real pulse `6ad8903df25a`) — "Daily Hive Pulse" (superseded `emerson-c023b7`, job `be00d36360d6`) and "resume-anima-civitai-download" (dead `…-333970-local`, job `231a093bddfc`). Vault mirrors archived to `Operations/Vault Migrations/2026-07-07-ghost-schedule-cleanup/` with rollback MANIFEST; state backed up to `~/.hivemindos/dashboard-state.json.pre-ghost-cleanup.*.bak`. Fully effective after a dashboard reload.
  - Summary: Two Automations/Schedules bookkeeping bugs. (1) A machine's hostname churn (the NYC MacBook wore ≥5 hostnames) minted a fresh per-machine schedule id for the SAME underlying cron, so the dashboard accumulated one enabled "copy" per dead identity (5 "Mac memory pressure watchdog" rows, all sharing underlying Hermes job `567853dca5a5`; 2 "Daily Hive Pulse — Base & AI Agents"). New pure `scheduleLoopSignature`/`dedupeSchedulesByLoop` collapse forks by (runtime + underlying job id + normalized name), keeping the live (freshest-updated) row and carrying the newest run stamp forward; wired into both the runtime-import and shared-vault-merge paths so existing forks collapse on next refresh and future renames can't re-fork. (2) "enabled but no recorded runs / may be silently dead" fired on jobs that demonstrably run (memory-pressure watchdog → Telegram for weeks; Daily Hive Pulse → ran daily) because `lastRunAt` was never persisted: `schedulerSharedSnapshot` omitted it so every shared-vault round-trip dropped it, and the Hermes adapter only reported the coarse `jobs.json` file mtime. Now the snapshot serializes/deserializes `lastRunAt`/`lastStatus`/`lastSummary`, the merge keeps the newest run stamp, and the Hermes adapter surfaces each job's real `last_run_at` from `jobs.json`. Residual (named, not fixed): remote schedules imported via a collector `/schedules` still depend on that collector (the Go `linkd`/collector binary, out of this repo) reporting `last_run_at`.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-schedule-health.mjs` passed (added `dedupeSchedulesByLoop` coverage: 5 forked watchdog rows → 1 with run stamp preserved; plus dismiss coverage: `scheduleHealthWarningKey` stable, `visibleScheduleHealthWarnings` hides exactly the dismissed warning and keeps new ones). eslint clean on `SchedulerPanel.tsx` + `schedule-health.ts`. Typecheck passed in-gate; live tauri-next dev server `GET http://127.0.0.1:5021/` → HTTP 200 (panel HMR-compiled, no build error). `pnpm test` = 96/100; the 4 red steps are a moving target from ~108 concurrently-dirty files (baselined at clean HEAD 8d43de69: `check-sizes`/`agent-providers`/`hive-action-route-drift` fail there too; `dashboard-nav`↔`wallet-paid-models` flip between runs as other sessions edit them) — none are my files. `SchedulerPanel.tsx` 702 lines, `use-scheduler-controller.tsx` 1480 (< 1500). Ran real `computeScheduleHealthWarnings` over live state: `lastRunAt` non-null for 0/120. Ghost-row removal verified: 2 warnings → 0 after removal; backups `~/.hivemindos/dashboard-state.json.pre-schedule-dedup.20260707-115615.bak` and `…pre-ghost-cleanup.*.bak`. NOT live-clicked in a browser (auth-gated Tauri WKWebView + banner needs warnings present); dismiss logic proven hermetically instead.
  - Intended commit message: `schedules: dedupe hostname-forked rows + persist real last-run time`

  - Status: Uncommitted

  - Summary: Gemini Live realtime is now wired end-to-end and LIVE-VERIFIED against a real key. The Calls voice picker → dashboard-agent-call → `call-gateway` (new `mode:"gemini-live"` branch, reusing `buildByokAgentCallInstructions` + `toolsForVoiceToolBundle`) → `AgentsPanel` → `AgentCallModal` → the new `useDashboardGeminiLive` hook: it mints a short-lived token server-side (`/api/phone/cloud-voice`), opens the Gemini bidi WebSocket with `?access_token`, streams 16 kHz mic PCM up and plays the 24 kHz PCM Gemini returns, with server-side VAD/turn-taking, barge-in flush on `interrupted`, transcripts, and `ask_computer_agent` tool calls. Also: MULTI-KEY — the panel detects every hive-env name that can hold a Gemini key (`GEMINI_API_KEY` / `GOOGLE_AI_STUDIO_API_KEY` / `GOOGLE_API_KEY`) and, when more than one is set, shows a chooser; the pick persists as `voiceKeyEnv` and flows through to the token mint.
  - Live verification (real GOOGLE_AI_STUDIO_API_KEY in the hive env): (1) token-mint protocol — the documented body was WRONG (`POST /v1alpha/auth_tokens` rejects `liveConnectConstraints` — "Unknown name … Cannot find field"); fixed to the minimal body, which mints HTTP 200. (2) Full protocol via a `ws` Node test: token → WS `BidiGenerateContentConstrained` (v1alpha) → `setup` → text turn → **26,880 bytes of audio returned** + `turnComplete`. (3) The authenticated server route on a running dev server: `POST /api/phone/cloud-voice {action:"gemini-live-token"}` → `ok:true`, 76-char token, wsUrl, 16k in / 24k out. (4) `tsc --noEmit` clean (0 errors) across the whole client+gateway+UI wiring. So the Gemini matrix status was promoted `preview → available`.
  - NOT verified: the in-app mic voice exchange end-to-end (needs a real microphone on a device — the preview browser is headless and its throwaway-auth instance stalled on an unrelated "local desktop runtime" boot gate, so the multi-key chooser render + a real call were not screenshotted this run). - 2026-07-07 00:05 +0800 - Queen Bee dispatch is now load-aware ACROSS MACHINES: a burst fans out to idle fleet machines instead of piling onto one saturated box
  - Status: Committing now
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/queen-bee/router.ts` (`rankQueenBeeDelegates` now applies a MACHINE-level in-flight-load penalty — total working/ready tasks across a machine's agents, from the existing `assignments` signal — so an equally-capable agent on a busier machine is deprioritized; gentle by design [per-task 4, max 30] vs the +100 exact-class-match, so it breaks ties between equal agents without ever sending specialized work to a wrong-but-idle one; skipped when a machine is explicitly pinned, kill switch `QUEEN_BEE_MACHINE_LOAD_SPREADING=0`), `scripts/test-queen-bee-router-delegation.mjs` (idle VPS wins over a saturated This Mac for equal code work; kill switch restores machine-load-blind behavior; explicit pin still overrides; class match still dominates).
  - Why: Liam's standing demand — "if the machines' resources really look occupied, why don't we delegate to an agent on another machine? why is the full power of ALL my connected devices not being leveraged?" The router already spread across the least-busy AGENTS, but those could all sit on ONE saturated machine — so tasks timed out there instead of using idle fleet boxes. Adds the missing machine-level signal using data already in hand (no new plumbing, no circular import).
  - Verification: `test-queen-bee-router-delegation` + `test-queen-bee-routing` green; `tsc`/eslint clean on the router. NOT yet observed on a live saturated dispatch (board currently calm) — proven by hermetic scenarios against the real ranking function.
  - Intended commit message: `queen-bee router: load-aware cross-machine spreading (machine-level in-flight penalty)`

Traced through the code: `IssuesPanel` composes three async sources — the kanban review-issue cards (already skeleton-gated by `initialTasksLoading`), `SetupBlockerBand` (fast local env check), and `EmailQaBand`, which fires TWO requests: a fast deterministic GET then a slow deep-AI POST (`{ deep: true }`). The deterministic findings render immediately (the "2"); the AI findings land 5-10s later (the "3") with NO loading placeholder — the only prior indicator was a header spinner and a text line that showed just for clean companies. The 5-10s latency is the LLM content review (the one source in this panel with that signature — SetupBlockerBand and the kanban issues both land fast/atomically), so this band was the last uncovered wave. The "badge shows 5 but 2 cards" part is separate and by design: the tab badge counts `activeIssueCount` (all non-done kanban issues) while the panel body shows the needs-you subset + these bands.
  - Fix: when we KNOW the company has sent mail (`report.emailCount > 0`) and the AI pass is still running, render a shape-matched skeleton card — as a trailing placeholder below the deterministic findings, or as the whole band's content when the deterministic pass was clean. Gated on `emailCount > 0` so companies with no email crew never flash an email-QA skeleton (and it also fixes a pre-existing minor bug where the old "Reviewing sent emails…" line showed for 5-10s even on companies that never sent email). `SetupBlockerBand` deliberately left alone — its fetch is fast and additive-empty in the common case, so a skeleton there would flash-then-vanish on every company (net negative).
  - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (whole-project, contention-free run — clean); `eslint` exit 0 on `EmailQaBand.tsx`. NOT verified live — the transient AI-review window needs the running dashboard on a company with sent mail; the dev server won't stabilize under the current concurrent-session churn. The one claim I'd most expect to need a live check: that on WEBS the trailing skeleton actually bridges the deterministic→AI gap rather than flashing.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: skeleton for the email-QA deep-AI second wave so findings don't silently pop in`

  - Summary: Live 20:00 incident — Liam typed "hi"; telemetry shows a 4-round loop (round 1: 83-char greeting + a read_work_board call; rounds 2-3: more read-only tool calls; round 4: forced-prose 187-char answer). The create_hive_task gate (18:30) correctly stopped the unilateral task, but Scout still tool-spun on a bare greeting and produced a SECOND paragraph — the "two messages back to back". Fix (grounded by a 4-agent read-only investigation workflow + adversarial skeptic): a whole-message-anchored greeting detector forces the FIRST round to answer with NO tools, so a greeting is one clean reply. The regex is anchored end-to-end with a CLOSED trailing-token set — a greeting that prefixes a real ask ("hey queen, is HermesMain down?", "gm, swap 1 usdc to eth", "thanks, now open my wallets", "how are you doing on the collector fix?") does NOT match and still runs its mandatory tools; affirmatives (yes/ok/sure) are excluded so they don't collide with the create_hive_task authorization path. The skeptic also caught a real gap I'd introduced: `stripLeakedToolCallMarkup` ran ONLY on the buffered/blocking path (which Scout uses via the streaming:false gateway, so Scout was already covered), while the SSE streaming path forwarded raw deltas and returned unscrubbed `finalizeQueenChatStream` content — so a streaming brain (catalog/OpenAI lane) that leaked would flash raw `<|tool_call|>` tokens. Now the streaming path stops forwarding deltas once a marker appears and scrubs the finalized content + assistant message.
  - Verification: `isTrivialConversationalTurn` unit-checked 66/66 via the real TS import (incl. chained greetings and traps like "how are the companies doing today" → keeps tools, "how are you doing on the collector fix?" → keeps tools); `node scripts/test-queen-chat-stream.mjs` PASS with the new contract block (greetings suppress, greeting+ask keeps tools, affirmatives excluded); eslint 0 on all four files; full `pnpm test` re-running at write time. The unilaterally-created task `t_mr91zxmy_3zi39` from the 18:04 incident is still on the board (Liam decides keep/delete).
  - Intended commit message: `queen typed chat: force no-tools on bare greetings; close streaming-path tool-call-markup scrub gap`

- 2026-07-06 20:05 +0800 - Shared-env sync now self-heals strands: periodic maintenance PUSHES keys a peer is missing, not just pulls (root-cause fix for "should be automatic") — IN-REPO, NOT DEPLOYED
  - Status: Uncommitted (needs a per-machine-key audit + fleet deploy — see below — before it goes live)
  - Areas changed: `scripts/hive-env-add` (new `push_missing_reconcile` + `push_missing_enabled`; wired into `--sync-maintenance` AFTER pull_reconcile; new standalone `--push-missing [--dry-run]` flag), `scripts/test-env-sync-queue.mjs` (new push-missing scenarios: seeds a key the peer entirely lacks, NEVER clobbers a value the peer already holds, honors the `HIVE_ENV_PUSH_MISSING=0` kill switch, and asserts `--sync-maintenance` reports the push phase).
  - Root cause (confirmed live 2026-07-06): the collector's periodic `hive-env-add --sync-maintenance` (every 10 min) only retried failed writes and PULLED newer keys from peers. It never proactively PUSHED healthy local keys to peers that lack them (that only happened at write-time and in the manual `--reconcile`). Combined with asymmetric reachability — `PORTFOLIO_OFFER_API_TOKEN` lived ONLY on This Mac (a laptop the hel1-2 VPS can't dial inbound), and This Mac (which CAN dial hel1-2 and HAS the key) only ever pulled — the key stranded permanently and silently (all collectors reported `lastError: null`; hel1-2 served 100 keys vs This Mac's 119). Verified: This Mac serves the token via `/env`; hel1-2 and NYC do not.
  - The fix: after pulling, `--sync-maintenance` now seeds each reachable peer with ONLY the keys it is entirely missing. Missing-only is the safety property — it never overwrites a value the peer already holds, so it cannot clobber a newer copy or ping-pong (the receiver stamps pushed keys as now/last-writer-wins, which is why a blind full-push is unsafe — that was the 2026-07-05 clobber incident). Kill switch `HIVE_ENV_PUSH_MISSING=0`. Known residual: a key deleted on a peer during a partition but still live locally could be re-seeded once; rare + self-correcting; the complete fix is receiver-side timestamp/tombstone honoring on the `/env` POST ingest (follow-up).
  - Verification: `test-env-sync-queue` green with the new scenarios; `test-hive-env-roundtrip` green; `python3 -m ast` parse clean; `--help` OK. LIVE DRY-RUN against the real fleet (`--push-missing --dry-run`, zero writes/pushes) confirms it would seed hel1-2 with 21 missing keys INCLUDING `PORTFOLIO_OFFER_API_TOKEN` (and NYC with 6) — healing the exact strand that blocked the two send cards — and correctly does NOT re-push `AGENTMAIL_API_KEY` (hel1-2 already has it).
  - ⚠ CORRECTION (2026-07-06 21:10): my "NO live effect until deployed" claim above was WRONG. `~/.local/bin/hive-env-add` is an 844-byte WRAPPER that `exec`s the repo checkout (`/Users/liam/Documents/code/projects/hivemind-os/scripts/hive-env-add`), so on THIS MAC the repo edit is LIVE the moment it's saved — This Mac's collector already runs push-missing + the new local-only rules (verified: This Mac's `/env` now serves 0 `USEPOD_HOST_*` keys, and its maintenance ran with `lastError: null`). hel1-2/NYC run THEIR OWN checkouts, so they get the fix only after they `git pull`. The per-machine-key hazard was resolved by the follow-up entry below (usepod keys → local-only).
  - Intended commit message: `hive-env sync: periodic --sync-maintenance seeds peers with missing keys (heal strands) + --push-missing dry-run flag`

- 2026-07-06 14:55 PST - Queen voice: free transcription for everyone — caption-source capability matrix (macOS on-device SFSpeechRecognizer + Web Speech) with OpenAI as the paid fallback, zero install bloat
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: NEW `src/features/queen-voice/caption-source.ts` (capability matrix: native-speech → web-speech → openai-realtime, availability probed once per app session and logged), NEW `src/features/queen-voice/native-speech-caption.ts` (Tauri bridge wrapper), NEW `src/features/queen-voice/web-speech-caption.ts` (reuses the app's established `speechRecognitionConstructor()` from chat-composer), NEW `src/features/queen-voice/recorded-turn.ts` (Whisper-upload turn extracted from the hook for the size cap), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts`, NEW `src-tauri/speech-native/` (isolated ObjC FFI crate), NEW `src-tauri/src/speech.rs` (`speech_recognition_available`/`start`/`stop` commands + `hivemindos:speech-partial` events), `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` + `Cargo.lock`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` (command registration), `src-tauri/Info.plist` (`NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription` — required or `requestAuthorization` terminates the app), `src-tauri/build.rs` + `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` (the three-way command ACL — caught by `check:tauri-acl`), `scripts/test-queen-clap-activation.mjs`
  - Summary: Live captions and key-free transcription no longer require OpenAI. The caption matrix prefers free sources: (1) macOS `SFSpeechRecognizer` via the Tauri shell — on-device, zero install bloat (the OS ships the model; the objc2 binding crates add kilobytes, not megabytes), fed by a native AVAudioEngine input tap, partials streamed as Tauri events; (2) the browser's Web Speech API in Chrome/Edge browser contexts. The paid OpenAI caption session runs only when neither exists — so when a free source is present it also stops paying the ~$0.017/min whisper caption session. Bigger: the recorder fallback path now runs free captions too and, when caption text exists at commit, uses it AS the transcript — no Whisper upload, no key — making the whole voice loop work keyless on machines with a free source (replies can ride the HivemindOS-models free tier). Structural note flagged for review: every objc2 Speech/AVFAudio binding is an `unsafe fn` and the app crate pins `unsafe_code = "forbid"`, so the FFI lives in a new tiny path crate `src-tauri/speech-native` WITHOUT that lint (the standard Rust unsafe-isolation boundary; the app crate's forbid is untouched). Known trade-offs, named: OS/browser recognizers capture their own mic, so the app's mute button doesn't silence them — caption callbacks and finalize are gated on the mute state instead; caption text wins over Whisper upload at commit even when a key exists (rare: only when realtime STT already failed).
  - Verification: `cargo check --lib` green (both crates, zero warnings, no unsafe in the app crate, re-checked after the build.rs ACL additions); whole-project tsc has zero errors in all touched TS files; eslint clean; `test:queen-clap` 19/19 including new matrix-order and recorder-caption contract assertions (free sources asserted BEFORE openai-realtime); size guard green (hook 1492/1500 after the recorded-turn extraction); `check:tauri-acl` 44 commands in lockstep. Full gate: 93/98 — the one red this change introduced (`check:tauri-acl`, commands missing from build.rs + capabilities) was fixed and re-verified green solo; the remaining 4 reds are concurrent sessions' files (check-sizes on their files, hive-action-route-drift `/api/company-revenue`, agent-providers, dashboard-nav), unchanged from the pre-change baseline. NOT runtime-verified: the native mac path needs a real `pnpm tauri:dev` run + mic + one-time speech permission prompt (I cannot exercise the packaged webview mic from here) — the availability probe fails closed, so a broken native path degrades to exactly today's behavior. Web Speech applies to browser sessions; the Tauri WKWebView is expected to lack it (the probe logs `[queen-voice] caption source` to the dev log either way).
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: caption-source matrix — free on-device (SFSpeechRecognizer) + Web Speech transcription before paid OpenAI; keyless recorder turns`

- 2026-07-06 15:07 +0800 - Collector-launched agents stop triggering repeated macOS "other apps" data prompts
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: NEW `scripts/lib/syncthing-api-key.mjs`, NEW `scripts/lib/macos-privacy-paths.mjs`, `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`, `scripts/install-telemetry-collector.sh`, `uninstall.sh`, `scripts/test-agent-collector-env-sanitize.mjs`, NEW `scripts/test-syncthing-api-key-cache.mjs`, NEW `scripts/test-macos-privacy-paths.mjs`
  - Summary: The local collector no longer treats Syncthing's protected app-data config as the live credential store on every status poll. It now prefers `SYNCTHING_API_KEY`, then a HivemindOS-owned `~/.hivemindos/syncthing-api-key` cache; background fallback into Syncthing's `~/Library/Application Support` config is opt-in only (`AGENT_TELEMETRY_ALLOW_APP_DATA_READS=1`). The collector also refuses to walk hosted-app project roots inside macOS protected app-data locations such as `~/Library/Application Support`, `~/Library/Containers`, and `~/Library/Group Containers`. The follow-up root cause was a collector-launched Hermes worker invoking file search as `rg --files --sortr=modified ... /Users/liam`; collector-spawned Hermes processes now receive safe file-search roots (shared vault, app checkout, Documents, HivemindOS state, Hermes home) and explicit defaults disabling home-wide and macOS app-data searches.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-syncthing-api-key-cache.mjs` passed; `node scripts/test-macos-privacy-paths.mjs` passed; `node scripts/test-agent-collector-env-sanitize.mjs` passed; per-file `node --check` passed for every touched `.mjs` file; `bash -n scripts/install-telemetry-collector.sh` and `bash -n uninstall.sh` passed; focused `git diff --check` on the touched repo files passed; `python3 -m py_compile /Users/liam/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/file_operations.py` and a focused Python probe confirmed the installed Hermes helper rejects `/Users/liam` before shelling out to `rg`. Live mitigation performed on this Mac: collector was temporarily booted out to stop the prompt loop, `~/.hivemindos/syncthing-api-key` was seeded without printing the key and verified `-rw-------`, the collector LaunchAgent was restarted, `/health` was healthy, and a redacted `/syncthing/status` smoke returned HTTP 200 with Syncthing running. Follow-up after the prompt persisted: collector was booted out again, recent TCC logs named `rg` under `com.hivemindos.collector-helper`, process inspection found Hermes-spawned `rg --files --sortr=modified` crawling `/Users/liam`, those exact processes were killed, and the installed Hermes helper on this Mac was patched to reject home-wide/protected app-data file searches before launching `rg` or `find`. After the final restart, process checks found no Hermes worker or broad `rg` crawl beyond the checker itself, and a fresh 5-minute TCC query found zero `display_prompt` or `kTCCServiceSystemPolicyAppData` entries for the collector.
  - Intended commit message: `collector: guard macOS app-data reads and Hermes file search`

  - Summary: Queen Bee voice exposed raw `Command failed: Command "nc"/"ls" is not allowlisted` lines after the runtime tried basic diagnostics. The local command tool now allows expected read/diagnostic executables such as `ls`, `cat`, `pwd`, `find`, `head`, `tail`, `wc`, `stat`, `file`, `du`, `df`, `sort`, `uniq`, `cut`, `jq`, `date`, `whoami`, `uname`, `hostname`, `ps`, `lsof`, `which`, `curl`, and `nc`, while still blocking shell escape hatches and destructive file commands like `bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `rm`, `mv`, `cp`, and `xargs`. - 2026-07-06 14:05 PST - Queen Bee voice: live captions while speaking (parallel caption stream) — no more "..." until end of utterance
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: NEW `src/features/queen-voice/stt-caption-stream.ts`, `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts`, `src/features/queen-voice/realtime-stt.ts` (gained `createRealtimeSttPrewarmCache` + `raceSttArmDeadline`, extracted from the hook for the 1500-line cap), `scripts/test-queen-clap-activation.mjs` (new contract assertions)
  - Summary: The server-VAD turn model (`gpt-4o-mini-transcribe`) transcribes a segment only AFTER its auto-commit — protocol probe confirms zero deltas during speech — so the live turn showed "..." for the whole utterance and the words landed 3-4s after the user started talking. The one model that streams words as they're spoken (`gpt-realtime-whisper`, mode "continuous") hard-rejects server VAD, so each armed listening turn now opens a parallel best-effort caption stream on it purely for the live caption; the authoritative turn-session transcript replaces the caption at settle. Guards: authoritative text wins the moment it exists; mute-discard resets captions and a trailing delta can't resurrect a dropped turn; a lost/empty authoritative transcript falls back to the caption the user watched build (visible words never vanish); caption mint failure/stall just leaves the ellipsis — never blocks the turn. Cost note: the caption session (~$0.017/min of listening) runs only while a server-VAD listening turn is armed, alongside the cheaper turn session.
  - Verification: protocol probe through the RUNNING app server (`stt-latency-probe.mjs` APPC, real spoken WAV at real-time pacing): app minted `gpt-realtime-whisper turnDetection=client`; first caption delta +533ms after speech began, mid-sentence (speech ran to +3450ms, first delta at +3033ms), final transcript correct. Turns mode re-probed green the same way (speech_stopped +114ms after utterance end, transcript +954ms). eslint + whole-project tsc clean on all touched files; `test:queen-clap` 19/19 including new caption/arming contract assertions; size guard: hook back under the cap (1499). Not verified live in the webview mic path (per the no-UI-loop instruction) — the probe exercises the identical mint + websocket + event protocol.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: parallel caption stream paints words while speaking; turn transcript stays authoritative`

Both holes closed; the next recycle should self-heal within the boot retry window (~4-20s).

- 2026-07-06 12:50 PST - Queen Bee voice: a stalled STT mint can no longer strand a listening turn (the "waveform reacted but nothing got transcribed" hole)
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts`, `src/features/queen-voice/realtime-stt.ts`
  - Summary: Diagnosed from the live dev log — the user's turn at ~12:30 opened voice chat while `/api/phone` (the STT client-secret mint route) was cold-compiling on the loaded dev server (compile started 12:30, still unfinished a minute later); `startRealtimeListening` awaited the mint with NO deadline, so the phase showed "listening" and the waveform (local analyser) reacted while speech went nowhere — no `stt listening armed` line ever appeared. Four fixes: (1) each listening turn now has a 6s absolute arming deadline (`STT_ARM_TIMEOUT_MS`) shared by prewarm adoption and re-mint — on expiry it logs `stt not armed (mint stalled)`, shows a notice ("this turn uses standard transcription... say it again"), and falls back to the recorder path for that turn only; the abandoned mint keeps running and becomes the next turn's prewarm; (2) the mint fetch itself gets a 45s `AbortSignal.timeout` so a hung fetch can't poison the cached prewarm promise forever; (3) the first STT mint now fires at session connect (overlapping the greeting) instead of inside the first listening turn — in dev this starts the one-time route compile a whole greeting earlier; (4) `preparedSttAt` stamps at mint RESOLUTION, so a slow mint isn't instantly discarded as a ">20s stale" prewarm the moment it lands. Pre-roll retention bumped 5s→8s to cover the arming deadline plus lookback.
  - Verification: dev-log diagnosis is CONFIRMED (log shows `○ Compiling /api/phone ...` at the exact minute of the failed turn, no `✓ Compiled` after it, zero `[queen-voice] stt` lines, no fallback warning — the await simply never returned). Fix verified statically: whole-project tsc reports zero errors in the two touched files, eslint exit 0 on both, `node scripts/test-queen-clap-activation.mjs` 19/19 pass (pre-roll flush + prewarm-guard contracts intact), hook at 1498/1500 lines. The 6s-timeout path itself was NOT live-reproduced (needs a cold `/api/phone` + a mid-compile turn); its log line `stt not armed (mint stalled)` will name it if it ever fires.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: bound STT arming with a 6s deadline + recorder fallback; mint at connect; bound the mint fetch`

- 2026-07-06 12:45 PST - Queen typed chat: leaked `<|tool_call>` markup scrubbed, repeated tool calls short-circuited, typed-chat lane extracted from the voice route
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: NEW `src/lib/services/queen-bee/typed-chat-turn.ts` (the whole typed chat-turn/chat-turn-stream lane moved verbatim out of `src/app/api/queen-bee/voice/route.ts`, which had crossed the 1500-line ratchet at 1590 — now 1127), `src/features/queen-voice/queen-chat-store.tsx`, `tsconfig.json`
  - Summary: Liam's "hi" turn burned all 4 tool-loop rounds and ended by rendering raw `<|tool_call>call:read_agent_status` + `{}` in chat (telemetry: round 1 = greeting + 1 tool call, rounds 2–3 = 0 chars + the same tool call again, round 4 forced-prose = 48 chars, 0 tool calls — the leak). Three fixes: (1) `stripLeakedToolCallMarkup` scrubs llama.cpp chat-template tool-call tokens out of buffered content server-side (Scout emits them as plain text when the forced-answer round omits the tools grammar), so they never render or poison the conversation history; (2) the forced-prose round now also SAYS tools are disabled via a trailing system message instead of only omitting them; (3) the client tool loop keeps a per-turn set of executed `name:args` calls — an exact repeat gets a synthetic "already ran this turn" tool result instead of re-executing, and forces the prose answer on the next round (saves 1–2 gateway requests/turn of free-tier quota on tool-spin). Bonus: `tsconfig.json` excludes `.next-tauri/verify-*`/`.next-tauri/dev-*` — Next auto-appends every isolated verify server's generated types to `include`, and a stale `verify-5039` validator.ts was breaking whole-project typecheck for everyone.
  - Verification: scrubber regexes unit-checked standalone against the exact leaked payload + 5 edge cases (all pass, inline `node -e`); eslint clean on all three files; `pnpm typecheck` exit 0 / 0 errors on the final tree (the `.next-tauri/verify-5039` errors gone via the exclude). Full `pnpm test`: baseline 4 red (check-sizes incl. this route at 1591, hive-action-route-drift, agent-providers, dashboard-nav) → after: this route OFF the check-sizes list (1127 lines); remaining reds are the same concurrent-session steps plus a one-off `typecheck timed out` (passes standalone in 69s–3m24 — the gate run overlapped another typecheck; contention, not code) and `test:collector-chat-abort` (green in the baseline run, red under the same load; concurrent file). Live Scout re-test of the leak path NOT run — the leak is nondeterministic model output and a re-test spends free-tier quota; the scrub is deterministic and unit-verified.
  - Intended commit message: `queen typed chat: scrub leaked tool-call markup, short-circuit repeated tool calls, extract typed-chat lane from the voice route`
  - 14:40 follow-up (same entry, still Uncommitted): a second live leak variant ("what's new?" turn, telemetry 14:27 — forced round returned 208 chars, 0 tool calls) rendered a MULTI-LINE args block with `<|"|>` quote tokens and a reversed `<tool_call|>` closer, which v1's single-line rules missed. Scrubber v2 cuts everything from the first tool-call marker onward (never prose) and sweeps both token orientations; the assistant message fed back into history is now scrubbed too (v1 left it raw — leaked markup was teaching the model to emit more); the client loop retries once with forced prose when a round yields no tool calls and nothing shown (a scrubbed leak otherwise ended the turn as a bare "Done."). Root cause of "the fix didn't take": the 5021/5121 dev server booted 11:47 with the Turbopack persistent FS cache still on (the 12:07 opt-in change needs a restart) — its compiled voice route is frozen at 12:11 (`stripLeakedToolCallMarkup` absent from `.next-tauri/dev-5121/dev`; authenticated GET 500s an HTML error shell; touch + request did NOT recompile). Server restart required before ANY of today's typed-chat fixes are live. Scrubber v2 unit-checked 9/9 including the exact new payload and prose-preservation cases; eslint clean on both touched files.

  - Status: Uncommitted

Second half: the voice runtime turn's 20s budget could not survive the gateway's buffered responses + free-host cold start, so wallet-paid brains now get a 120s budget (`WALLET_PAID_AGENT_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS`), mirroring the typed chat lane's existing 120s for the same brain class.

Full `pnpm test` gate: same 5 red steps as the concurrent-work baseline (check-sizes, hive-action-route-drift, agent-providers, collector-chat-abort, dashboard-nav) — none touch these files; `test:wallet-paid-models` green.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice + chat: authenticate the hivemindos-models self-fetch (proxy gate 401) and size the voice budget for the buffered gateway`

- 2026-07-06 12:07 PST - Dev: unrecoverable ChunkLoadError after hot reloads — proxy no longer kills chunk requests at 2.5s; Turbopack persistent FS cache now opt-in
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `next.config.ts`, `scripts/tauri-next-dev.mjs`
  - Summary: Two stacked causes of `ChunkLoadError: Failed to load chunk /_next/static/chunks/... (ecmascript, async loader)` in dev. (1) CONFIRMED on the live 5021 stack: the tauri dev proxy's `proxyTimeoutForRequest` gave non-HTML/non-API requests — i.e. every lazy chunk — only 2.5s before 503ing (`curl` of the exact failing chunk URL returned the proxy's "timed out after 3s" 503 body). A chunk blocked behind a routine Turbopack recompile, or behind the proxy's backend respawn after the Next child dies (observed live: backend crashed and was auto-respawned at 14:10:35 while the proxy stayed up), blows through 2.5s, and Turbopack's async loader turns the 503 into ChunkLoadError; reloads keep failing until the backend settles, which reads as "must restart the dev server". Fix: `/_next/` requests now get 60s so they wait out the compile/boot. (2) `experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForDev` flips from default-on to opt-in (`HIVEMINDOS_DEV_FS_CACHE=1`) — it has a known Next 16.2 stale-chunk bug (vercel/next.js discussion #86912) with the same signature. Next 16.2.6 defaults the flag ON (`config-shared.js`), so the explicit `false` is required. Revisit both when stable 16.3 lands (ships default chunk-load retries for Turbopack).
  - Verification: cause (1) diagnosed live — dead upstream on 5121, proxy 503-timeout body on the user-reported chunk URL, backend respawn observed via process start times; the timeout fix itself is `node --check` clean but NOT live-verified (the running proxy predates it — takes effect on the next `pnpm tauri:next-dev` restart). Cause (2): fresh `next dev` boot with the new config shows no experiments banner and writes nothing to the persistent turbopack cache dir through a real page compile; full `tsc --noEmit` passed with the only errors in a torn generated `.next-tauri/verify-5039/dev/types/validator.ts` from the verify boot (deleted; regenerates).
  - Intended commit message: `dev: give /_next/ chunk requests a real proxy timeout; make Turbopack FS cache opt-in (ChunkLoadError)`

  - Areas changed: `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` (two fixes in the live view's `refresh()`: (1) `setTasksLoaded(true)` moved OUT of the blanket `finally` INTO the successful-kanban branch — it now flips only when the `/api/kanban` fetch actually resolves with a task array; (2) the company list and the Work Board / approvals / agents fetches now fire concurrently instead of companies-then-the-rest in series).

(1) The loading skeletons are gated on `initialTasksLoading = !tasksLoaded`, but `tasksLoaded` was set in the refresh's `finally`, which runs even when the kanban fetch rejects, times out, or returns a non-array — exactly what happens under load (the same load that makes it slow). So on a flaky/slow first fetch the flag flipped true with no tasks, the skeleton vanished, the board/Issues tab showed "empty"/"No unresolved issues", and the data only appeared on a later successful poll — looking like it "popped in" with no loading state. Now the flag flips only on a genuine successful fetch, so the skeleton persists through the wait and until real tasks land. (2) `refresh()` awaited `/api/companies` and only then started `/api/kanban`, so the board sat empty for the SUM of both round trips; they now run in parallel (total ≈ the slower single trip), while companies still resolves first to reveal the portfolio early. Measured the endpoints live on a throwaway-auth verify server: `/api/companies` 179ms, `/api/kanban` 394ms (230 tasks) — so the API code itself is fast; the user's 5-10s is dominated by their managed server being under heavy concurrent load (many agent sessions + dev servers on one machine), which this parallelization and the corrected skeleton gate both mitigate.
  - Verification: `pnpm typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) has ZERO errors in this change's files; the only red is a concurrent session's `src/lib/services/company-email-qa.ts:127` (TS2367, not touched here, was clean earlier). `pnpm exec eslint ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` exits 0. Live-measured the API latency (above). NOT observed live in the browser: the verify server's dashboard stayed on its boot shell because the shared working tree is being continuously edited by concurrent sessions (constant Fast Refresh rebuilds keep `page.tsx` re-suspending), so I could not stabilize a ZHC render to watch the skeleton. The loading-gate fix is grounded in the traced code path (flag flipped in `finally` regardless of fetch success); the parallelization is a standard sum→max latency reduction.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: flip tasksLoaded only on successful kanban fetch (keeps skeleton through slow/flaky loads) + parallelize cockpit refresh fetches`

- 2026-07-06 09:50 PST - Queen typed chat: replies no longer vanish/get replaced mid-turn, no more blank bubbles, tool loops always end in a real answer, and the typed lane finally has telemetry
  - Status: Uncommitted

Accumulate-across-tool-rounds was exercised live on BOTH Scout (pre-cap rounds) and the fallback lane. `queen_chat.turn` telemetry rows confirmed written. tsc/eslint clean on both files; `test:queen-chat-stream` passes. NOT live-verified: the forced-final (`toolChoice:"none"`) round specifically on Scout — it needs Scout to loop to round 4 again, and today's allowance is spent; the param path is exercised by gpt-4o-mini runs and is a deterministic body-shape change.
  - Intended commit message: `queen typed chat: append-only tool-loop display, forced final answer at the loop cap, and queen_chat.turn telemetry`

  - Status: Uncommitted

The upstream producer (a dispatch path stamping an unrelated session's output; the 00:57 cluster) remains to be traced — the guard makes it harmless meanwhile.

  - Intended commit message: `kanban: reject byte-identical cross-task results at completion — misattributed session output is not work`

(1) Driver code updates now apply themselves — the loop routes each tick through the freshly-compiled route. (2) Long tasks are legitimate: the chat window follows the task's declared runtime and the claim heartbeats while work is in flight, removing both the 240s amputation and the reclaim-stall class at the source. (3) Failure records name the machine so load-balancing gaps are measurable. Fleet-wide load-aware delegate spreading (use ALL connected machines when one is saturated) is the next tracked item.
  - Verification: pickup/company-autonomy/infra-rescue/kanban-shards suites green. LIVE no-restart proof during a real outage: the 5020 dev server had died (port dead, load ~50) and the 5121 standby held a stale lease with NO loop running; POST `action:"start"` to the existing 5121 process booted the driver from the route's fresh modules — status running, lease held, tickCount 1 — and the board went to 4 working / 0 needs-human with the email-pipeline tasks actively assigned. Full 97-suite gate NOT re-run this round (machine at load ~50; all touched suites ran individually green — gate delta due next quiet window).
