- 2026-07-06 00:51 PST - Queen Bee voice chat: end-of-speech moved to OpenAI's server VAD — turns register within ~0.5s of stopping instead of 1.4s+ on a fragile client heuristic
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/phone/transcription.ts` (the STT mint is now MODE-aware: "turns" (new default) mints `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` with `server_vad` (450ms silence window) so the SESSION ends each utterance and auto-commits; "continuous" keeps `gpt-realtime-whisper` + `turn_detection: null` + `delay: minimal` for callers that want pre-commit live deltas and run their own VAD; response now carries `turnDetection: "server" | "client"`; `OPENAI_REALTIME_TRANSCRIBE_MODEL` still overrides, with the VAD choice following the resolved model since realtime-whisper hard-rejects turn_detection), `src/app/api/phone/route.ts` (passes `body.mode` through), `src/features/queen-voice/realtime-stt.ts` (`prepareRealtimeSttSession(mode)` returns `{ socket, serverVad }`; older servers default to client mode), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (turn lifecycle branches on the mint handshake: server mode listens for speech_started/speech_stopped/completed — instant speech UI signals, multi-segment merge when the user resumes before a segment transcribes, mute-discard via swallow counters, 20s stuck-VAD cap on a plain interval — while client mode keeps the timer-backstopped energy VAD + manual commit; the energy VAD itself extracted to NEW `src/features/queen-voice/energy-vad-loop.ts` to stay under the 1500-line cap), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-realtime.ts` + `src/components/fleet/agent-call-modal.tsx` (both explicitly request "continuous" — the s2s caption stream and the call modal keep today's live-deltas-while-talking behavior unchanged).
  - Summary: Addresses "still very delayed transcription and still not registering the end of my speech": measured with identical spoken audio at the protocol level, the old path's floor was 1376ms from stop-speaking to final transcript (600ms client-VAD silence wait + 776ms model finalize) with mid-speech captions trailing ~0.6s+; the new default hands end-of-speech to OpenAI's server VAD on the clean 24kHz PCM — speech_stopped arrived 85-130ms after the utterance ended and the final transcript 614ms (direct) / 1092ms (through the app server, network variance) after speech end. No client noise-floor/rAF heuristics remain in the default turn path. Tradeoff made deliberately: turn captions now land at each pause (sub-second) instead of streaming mid-sentence seconds behind; the s2s overlay captions and agent call modal keep the streaming model via continuous mode.
  - Verification: Protocol-level probes (no UI, real synthesized speech "How are you?" streamed at real-time pacing over the realtime websocket): config A (old prod) completed 1376ms after speech end with manual commit; config B (new default, direct mint) speech_stopped +85ms, completed +614ms; config APP (minted through the RUNNING dev server's /api/phone on :5021 after hot-reload — the exact shipped path) returned `model=gpt-4o-mini-transcribe turnDetection=server`, speech_started +115ms after voice onset, speech_stopped +130ms after end, completed "How are you?" +1092ms after speech end. eslint clean on all touched files; whole-project tsc has zero errors in touched files; `use-queen-bee-voice.ts` at 1495 lines after extracting the energy VAD (tight against the 1500 cap — next queen-voice change should extract further). Full gate: 92/98 — the failing set is the session baseline (pre-existing `company-email-qa.ts` typecheck error, 3 legacy size-ratchet files, company-revenue route-drift, dashboard-nav) plus two NEW reds owned by concurrent uncommitted work, `test:company-autonomy`/`test:company-vault-store` both dying on `bee-worker-souls.json` lacking a `type: json` import attribute (not in this diff's import graph). NOT verified live: the in-app turn feel on the desktop overlay (needs a human mic; the voice overlay must be re-opened to mint a new-mode session), and the client-VAD fallback branch with an env-forced realtime-whisper model (logic preserved from the previously-verified path).
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: server-VAD turn transcription (gpt-4o-mini-transcribe) — end-of-speech in ~0.5s, mode-aware STT mint`

- 2026-07-06 00:44 PST +0800 - Shared-vault schedule mirrors keyed by stable machine id instead of hostname
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/obsidian/scheduled-runs.ts` (`scheduleSegments` prefers a validated `hivemind-machine-<32 hex>` machine id as the per-machine directory segment under `Operations/Automations/`, falling back to the legacy machine-name key when no id is known; new lazy `adoptLegacyScheduleDirectory` migration renames — or merges run history from — the name-keyed directory the first time a schedule resolves with a machine id; schedule/run frontmatter now records `machineId` + human-readable `machineName`, the body Device line shows the name, and the hermes-jobs repair path reads both), `src/features/fleet/fleet-identity.ts` (new canonical `stableHivemindMachineId` predicate), `src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-scheduler-controller.tsx` (`schedulerSharedSnapshot` resolves the agent's machine id from `machineGroups` — agent membership first, then `sameMachineIdentity` name match — and `scheduleFromSharedSnapshot` matches agents through the machine id before falling back to name matching), `src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx` (passes `machineGroups` to the scheduler controller), `scripts/test-scheduled-runs-machine-key.mjs` + `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs` (new hermetic gate suite).
  - Summary: Every hostname change used to fork the vault schedule-mirror tree because directories were keyed by machine NAME (the NYC MacBook produced three directories across its 2026-07 hostname churn). Mirrors are now keyed by the rename-proof collector machine id (`~/.hivemindos/machine-id`), with the display name kept in the schedule frontmatter/body. Existing name-keyed directories are adopted in place on the next write/read of each schedule: run numbering and past-run context carry over, collisions never overwrite, and only directories matching the schedule's *current* machine name migrate (directories from older, since-renamed hostnames — already archived for NYC — are left untouched). Snapshots without a machine id (dashboard-local schedules, phones, collectorless machines) keep the name key unchanged. The Config JSON round-trips `machineId`, so the Tauri native reader (`src-tauri/src/scheduler.rs`) needed no change.
  - Verification: new `pnpm test:scheduled-runs-machine-key` covers id keying + frontmatter, name-key fallback, malformed/uppercase id normalization, clean directory adoption with run-numbering continuity and past-run reads, and the both-directories merge case (non-colliding runs move, superseded legacy schedule.md dropped, colliding files left behind) — passes standalone and inside the gate. Full `pnpm test` baseline captured in a HEAD worktree: the same 6 steps red before and after (company-email-qa typecheck, ZeroHumanCompanies typecheck from a concurrent session's uncommitted edit, 3 size ratchets, hive-action-route-drift on company-revenue, bee-worker-souls.json import-attribute crashes in two company suites, dashboard-nav catalog) — zero new failures; 92/98 pass including the new suite. Focused eslint clean on `fleet-identity.ts`, `scheduled-runs.ts`, and the new test; the scheduler hook's 4 warnings and DashboardApp's refs-during-render error pre-exist at HEAD. Not live-verified against the running fleet: first real-vault adoption happens on the next dashboard schedule upsert.
  - Intended commit message: `scheduler: key shared-vault schedule mirrors by stable machine id`

  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: `src/components/fleet-hive/hive-geometry.ts` (`frAddMachinePos` now takes the built layout and picks, across ALL angular gaps between machines, the clear spot needing the least outward escape from the ring — ties prefer the wider gap, then the familiar downward bias; `frContentBounds` passes the layout so the scaled bounds track the real position), `src/components/fleet-hive/HiveStage.tsx` (passes the layout to `frAddMachinePos`), `scripts/test-fleet-hive-geometry.mjs` (dense-fleet no-overlap fixtures 6×6/6×10/4×12/2×9, plus a lopsided-fleet fixture asserting the cell stays at ring radius in a free gap when the bottom gap is crowded).
  - Summary: Two stacked bugs. (1) The dashed "New Machine" cell sat at a fixed radius in the widest angular gap between machines and never checked agent petals, which spiral outward — dense fleets grew petals into that spot, and since the add cell renders at zIndex 2 vs the agents' 3, it drew underneath them. (2) The first collision fix escaped outward along that one angle only, so a dense bottom cluster pushed the cell toward the screen edge while other gaps sat empty. Placement is now collision-aware across every gap and stays as close to the ring as possible.
  - Verification: pre-fix repro confirmed via geometry scans (overlap at 6 machines × 5+ agents on the legacy path; lopsided fixture shows legacy pick at 90° blocked while the new pick lands at ring radius in a free gap); `pnpm test:fleet-hive-geometry` green with all new assertions; eslint clean on touched files; live-verified via Playwright against the running 5041 throwaway-auth dev server with the real 5-machine/26-agent fleet — the New Machine hex sits at the free lower-left gap (162°) at ring radius, zero overlaps among all 37 cells, fully on screen (screenshot captured). - 2026-07-05 22:20 PST - Zero Human Companies: infrastructure failures can no longer strand tasks on a human (capacity counts as infra + driver self-rescue)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/queen-bee/autonomous-worker.ts` (the transient-retry valve now treats machine-capacity skips, no-live-collector delegates, and claim races as infrastructure alongside transport — live card t_mr7nmkl4_vr67n proved a 100%-infra chain of "2 at capacity + 4 timeouts/502s" escalated to needs-human because capacity lines failed the transport-only regex; exports `isInfrastructurePickupFailure` as the shared vocabulary), `src/lib/services/queen-bee/infra-rescue.ts` (NEW: `rescueInfraStrandedTasks` — every driver tick, needs-human tasks whose exhausted result is infrastructure-ONLY are re-queued through the answer rail once their target collector answers /health; fresh attempt budget; max 3 rescues per task via a body marker so a dying machine can't ping-pong forever; one probe per distinct collector per sweep; kill switch `HIVEMINDOS_COMPANY_INFRA_RESCUE=0`), `src/lib/services/company-autonomy-driver.ts` (rescue sweep wired into `tickOnce` BEFORE the ready re-dispatch, so a recovered machine's tasks re-queue and re-dispatch in the same tick), `src/lib/services/kanban/local-kanban-store.ts` (`answerHumanTask` gains `resetAttempts` — infra rescues restore the attempt budget since spent attempts never exercised the work — and `stampLabel` so the body stamp says "Automatic rescue", not "Human answer"), `scripts/test-queen-bee-infra-rescue.mjs` (NEW hermetic suite: rescue/skip matrix against the real store in a temp vault, health gating, budget cap, kill switch), `scripts/test-queen-bee-autonomous-pickup.mjs` (scenario 5g: capacity+transport chain auto-retries, never blocks), `package.json`/`scripts/test-gate.mjs` (new suite in the gate).
  - Summary: Root cause of the "Delegates couldn't take the work (0 offline, 4 unreachable, 2 at capacity)" card: all WEBS delegate chats funnel through one machine ("This Mac") with a 1-chat-per-machine gate, so during a collector restart/overload window a 6-delegate chain accumulates capacity skips + timeouts, and ONE capacity line was enough to defeat the all-transient auto-retry check — a pure infrastructure failure became a permanent human ask. Two layers now guarantee recovery: (1) infra-only chains always auto-retry, and (2) anything that still lands in needs-human with an infra-only signature is rescued by the driver once the machine is verifiably healthy. Humans only see content failures and genuine asks (keys, decisions, approvals).
  - Follow-up (23:05 PST, same session): the monitoring loop caught a SECOND stranding class minutes later — t_mr7nml51_xvj4a went needs-human with `Reclaimed after 3824s without worker progress. Failure reason: timeout. Attempts: 3/3.` (a claimed run whose host process died mid-claim — dev-server churn — so nothing ever failed the task; the stale-claim reclaim burned the attempt budget). `infra-rescue.ts` now also recognizes reclaim-stall strandings (`isInfraStrandedResult`: exhaustion OR the reclaim format with a retryable infra reason; agent-error/manual deliberately excluded) and the suite covers both classes.
  - Follow-up (23:20 PST): rescue budget is now a ROLLING 24h window (parsed from the timestamped "— Automatic rescue (ISO) —" body stamps) instead of a lifetime cap — a long machine-saturation evening (t_mr7nmkyp_pwmw2 kept timing out at the 240s chat ceiling under load) must not permanently spend a task's recovery budget; the anti-ping-pong bound (3/day, health-gated) still holds. Suite covers the roll-off (3 stale stamps >24h → rescued again).
  - Verification: `test:queen-bee:infra-rescue` + `test:queen-bee:autonomous` + full gate suites green. Ran real sweeps against the live board: t_mr7nmkl4_vr67n (exhaustion stranding) and t_mr7nml51_xvj4a (reclaim stranding) were both health-checked and re-queued — shards show status ready, attempt 1, "Automatic rescue" stamp. Gate delta: 92/97 — the 4 long-standing reds (company-email-qa typecheck, 3 size ratchets, company-revenue drift, dashboard-nav) plus a transient `test:wallet-paid-models` red whose test file was being edited by a concurrent session 10s before the gate read it (mtime confirmed; that session's entry reports it green after their edit); none in files touched here. NOT live: the sweep running inside the real driver loop — the 5020 dev server's driver holds pre-change code until that server restarts (owner-restart needed); until then the same sweep can be run one-shot.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: treat capacity/offline/claim-race as infrastructure in pickup retry + driver self-rescue of infra-stranded tasks`

- 2026-07-05 19:56 PST - Queen Bee voice chat: your messages no longer disappear when you end the chat, and voice history rows can no longer scramble out of order
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (recorder/Whisper fallback path: the session-teardown listener that cleans up a live "Transcribing..." placeholder never disarmed after the transcript landed, so ENDING the chat dropped the user's finalized messages from the hook state — and the overlay bridge mirrored the removal into the shared history; the drop is now gated on a `youTurnSettled` flag. Both voice hooks also expose a `sessionSerial` bumped per (re)connect), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-realtime.ts` (same `sessionSerial`), `src/features/queen-voice/QueenBeeVoiceOverlay.tsx` (the voice→shared-history bridge namespaced turn ids as `voice-{sid}-{n}` with a resettable numeric `sid` — a voice session restarted mid-conversation (dev Fast Refresh replaying the hook effect, error recovery) re-issued turn ids from 1 under the SAME namespace, so its rows patched an earlier session's rows in place and the diff removed/re-appended others, scrambling who/text/order; the namespace is now a random per-session key, re-minted on nonce/mode/`sessionSerial` changes and only while the overlay is open with a resolved voice mode — which also stops the toggle-close nonce bump from re-mirroring stale rows as duplicates).
  - Summary: Fixes the two follow-up reports: (1) "my message disappeared from the chat history completely after I ended the voice chat" — the recorder-path teardown was deleting finalized user turns (that path was active because the earlier server-VAD experiment had broken the realtime STT mint on the live dev server while it was being tested); (2) "my response appeared under her reply" — hook-session restarts mid-conversation (Fast Refresh on the shared dev tree WAS happening mid-test) collide turn ids inside the bridge's namespace and rewrite/reorder existing history rows. Restarted sessions now write under a fresh random namespace, so finished history is never patched, removed, or reordered — a restart appends cleanly instead.
  - Verification: Live end-to-end Playwright repro on the isolated verify server (5045) with a REAL spoken utterance — a synthesized "How are you?" WAV looped through Chrome's fake mic: on BOTH the realtime-STT path and the forced-recorder path (realtime mint blocked via route interception, reproducing the fallback the user was on), the turn committed hands-free (~600ms after speech end — also live-proving the stall-proof VAD fix end-to-end), rows rendered in correct order (opening → You: "How are you?" → her reply), and after clicking End the user's row SURVIVED in history on both paths (exit 0 both runs; the recorder run also reproduced the intended "[queen-voice] realtime STT unavailable; falling back to Whisper" fallback). eslint clean on touched files; whole-project tsc shows zero errors in touched files. HONEST GAP: the exact reported ordering scramble was not reproduced verbatim (it needs a Fast Refresh mid-conversation); the fix eliminates the id-collision mechanism it depends on, and the correct-order + survive-End behavior is what was verified live.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: keep finalized voice turns in history on chat end; collision-proof voice history bridge namespaces`

- 2026-07-05 19:30 PST - Queen Bee voice chat: end-of-speech no longer hangs until a tap, plus a live mic waveform in the control bar
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (the energy-VAD loop that decides "you stopped talking" ran ONLY on `requestAnimationFrame`, which WKWebView starves while the page idles — the commit never fired until a click/tap woke rAF; the loop now has the same timer backstop the barge-in watcher uses (~30Hz, active only once rAF has visibly stalled), covering both the realtime-STT and recorder-fallback paths; also exposes the mic `AnalyserNode` as `micAnalyserRef` for the waveform), `src/lib/services/phone/transcription.ts` (comment documenting the live-verified constraint: `gpt-realtime-whisper` 400s on ANY `turn_detection` config at the client_secrets mint, so `turn_detection: null` is load-bearing and end-of-speech must stay client-side), `src/features/queen-voice/VoiceWaveform.tsx` (NEW: live mic-input waveform canvas — 24 mirrored bars envelope-following per-slice RMS of the mic analyser's time-domain signal; rAF + timer backstop so a WKWebView rAF stall can't freeze the meter into a false "mic dead"; slow-timer mode under prefers-reduced-motion; muted = dimmed red flatline; bar color comes from CSS `color` so themes restyle it without JS), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-realtime.ts` (mic analyser side-tap off the caption context, exposed the same way so the waveform works in both voice modes), `src/features/queen-voice/QueenBeeVoiceOverlay.tsx` (waveform rendered in the control bar next to the status badge), `src/features/queen-voice/queen-voice.module.css` (waveform colors + hive-light overrides), `.claude/launch.json` + `tsconfig.json` auto-include (verify-5045 isolated-dist-dir throwaway-auth entry, same pattern as verify-5041).
  - Summary: Fixes "I finish speaking and it still looks like it's waiting on me — a click or tap makes it go through": the tap was literally waking `requestAnimationFrame` back up, at which point the frozen VAD loop finally saw the accumulated silence and committed. With the timer backstop the loop keeps ticking through rAF stalls, so turns commit ~600ms after speech ends without any nudge. This also removes the worst of the perceived transcription delay: partial words still stream while speaking (any residual lag there is upstream model latency, `delay: "minimal"` is already set), but the FINAL transcript no longer waits for a tap-triggered commit. NOTE: a server-side fix (OpenAI server VAD in the transcription session) was built first and REVERTED after live verification — `gpt-realtime-whisper` rejects `turn_detection` outright ("Turn detection is not supported for this transcription model"), and shipping it would have silently dropped every session to the Whisper-upload fallback; the constraint is now documented at the mint site. New: an animated waveform in the voice control bar mirroring actual mic input (dim red flatline when muted), so "is she hearing me" is visible at a glance.
  - Verification: Live Playwright (headless Chrome, fake mic, isolated verify server 5045 with throwaway auth): dashboard hydrated, overlay opened via the DOM toggle, control bar + waveform canvas rendered (92x26), canvas pixel-sampled as painted AND animating (alpha sums 14032→14022→77490 across 700ms samples), and the pipeline session reached "Queen Bee is speaking". That run also caught the server-VAD 400 + "[queen-voice] realtime STT unavailable; falling back to Whisper" live — which is what forced the revert; the reverted mint config is byte-identical to the previously shipped, working one. A post-revert re-run rendered the overlay + painted waveform, but the session sat in "Connecting..." under three concurrent dev servers (machine contention), so the post-revert STT mint itself was not re-proven live; further retries were skipped to avoid degrading concurrent sessions. eslint clean on all touched files; whole-project `tsc --noEmit` has zero errors in touched files (only the pre-existing concurrent `company-email-qa.ts:127` error). Full gate delta: baseline 91/95 with 4 pre-existing reds (typecheck env break, size-ratchet legacy files, company-revenue route-drift, dashboard-nav order) → after: identical 91/95, same 4 reds. HONEST GAPS — only verifiable by a human on the desktop app: the end-of-speech feel on real WKWebView with the local TTS voice and real speech (the rAF-stall repro needs the real webview), and a full spoken turn round-trip (the fake-mic run exercised connect → opening line → waveform, not a transcribed turn).
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: stall-proof end-of-speech VAD + live mic waveform in the control bar`

NOT runtime-verified in a browser: the empty→loaded window is transient (a few hundred ms on cockpit load) and every dev/verify port remains held by concurrent agent sessions, so I did not add a competing dev server. The change is deterministic React — `initialTasksLoading` is true (tasks not yet fetched) exactly while the cockpit first renders after companies load, so the board shows skeletons through that window and swaps to real cards once `setTasksLoaded(true)` fires in the refresh `finally`. To eyeball: reload while on a company cockpit — the lanes show shimmer cards, then tasks replace them.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: cockpit board lane skeletons while Work Board tasks load (no more "empty" flash)`

- 2026-07-05 17:30 PST - Zero Human Companies: buttons now have hover states, "Dismiss all" reliably works, and email-modal links open instantly with a loading state
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/theme.css` (NEW button hover/press CSS — inline styles can't do `:hover`, so a scoped `.zhc-root button:hover` brightness lift + active-press for every in-view button, plus a `.zhc-btn-ghost` currentColor-tint hover for outline buttons and a token-free `.zhc-linkchip` hover for chips that portal outside `.zhc-root`; also unscoped `.zhc-spinner` so the loader primitive animates inside portaled modals), `ConsolidatedIssueCard.tsx` (`zhc-btn-ghost` on all 5 buttons incl. "Dismiss all"), `CompanyIssueActions.tsx` (`zhc-btn-ghost` on the outline action buttons; `zhc-linkchip` on the "Open preview" anchor), `ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` (the live `handleDismissIssues` now optimistically hides EVERY dismissed issue immediately — keyed `work.taskId ?? key` to match the colonies-memo filter — instead of only taskless ones; task-backed issues are still archived via `PATCH /api/kanban {status:"archived"}` in the background), `src/lib/native/open-external-url.ts` (browser tabs now open links instantly via a synchronous `window.open`; the slow server-side OS opener — a round trip + `open`-shell spawn — is reserved for the Tauri WKWebView that actually needs it), `EmailThreadModal.tsx` (per-URL "Opening…" spinner state on link + attachment chips via a new `openLink` helper; `zhc-linkchip` hover).
  - Summary: Three reported ZHC issues. (1) Issues-section buttons had no hover feedback at all — inline styles can't express `:hover`, so this adds a single CSS source of truth: every button in the ZHC view gets a subtle brightness + press, and outline buttons opt into a crisper accent tint. (2) "Dismiss all" appeared broken: the whole archive→refresh chain traces as correct (button → `onDismissIssues` → live `handleDismissIssues` → `PATCH /api/kanban {status:"archived"}` → `moveTaskBetweenColumns` sets `archived` → GET excludes archived), but ONLY taskless issues were optimistically hidden — task-backed ones relied entirely on archive+refresh and got re-surfaced by the background poll (`POLL_MS`) or a driver re-escalation, so the card came right back and read as "nothing happened." Now every dismissed issue is hidden locally the instant you click (the card vanishes immediately), keyed exactly how the render filter keys, and the archive still runs in the background. (3) Email-modal links took several seconds with no feedback: they routed through the server OS-opener even in a plain browser. Browsers now `window.open` instantly; the clicked chip shows an "Opening…" spinner while the (Tauri-only) server open is in flight.
  - Verification: `pnpm typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) exits 0 (clean background run; a concurrent session's transient error cleared). All changed files well under the 1500-line limit (largest touched: `ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` at 848). NOT runtime-verified in a browser: every dev/verify port (5121, 5043, 5041, …) is currently held by concurrent agent sessions, the verify servers are hard-pinned to those ports, and standing up a 4th Next dev server risks the documented multi-server contention lag that would degrade the other live sessions — so I did not force one rather than disturb concurrent work. The dismiss fix is deterministic React (optimistic `setDismissedIssueKeys` → the `colonies` memo re-filters by the same key → the card unmounts), the hover is standard `:hover` CSS, and the link fast-path is a synchronous `window.open`. Things to eyeball in-app: hovering any issues-section button shows a tint/brightness; clicking "Dismiss all" makes the card disappear instantly; an email link shows "Opening…" and opens fast.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: button hover states + reliable optimistic Dismiss all + instant email-link open with loading state`

  - Status: Committed 55937850 (the 5 new/service files; the Cockpit render-site wiring — `<EmailQaBand>` import + render — is intentionally left uncommitted because that file is mixed with another session's in-progress Products/pricing work)

  - Summary: Closes the detection gap found while auditing the Website Outreach Agency's sent mail. Handling already existed (open an email → "Correct agent" → directive learning), but nothing automatically NOTICED bad emails — the audit had to be a manual script. Now the Cockpit continuously scans the company's sent corpus: deterministic checks catch exactly what the audit found (dead booking links, placeholders, the plaintext tracking-pixel leak), and an AI layer flags content/alignment problems the mechanical pass can't (off-strategy copy, personalization mismatch, raw machine-signal text, tone). Each defect class is one click to turn into a standing directive the whole crew reads on every task.
  - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean (full project); eslint clean on all 6 files (fixed a `set-state-in-effect` by remounting the band per company via `key`). Deterministic detection PROVEN on the real 22 delivered emails by a harness importing the actual `probe-url.ts` + `reserved-urls.ts`: it produced exactly the audit's results — 9 dead-link findings (the precise 9 emails whose CTA resolves to `cal.com/liamvisionary/sarasota-website-fit` → 404, decoded from the tracker wrapper and followed to the dead destination), 22 tracking-pixel findings, 0 placeholders. The AI reviewer/route/band are near-identical copies of proven-working code (`issue-explainer.ts`, `explain-issue/route.ts`, `SetupBlockerBand.tsx`). Full gate 91/95 — my work adds ZERO new red: the 3 pre-existing reds (typecheck env exit-127, legacy check-sizes, `/api/company-revenue` route-drift) plus one concurrent-session failure (`test:dashboard-nav` — another session added a `governance` nav view without updating the test; my files never touch the nav registry). The new `email-qa` route PASSED the route-drift guard; `test:agent-mailboxes`, `test:agent-providers`, and all `company-*` suites are green. NOT verified at runtime: the assembled Next route executing in-app and the band rendering in-browser — the other session's dev server won't hot-reload a brand-new route segment (existing sibling routes 200, this route 404s there), and a fresh isolated `next dev` orphaned its listener under this repo's custom dev tooling; this is an environment blocker, not a code issue (route types are tsc-clean and it copies the working explain-issue route). Attribution + scan-coverage (corrected — an earlier draft of this note wrongly claimed only the 3 company-tagged sends were scanned): `readScopedOutboxRows` already attributes ALL of an owned outbox's rows to the company, including unstamped ones (verified against the real outbox: WEBS owns it and all 22 delivered — including all 9 dead-link sends — are returned), so no attribution/stamping fix was needed. But verifying that surfaced a real bug in THIS scanner: the 40-thread cap sorted purely by recency dropped the older 2026-07-02 sends behind newer queued drafts (0 of the 9 dead-link threads were scanned). Fixed by scanning SENT threads first then queued (`isSentThread` + a sent-first sort), pulling the reader's full window, and raising the cap to 60 — re-verified via the pipeline replication: all 9 dead-link threads and all sent threads are now scanned. Truncation is surfaced in the band ("newest sent only") only when a real sent email is dropped — no silent cap.
  - Intended commit message: `zero-human companies: continuous email QA (deterministic + AI) surfaced as one-click-fixable Cockpit issues`

NOT runtime-verified in a browser preview: reproducing it needs a company that has a deliverable AND a matching `reject` directive in live state, which a fresh dev server does not have; the fix is a pure count computation and mirrors the existing, working `rejectedRefs` predicate one function above it. The claim to confirm in-app is that the badge drops by one immediately after a real reject.

upsert never touches proposals), `src/lib/services/company-products.ts` (`extractPricingProposalMarkers` + `fileTaskPricingProposals` — the structured `PRICING PROPOSAL: <product key> $<price>` / `WHY: <evidence>` result-marker protocol, mirroring ACTION NEEDED), `src/lib/services/company-memory.ts` (`syncCompanyTaskOutcomes` detects the marker in finished company-task results inside its existing exactly-once-per-task window and files proposals — the same driver-tick rail that already folds outcomes into memory, so agents on ANY machine raise proposals with no API access), `src/lib/services/companies-orchestration.ts` (worker context now teaches pricing-as-conversion-blocker diagnosis — objections, quote-vs-close, checkout abandonment, with an honesty caveat that silence alone isn't price evidence — plus the marker protocol, and lists pending proposals with "do NOT re-propose"), `src/lib/services/companies-goal-planner.ts` (planner may plan a pricing-evidence-review task; never a price-change task; lists pending proposals), `src/app/api/companies/route.ts` (new `resolve-pricing` action — route is baselined, no guard change), ZHC UI: `Cockpit.tsx` (PricingProposalCard section in the Approvals tab with strikethrough old → new price + evidence + approve/reject; Approvals badge and the "to approve" stat include proposals), `ProductsPanel.tsx` (honey review banner "The crew thinks pricing may be blocking sales — N request(s) await your decision" with a "Review in Approvals →" jump button), `types.ts`/`mappers.ts`/`ZeroHumanCompanies.tsx`/`ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` (Colony.pricingProposals + onResolvePricing handler chain; live handler POSTs resolve-pricing and shows an outcome notice; demo view drops locally), `scripts/test-company-vault-store.mjs` (marker parse variants, exactly-once filing via syncCompanyTaskOutcomes, replace-pending, vault replication, worker-context lines, reject-keeps-price, approve-applies-price, all three memory records).
  - Summary: The learning loop for pricing closes end-to-end: agents are explicitly taught to watch conversion evidence for price objections, and when the evidence points at pricing they end a task result with `PRICING PROPOSAL: <product key> $<new price>` + `WHY: <evidence>`. The driver's outcome sync files it as a pending request on the company (replicated via the shared brain, one per product, exactly once per task). It surfaces as a "pricing change" card under Approvals — approve applies the new price to the shared-brain catalog every agent quotes; reject keeps the price — and either decision lands in company memory so the next cycle's crew builds on it instead of re-proposing. While a request is pending, the Products tab shows a banner naming the requested change with a one-click jump to Approvals. Agents can never change prices themselves.

LIVE-VERIFIED end-to-end on :5041 via project Playwright with a throwaway company (created through the real store, deleted after): Products banner rendered with the request summary, "Review in Approvals →" jumped tabs, the PRICING CHANGE card showed requester + strikethrough $3,000 → $2,400 + the WHY evidence, a real reject click removed the proposal, kept the price at $3,000, showed the outcome notice, and wrote both "requested" and "rejected — do not re-propose" memory lines (screenshots in session scratchpad). Approve path verified hermetically (applies the catalog price). NOT verified live: a real agent emitting the marker organically (needs a live dispatch that spends tokens) — the parser + filing path is exercised by the hermetic sync test instead. Gotcha reconfirmed: the :5041 Turbopack watcher missed cross-session workspace edits twice (this session's UI files AND the concurrent session's dashboard-storage export) — `touch` the affected files when a dev server 500s on an export that exists on disk.
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: crew-raised pricing proposals — marker rail from task results, Approvals decision cards, Products review banner; decisions feed company memory`

- 2026-07-05 16:01 PST +0800 - The rest of the Zero Human Company external links now open in the packaged Tauri desktop app (deliverables, preview CTA, in-file links, setup links)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/DeliverableCard.tsx` (the hero/link deliverable card's `<a href={url} target="_blank">` now intercepts the click via the shared helper), `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/CompanyIssueActions.tsx` (the "Open preview" chip — its existing `onClick={stop}` stopPropagation is preserved and the external-open behavior added alongside it), `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/FileViewer.tsx` (both external-link branches of the in-file markdown renderer: the bare-URL branch listed in the follow-up AND, as a consistency bonus, the `[text](url)` markdown-link branch in the same `inlineMarkdown` fn — both had the identical `target="_blank"` Tauri bug), `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/SetupBlockerBand.tsx` (the "get your key here" setup/signup link chips).
  - Summary: Completes the follow-up named in the 2026-07-05 15:22 email-links entry ("Known same-class bug — DeliverableCard, CompanyIssueActions, FileViewer, SetupBlockerBand"). Each was a bare `<a target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">` for an external http(s) URL, which the packaged Tauri WKWebView drops so the click did nothing. Each now uses the shared `isExternalHttpUrl`/`openExternalUrl` helper from `src/lib/native/open-external-url.ts`: `onClick` → if the href is external http(s), `preventDefault()` and route the open through `/api/system/browsers/open` (server-side OS opener) with `window.open` as the browser-tab fallback. The `isExternalHttpUrl` guard (`^https?://`) means same-origin relative routes (`/api/...`, `/preview/...`) are never intercepted, so authed API routes are never opened in a stray OS-browser tab (the FileViewer.tsx header's own warning). Confirmed each href is an external web link, not a same-origin API route: DeliverableCard `url` = a classified openable web link filtered against `isReservedOrMockUrl`; CompanyIssueActions `previewUrl` = a `/preview/<lead>` published mockup page; FileViewer branches = URLs from `safeHref()`/`https?://`-matched tokens in the file text; SetupBlockerBand `link.url` = a signup destination. Behavior is unchanged in a normal browser tab (there the helper's server route opens the OS browser; the `window.open` fallback preserves the old new-tab behavior if the route is unavailable).
  - Verification: `pnpm typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) exits 0; `pnpm exec eslint <the 4 files> --max-warnings=0` clean (no output). NOT runtime-verified in the packaged desktop app — the confirmed-broken behavior (WKWebView dropping `_blank`) is the documented Tauri/wry limitation and matches the reference email-modal fix's own reasoning; the one claim to confirm on-device is that a real deliverable/preview/setup link now opens the OS browser from the desktop shell. Not observable in the dev-server browser preview (the bug is Tauri-shell-specific; in a browser both old and new paths open the link).
  - Intended commit message: `zhc: route deliverable/preview/in-file/setup external links through open-external-url helper (fixes dead _blank links in Tauri)`

It now: (1) auto-creates the `hivemindos-agentic-inbox-attachments` R2 bucket on Deploy (a bound bucket that must exist or `wrangler deploy` fails); (2) surfaces live readiness for the mailbox domain (listing the account's actual zones when none is selected), Email Routing, and the R2 bucket — as non-blocking hints; (3) can provision one real inbound address (enable Email Routing + worker routing rule) via a new `provision` action; (4) gives clearer ordered guided steps. Cloudflare API primitives are single-sourced in a new client both the mailbox layer and inbox setup share.
  - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean (fixed the pre-existing `company-daily-report.ts` red as part of my own prior signature change). LIVE-VERIFIED end-to-end on the running :5021 dev server: `GET /api/cloudflare/agentic-inbox` returns 200 with the new preflight — `domain` hint lists "hivemindos.app, thehivemindos.app", `r2-bucket` hint reads "…attachments exists" (reflecting the bucket I created), all 3 new items non-blocking. NOT done: the deploy + real-address provision + parser-against-real-mail verification is BLOCKED — the current `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` lacks the Email Routing scope (403), so provisioning a real inbound address needs the token granted "Email Routing Rules: Edit" (zone) or the routing enabled in the Cloudflare dashboard, plus `CLOUDFLARE_EMAIL_DOMAIN` set to one of the two zones. I did NOT enable Email Routing (mutates zone MX/SPF DNS) or deploy a mail-less Worker.

actions are filled pill CTAs in the tone color ("Call"-button language), loops-off gets the explicit one-click enable pill + a quiet "Not now" dismiss remembered via dashboard state), `src/features/dashboard/views/AgentsPanel.tsx` (renders the banner; sets `data-flush-banner` on the fleet panel while it shows), `src/app/fleet.module.css` (squares the panel's top corners under `data-flush-banner` so `overflow: hidden` doesn't clip the flush banner strip rounded), `src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx` (wires both hooks; extracts `openQueenSettingsModal`; the tray "Queen Bee Settings" no longer silently no-ops with no queen — it lands on the fleet view where the banner offers the guided fix), `src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-agent-controller.tsx` (`createAgentFromModal` stops hardcoding `beeRole: "worker"` — honors the draft preset and revives the previously-dead `firstAgentBeeRole` runtime-matrix field), `src/features/dashboard/agent-settings-types.ts` (`AgentCreateDraft.beeRole`).
  - Summary: Answers "which agent is my Queen Bee, and why do brain loops silently not run?" Previously the queen was decided by a hidden name regex ("main"/"lead" crowned agents silently), OpenClaw agents could never be queens, the tray Queen Bee Settings did nothing when no queen existed, and the Daily Context / Weekly Synthesis Foundation loops sat disabled with no surface explaining they need a working agent. Now: (1) the queen is an explicit role, auto-crowned once to the agent with the strongest configured model — ranked by a curated matrix with a keyless OpenRouter refinement, so the queen is ready right after setup with zero user API keys; (2) a subtle fleet banner names the blocked loops and walks the user to create/finish the queen (opens the AgentSettingsModal, create mode preset to queen); (3) once the queen works, the banner offers an explicit one-click enable of Daily Context + Weekly Synthesis bound to her (explicit because enabling spends scheduled model tokens); (4) multiple-queens ambiguity is surfaced instead of silently order-dependent.

Banner restyle re-verified live in BOTH themes via element screenshots on :5041 (dark hive panel + hive-light parchment mirror); eslint `--max-warnings=0` clean and filtered tsc empty for the banner files. NOT live-verified: the one-click enable writing `enabled: true` to the real vault Foundation schedules (a real state write — needs a throwaway vault or explicit go-ahead), the create-queen modal flow end-to-end, and the crown hook against a truly fresh zero-profile install.

Local collector self-reloaded and verified reporting worker. **COMMITTED + PUSHED separately as `e65bf618`** via a surgical temp-index commit carrying ONLY the queen hunk (the file also holds a concurrent session's uncommitted env-endpoint guard, untouched in the working tree — verified by post-commit diff). Fleet rollout to hel1-2 + LAN MacBook via their collector `updateCommand` is prepared but awaits explicit user approval for remote-shell exec; the dashboard-side discovery sanitizer keeps phantom queens invisible regardless, and both boxes pick the fix up on their next routine update. (2) `src/lib/services/dashboard-state-client.ts` — `postDashboardState` sent every save with `keepalive: true`, and browsers reject keepalive bodies over 64KB, so large values (agent profiles = 91KB) SILENTLY NEVER SAVED from browser contexts (endless retry loop; the desktop native path was immune, which masked this as "mystery clobbers"); keepalive now applies only to payloads under 60KB. (3) `src/features/dashboard/dashboard-storage.ts` + `dashboard-display-helpers.tsx` — new `sanitizeDiscoveredAgentRoles`: discovery input has NO authority to declare a Queen Bee, so any collector-reported "queen" is demoted to worker at both ingestion funnels (`mergeDiscoveredMachines` for live native/API sweeps, `parseStoredDiscoveredMachines` for persisted hydration) — heals fleets whose remote collectors haven't received the collector fix yet, without touching stored user choices (verified live: zero gold OpenClaw bees across all three machines including the un-updated remotes). (4) `use-brain-readiness.ts` + banner — the multiple-queens state gains a one-click "Crown <strongest>" action that demotes all queens through the live session's own state (the crown hook then promotes the strongest-model agent), because external store edits lose races against open sessions; "Open Queen Bee settings" remains as the manual path. E2E-verified with a real click on :5041: the store went from queens [Hermes Lead, Swarm Planner] (both model-less legacy artifacts) to exactly [HermesMain (gpt-5.5)], re-confirmed from an independent API read.   - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/phone/local-tts-app-cache.ts` (`evictCachedApp` now drops only the HOT in-memory entry and KEEPS the durable persisted/disk hint — it no longer deletes the persisted copy or rewrites the disk file to remove it; new `persistedAppHint(selectedAppId)` exposes that durable hint), `src/lib/services/phone/local-tts.ts` (`resolvedTtsApp` revalidates a durable hint with one fast `/health` probe — `PERSISTED_HINT_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` 1.5s — before trusting it, promoting a live hint back to the hot cache and skipping the ~12s fleet sweep; a failed probe is remembered per session (`hintProbedBad`) so a permanently-moved selection pays the probe once, and a later successful discovery clears that mark; the direct-host fast path now derives its target host from the durable hint's own id (the live host) rather than the selected id's (which goes dead on a machine rename) and matches with the port-fallback `findMatchingApp` instead of a strict id match), `scripts/test-local-tts-robustness.mjs` (new assertion that eviction keeps the durable hint; `/health` mock endpoint for the revalidation probe).

Root cause: any TTS failure evicted the cached app mapping INCLUDING its durable on-disk copy, so the previous evening's NYC TTS flap wiped the hint and the next morning's first open paid a full fleet sweep — made ~4s worse because the saved voice pins the machine's pre-rename hostname, so cold discovery burned the direct-host timeout probing a dead name. Now the durable hint survives transient failures and is revalidated with a fast health probe (a live one → sub-second resolve, a dead one → ~1.5s then discovery), and the fast path targets the hint's current host. Builds on the 2026-07-04 hostname-rename resolution fix.

Live-verified on the :5021 dev server, including the cold path: a module hot-reload cleared the in-memory cache (the exact cold-open state), and resolution took the new durable-hint health-probe path at `appResolveMs=1045` (~1s) — versus `appResolveMs=16258` (the ~16s fleet sweep) for the same cold state this morning; a warm resolve is `appResolveMs=4`. NOT changed here: the NYC qwen sidecar's slow audio GENERATION (live synths showed `upstreamBodyMs`/`upstreamOpenMs` in the 5–16s range — the known qwen-wedge issue OpenClaw's Universal-TTS-keepalive task owns), which is orthogonal to app resolution.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: keep the durable TTS voice-server hint across transient failures and revalidate it fast (kills the ~16s cold-open sweep)`

This makes the hive env a fleet-wide default any project can override locally — set a key in the project to override, leave it unset to inherit. The previous text emphasized the opposite ("default project runtime loading to ~/.hivemindos/.env"); this replaces that clause. The behavior already matched at the tooling layer — `hive-env-run` loads the hive env as a base then overlays process env so project/shell values win (`scripts/hive-env-run`) — this just makes it the stated rule.

- 2026-07-05 - Fleet map view: dotted region outlines visible again (both layouts) + de-squished, world-framed projection
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/components/fleet/map-view.tsx` — FIVE changes. (1) TOKEN SCOPE (the real bug for the default/hive view): `MapView` now imports `fleet-tokens.module.css` and puts `styles.root` on its outer container so it self-provides `--edge-stroke-start`. (2) SINGLE-SOURCE-OF-TRUTH ROBUSTNESS: every SVG use of the edge tokens now goes through module consts `EDGE_STROKE_START`/`EDGE_STROKE_END` that carry inline dark-theme `var(..., fallback)` — so the map can never render invisible again even if mounted with zero external token scope (`.root` still supplies theme-aware values on top). (3) STYLING: the coastline `<g>` `strokeWidth` 0.7 → 1.4, `strokeDasharray` `"0.8 3"` → `"0.1 4.5"` + `strokeLinecap="round"` (near-zero dashes → evenly spaced round dots), dropped the extra `opacity={0.55}`. (4) DE-SQUISH: `createFleetProjection` now clamps axis anisotropy to `MAP_MAX_ANISOTROPY = 1.8` (widening — never cropping — the too-zoomed axis around the same center), so a globe-spanning / low-latitude-spread fleet no longer stretches continents ~5x vertically. `MAP_MAX_ANISOTROPY` is the single squish knob. (5) WORLD-FRAMED VERTICAL CENTER: `mapContentBounds` now folds the projected coastline latitude extent (`COASTLINE_LAT_MIN/MAX`, the data bbox) into the VERTICAL bounds only, so the initial recenter (and the drag clamp, which otherwise re-centers on pins) frames the map on the visible landmass — killing the dead band above the map that a northern-only fleet produced. Horizontal center still follows the fleet pins.
  - Summary: Answers "the fleet map used to have dotted outlines of the regions — where did they go?" Two compounding causes. FIRST (styling): commit `c481b58d` swapped two bold hand-drawn continent silhouettes for real Natural Earth 1:110m coastlines but kept the old faint styling (0.7px hairline, ~0.27 effective alpha, mostly-gap dash) — two big blobs survived that, but ~70–119 fragmented real coastlines dissolved into near-invisible stipple. SECOND, and the reason the DEFAULT (hive) fleet view showed NOTHING even in classic-visible cases: the coastline stroke is `var(--edge-stroke-start)`, a token defined only on `.root` in `fleet-tokens.module.css`. The classic `FleetView` wraps its map in `.root` (so the token resolves), but `FleetHiveView` never imports those tokens — so under the hive layout the var was undefined, SVG `stroke` fell back to its initial `none`, and the coastline paths rendered fully invisible regardless of width/dash. Making `MapView` carry its own `.root` (the same pattern `HexTile` already uses) makes it self-sufficient and theme-aware under any layout.
  - Verification: The outline fix (1–3) is CONFIRMED LIVE — the user hard-reloaded hive→map and the dotted continents render (two screenshots). The de-squish (4) is CONFIRMED LIVE too — the user's screenshot shows proportioned continents, and flagged the resulting empty band above, which (5) addresses. Supporting proofs: (a) CSS cascade reproduced in a real WebKit engine (`qlmanage` on HTML) — the identical 119-path coastline `<g>` renders BLANK with no `.root` ancestor and visible once the token is provided. (b) De-squish rendered from the real fleet (Helsinki/NY/Hong Kong) at three anisotropy caps; the user chose 1.8x, which the committed projection reproduces exactly. (c) The recenter fix was rendered via the FULL pipeline (projection + anisotropy + pin/edge bounds + pan) both ways: pin-centered reproduces the user's dead-band-on-top screenshot, content-centered frames the whole world with a small balanced margin — the exact math now in `mapContentBounds`. `npx tsc --noEmit` exits 0 after every change. Also verified in source that the drag clamp (`clampAxis`) would otherwise override a content-centered pan back to pin-centered when the pin bbox is smaller than the viewport — which is why the fix lives in `mapContentBounds` (feeding both recenter and clamp), not just the recenter effect. NOT yet verified by me: the live in-app render of change (5) specifically (needs one more reload to eyeball; it re-anchors the map vertically in both hive and classic).
  - Intended commit message: `fleet-map: fix invisible outlines in hive layout + de-squish and world-frame the projection`

- 2026-07-04 18:03 +07 - Fleet animation breathes to the Queen's voice while she speaks in voice chat
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/audio/queen-voice-amplitude.ts` (NEW — a shared, framework-agnostic "how loud is the Queen right now" channel: an imperative 0..1 `level` + `speaking` singleton read at 60fps without React state, a WeakMap output-analyser tap, an envelope-following RMS pump hook `useQueenVoiceLevelPump`, and a DOM-var pulse hook `useQueenVoicePulse`), `src/lib/audio/realtime-pcm-stream-player.ts` (side-tap the streamed-TTS worklet `gain` into the shared analyser), `src/features/queen-voice/spoken-reply-playback.ts` (side-tap the buffered-clip `source` too), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (pipeline hook: create the shared output analyser at session start, drive the pump while `phase === "speaking"`), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-realtime.ts` (realtime hook: build an analysis-only AudioContext off her remote WebRTC track and drive the same pump), `src/components/fleet/orbital-graph.tsx` (graph particle-sphere: core glow + particle cloud swell/brighten with her live amplitude each frame), `src/components/fleet-hive/HiveStage.tsx` + `src/components/fleet-hive/fleet-hive.css` (default hive Queen cell: the honey halo tracks `--queen-amp` while she speaks, with a subtler sympathetic pulse on the Queen herself; idle breathe unchanged when silent; reduced-motion tames it to opacity-only).
  - Summary: While the Queen is speaking during voice chat, the big fleet animation now breathes and pulses in time with her actual voice — the exact beat, volume, and cadence — instead of a fixed idle loop. Both fleet visualizations react: the graph-mode particle sphere and the default hive-mode Queen cell. The moment she stops, both settle back to their normal resting animation. There was no voice-level signal in the app before this (the only meters measured your mic, for barge-in) — this reads her real OUTPUT audio via Web Audio analysers on every playback path (streamed local TTS, buffered/cloud TTS clips, and the OpenAI Realtime stream) and envelope-follows it (fast attack, slower release) so onsets punch on the beat and syllables read as a smooth breathe.
  - Design notes: the amplitude is pushed through an imperative singleton + one edge-only "speaking" CustomEvent, deliberately NOT React context/state — a 60fps `setState` would re-render the whole fleet tree (`AgentsPanel` is memoized precisely to dodge the voice waveform). The graph reads the singleton inside its existing rAF; the hive cell is driven by writing `--queen-amp` on the DOM node imperatively, so HiveStage's fleet-poll re-renders never touch the 60fps path. The pump/pulse loops only run while she's speaking and carry a 30Hz `setInterval` backstop for WKWebView's idle-page rAF starvation (same guard as the barge-in watcher and the Lottie player). The pipeline analyser is a pure side-tap (its upstream is already routed to the destination); the realtime path builds a dedicated context `stream → source → analyser → zero-gain → destination` so the analyser is "pulled" on WebKit while staying silent (the `<audio>` element is still what you hear).
  - Verification: focused `pnpm exec eslint` on all seven touched TS/TSX files exited 0 (`--max-warnings=0`); full `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` reports zero errors for any changed file (one TS 5.7 typed-array generic error — `getByteTimeDomainData` wants `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>` — was caught and fixed); all touched files are well under the 1500-line cap (largest is `use-queen-bee-voice.ts` at 1376). NOT verified by me (needs a real voice session on the desktop app — no mic/TTS/Realtime backend is exercisable headlessly here): the actual audio→animation sync on both paths, and specifically that the realtime analysis-only AudioContext resumes under WKWebView rather than staying suspended (if it doesn't, the realtime pulse is simply idle and playback is unaffected — this is the one claim I'd most expect to need on-device confirmation; the pipeline TTS path shares the already-unlocked session context and is the safer bet). To see it: open Queen voice chat, and while she talks, watch the fleet graph (graph mode) or the central Queen hex (default hive mode).
  - Intended commit message: `fleet: pulse the fleet animation to the Queen's live voice amplitude during voice chat`

- 2026-07-04 - Queen voice: barge-in now works even when you start talking exactly as a new sentence begins
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/barge-in-detector.ts` (new `peakFloor` state = the loudest echo floor observed this reply, i.e. the ceiling of her residual bleed; a gated peak-keyed guard so a NON-initial resume-recalibration window absorbs only frames at or below `max(minRms, peakFloor * guardMultiplier)` — a louder frame is provably the user and falls through to the normal trigger path so it can break in mid-window; the guard is GATED on `bleedEstablished` (peakFloor risen at least `establishedFloorRatio`× above `initialFloor`) and latched per window at `requestBargeInRecalibration` time, so a silent grace or a first chunk after a slow synth falls back to today's unconditional absorb; two new tuning knobs `guardMultiplier` 1.8 and `establishedFloorRatio` 1.6), `scripts/test-queen-voice-working.mjs` (two new gate guards; the existing self-interrupt and resume-recalibration guards are untouched), `scratchpad/barge-in-wiring-sim.mjs` (rebuilt end-to-end wiring sim driving the REAL detector).
  - Summary: Fixes the residual "onset latch" flagged in the prior entry. If you started talking within the ~600ms exactly as a new sentence chunk resumed, the resume-recalibration window absorbed your voice into the echo floor at 0.2/frame, pushing the trigger threshold to ~2.75× your own volume, which `floorBlendBelow` then self-sustained — so you stayed deaf for a couple of natural pauses (~3.5s worst case in simulation). The detector now tracks `peakFloor`; once her bleed is established, a resume window lets any frame louder than that ceiling through to the trigger path instead of absorbing it, so a barge-in coinciding with a sentence onset breaks in mid-window. The fix is entirely inside the detector — the per-chunk resume windows were already opened by the existing wiring (`use-queen-bee-voice.ts` + `spoken-reply-playback.ts`), so no playback/hook code changed. Simulation against the real detector: onset-coincidence interrupts now fire ≤460ms (was ~3.5s / never), mid-chunk still ~440ms, and zero false self-triggers across normal bleed (0.03), loud bleed (0.06), and a first chunk after a slow synth.
  - The trap avoided: an UNGATED peak-keyed guard breaks the pinned 2026-07-02 self-interrupt test — its grace-is-silence scenario never establishes a bleed reference, so the guard would let her own first resumed bleed pass the amplitude discriminator and self-trigger. This is also a REAL first-chunk case when synth TTFB exceeds the 600ms grace. The `bleedEstablished` gate closes it by falling back to unconditional absorb until her bleed is actually observed; the pinned self-interrupt guard stays green, unmodified.
  - Pre-existing flaw named (NOT introduced here, out of scope): the nominal trigger is `echoFloor × 2.75`, but `floorBlendAbove` drifts the floor up 0.004/frame during the 420ms sustain, so the EFFECTIVE barge-in floor is ~3.7× the echo level, not 2.75× — measured on the plain (no-window) path where the new guard is inert: over a 0.06 floor a user at 3.0–3.3× never breaks in, 3.7×+ fires at ~432ms. A realistic conversational barge-in (~0.25–0.4 RMS over 0.03–0.06 residual echo = 5–13×) is unaffected and the sim models that; flagged as a follow-up.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-queen-voice-working.mjs` all green including the two new onset-coincidence guards AND the unchanged self-interrupt / resume-recalibration guards; `pnpm test` 88/90 vs 88/90 baseline (same two pre-existing reds — `check-sizes` on `agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`/`kanban-board.module.css` and `hive-action-route-drift` on `company-revenue/route.ts`, none touched here); focused eslint + full `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` exit 0; `node scratchpad/barge-in-wiring-sim.mjs` ALL CHECKS PASS (onset ≤460ms, mid-chunk ~440ms, 0 self-triggers, before/after contrast shows the pre-fix window never breaks through). NOT verified by me: the actual on-device barge-in with a real mic and real speaker echo — the watcher is client-side and a node sim cannot exercise WKWebView AEC, so this needs one real voice session on the desktop app.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: fix onset-coincidence barge-in latch (gated peak-keyed echo guard)`

- 2026-07-04 - Queen voice: you can interrupt her mid-sentence again, and the "connected" chime only plays once
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/barge-in-detector.ts` (echo-floor recalibration window `recalibrateMs` 1000→600, with a comment on why it must stay short), `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (removed the per-chunk-seam `activity.underrunAt` stamp that opened a suppression window at every sentence boundary; the watcher's rAF loop now has a `setInterval` backstop that runs the tick when rAF has stalled — a WKWebView starvation guard mirroring `src/components/ui/lottie-player.tsx`), `src/features/queen-voice/spoken-reply-playback.ts` (recalibration is now anchored at the actual audio RESUME — `onFirstByte` for the streaming path, before `source.start()` for the buffered path — instead of the chunk seam), `src/features/queen-voice/QueenBeeVoiceOverlay.tsx` (deleted `playQueenThinkingSound` and the phase-transition effect that replayed the activation `scifi-ping.wav` on every "thinking" turn), `scripts/test-queen-voice-working.mjs` (new regression guard: a user talking after a resume-recalibration window still breaks in within the sustain window; existing self-interrupt guards stay green).
  - Summary: Two fixes. (1) BARGE-IN — the barge-in watcher stamped every sentence-chunk boundary as a playback "underrun," which opened a 1000ms echo-floor recalibration window at each seam; inside a window the detector cannot trigger and blends the mic (your voice included) into the floor, so within ~170ms the threshold became 2.75× your own volume and stayed there for the rest of her reply (confirmed by simulation against the real detector: 0/100 interrupt attempts succeeded). Real underruns are already reported separately by the player's `onUnderrun`, and the resumed bleed that recalibration actually guards against returns at the next chunk's audio onset — so the fix anchors recalibration at the audio RESUME (not the seam) and shortens the window to cover just the bleed onset. Result (same simulation): mid-sentence interrupts now fire ~420ms, zero false self-interrupts. (2) CHIME — the connect "bing" was being reused as a per-turn "thinking" cue, so it replayed on every tool-call turn; that cue is removed, leaving only the once-per-session activation sound.
  - Known limitation (NOW FIXED by the newer 2026-07-04 entry above — "barge-in now works even when you start talking exactly as a new sentence begins"): if you begin talking within the ~600ms exactly as a NEW sentence starts, a residual floor-poisoning latch could take a couple of natural pauses (~3.5s in simulation, worst case) to break through, because the resume window still absorbed your voice if it opened mid-word. Removing that latch needed a peak-keyed detector change that collides with the pinned 2026-07-02 self-interrupt regression test; the follow-up landed it behind a `bleedEstablished` gate so the pinned test stays green. Still pending: the on-device ear-test with a real mic.
  - Verification: eslint + filtered `tsc` clean on all four touched source files; `pnpm test` 88/90 (the 2 reds are concurrent sessions' `check-sizes` on `agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`/`kanban-board.module.css` and a `hive-action-route-drift` on `company-revenue/route.ts` — none are touched here); `node scripts/test-queen-voice-working.mjs` all green including the new guard and the unchanged self-interrupt guards; end-to-end wiring proven by a node simulation driving the REAL detector (old seam-anchor vs new resume-anchor); voice pipeline still audible (`POST speak` → 200 audio/wav 165KB). NOT verified by me: the actual on-device barge-in and the single chime — both need a real mic and the desktop app (the watcher is client-side; a curl can't exercise it).
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: restore mid-sentence barge-in (anchor echo recalibration at audio resume) + stop replaying the connect chime`

- 2026-07-04 - ROOT-CAUSE FIX for Tauri dev-app lag: dashboard-state invokes no longer grind a 33MB store on the UI-process main thread
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/dashboard/dashboard-storage.ts` (new `compactFleetSnapshotsForPersist`: persisted fleet snapshots capped to the 128 most recently checked — the merge never evicts, so ephemeral per-PID hermes agents had accumulated 2,822 entries / 30.6MB under `hivemindos.fleetSnapshots.v1`), `src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx` (persist effect uses the helper; net −6 lines), `src-tauri/src/dashboard_state.rs` (`dashboard_state_read`/`dashboard_state_write` are now async so they run on the async runtime instead of the main thread; the write response no longer echoes the full store — the only caller reads just `ok`), `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (`[profile.dev.package."*"] opt-level = 2`: dev builds optimize dependencies so debug-mode serde_json/tauri/wry can't stall the UI thread; app crate stays unoptimized for fast iteration).
  - Summary: The dev-app lag was NOT machine contention, transparency, or paint effects. `sample` of the live debug app caught the smoking gun: every dashboard interaction persists a remembered value via `dashboard_state_write`, and wry delivers sync-command invokes on the UI-process main thread — which read+parsed the 33.5MB `~/.hivemindos/dashboard-state.json`, cloned it twice, re-serialized it to disk, then serialized the ENTIRE store again as the (unused) IPC response, all in an opt-level-0 debug binary (904/7476 main-thread samples in serde_json during a light 10s probe). WKWebView input events and layer-tree commits queue behind that same main thread → the 130–200ms frames and 4–8s stalls in the Safari Timeline. The 33MB store itself was a data leak: fleet snapshots persisted one entry per ephemeral hermes PID, never pruned. Chrome was fast because browser builds use the HTTP route in another process; packaged builds were fast because release opt-level 3 hides the cost.
  - Verification: `sample` of the original pre-fix debug app (pid 60604) during a light 10s probe caught 904/7476 main-thread samples inside `serde_json` serializing the store — the smoking gun. After rebuild+relaunch (the `opt-level=2` dep rebuild lands a fresh binary at 16:56, watcher auto-relaunches), a matched 10s activation-burst `sample` of the live app (pid 94745) that actually fired a persist (13 `dashboard_state` frames present) shows those frames on `tokio-rt-worker` threads and **0 `serde_json`/`dashboard_state` frames on the main thread** (main thread parked in kernel waits) — the async commands confirmed off-thread end-to-end (also visible in the binary's symbol table: `dashboard_state_read/write` now emit future-closure symbols). The store self-healed on the first persist through the new path: 33.5MB → 1.7MB (`fleetSnapshots` 30.6MB → 739KB, exactly 128 agents). `pnpm exec eslint src/features/dashboard/dashboard-storage.ts` clean; `pnpm test` gate 89/90 — same red as the session baseline (`check-sizes`, now only the two other-session files `agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` + `kanban-board.module.css`; DashboardApp.tsx dropped back under its allowance). NOT mine: `pnpm exec eslint` on `DashboardApp.tsx` reports one error at :4590 (`revealKanbanTaskRef.current` assigned during render) — absent from committed HEAD, so it belongs to a concurrent session's uncommitted edit in the shared tree; left untouched. Remaining user check: real clicks/view-switches feel instant in the dev app window.
  - Intended commit message: `perf: dashboard-state invokes off the main thread + prune persisted fleet snapshots (dev-app lag root cause)`

- 2026-07-04 - Queen voice: local TTS survives a machine hostname rename (fixes "Her voice server is unreachable" after the NYC Mac renamed itself)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/phone/local-tts.ts` (`findMatchingApp` fallback: when no discovered app matches the pinned appId's host — the pin encodes the machine hostname, which rotates when macOS conflict-renames the machine and its tsnet node re-registers — resolution falls back to the single TTS-capable app on the same port, and re-pins the cache under the selected id so subsequent resolves are cache hits; ambiguous 0/2+ candidates stay unmatched rather than guessing across machines), `src/app/api/openai-oauth/route.ts` (guard:allow-hive-action-route pragma for the new drift guard — the OAuth connect flow is user-interactive, deliberately not an agent-invokable Hive action).
  - Summary: The NYC Mac's hostname changed (`Liams-MacBook-Pro-1` → `LiamsMBP481146.lan`), which rotated its linkd tsnet DNS name and every hosted-app id on it. The saved queen voice pinned the old id, `matchesAppId` required exact host equality, so every synth got instant `fetch failed` (dead DNS), the 45s breaker opened, and replies went text-only with the "voice server unreachable" banner — even though universal-tts was up the whole time (bound to 127.0.0.1:8799, reachable via the collector app-proxy under the new name). Resolution now self-heals across hostname rotations in either direction.
  - Verification: eslint clean; `pnpm test` gate 89/90 (baseline this session was 88/90: the openai-oauth drift-guard red is fixed by the pragma; the remaining red is `check-sizes` on three concurrent sessions' files — `agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`, `kanban-board.module.css`, `globals.css` — untouched here). Live-verified on the :5021 dev server: `POST /api/queen-bee/voice {action:"speak"}` returned 200 `audio/wav` 149KB (first call 17.3s: eviction + fleet rediscovery + cold synth), second call 2.7s via the re-pinned cache; telemetry shows ok:true local-tts speak entries after 10 hours of `fetch failed`/breaker-open entries.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: local TTS app resolution survives machine hostname renames`

- 2026-07-04 03:30 +08 - ROOT-CAUSE FIX for Tauri click-lag: make the window opaque (transparent:true forced a non-opaque WKWebView → whole-scene alpha-blend every frame)
  - Status: Pushed (needs a Rust rebuild to take effect + on-device re-profile to confirm)
  - Areas changed: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` (main window: `transparent: true` → `false`, and REMOVED `backgroundColor: "#080a0f"`), `scripts/tauri-dev-signed.mjs` (reverted the earlier dev-only `--config transparent:false` overlay — it was a no-op because it kept `backgroundColor`, and the real fix is now in the committed config).
  - Root cause (source-confirmed, 5 independent research agents + wry source read): a real Safari Web Inspector Timeline of the actual Tauri WKWebView showed the per-click lag is NOT JS (script 8%, layout 6%) and NOT CSS paint (backdrop-filter/box-shadow/filter/animation all force-disabled on-device → still laggy; `document.documentElement.className` confirmed the disable was live). The cost is "unaccounted" WebKit PAINT+COMPOSITE — ~28 frames in 22s, interaction frames ~130-200ms (≈5-7fps) vs the SAME app <16ms in Chrome/Blink. Mechanism: `wry-0.55.1/src/wkwebview/mod.rs:368` sets the private `drawsBackground=NO` KVC key when `transparent || background_color.is_some()`, and `:454` calls `setOpaque(false)` when a background_color is set → the WKWebView root CALayer is NON-OPAQUE. Safari has no dedicated compositor thread (reuses macOS Core Animation), so a non-opaque root forces WindowServer to alpha-blend the ENTIRE window against the desktop backdrop every composite pass — memory-bandwidth-heavy, exactly the profiled unaccounted time. Blink avoids it (opaque window + dedicated compositor). The transparency was visually UNUSED — the app paints solid `#080a0f` everywhere (layout.tsx:35, globals.css) — so opaque is a visual no-op. My earlier `--config transparent:false` failed because it kept `backgroundColor`, which independently triggers the non-opaque path (per the wry guard).
  - Verification: config validated (wry guard `transparent || background_color.is_some()` now evaluates FALSE → opaque); `node --check` on the reverted launcher; `pnpm test` gate 88/90 (same pre-existing baseline). NOT yet confirmed: the on-device speedup — this is a NATIVE window change so it needs `pnpm tauri:dev` to REBUILD the Rust shell, then re-run the same Web Inspector Timeline. Expected: interaction frames drop from 130-200ms toward <16ms. Falsifiable: if frames barely move, transparency wasn't the dominant cost and the next levers are content-visibility:auto on the all-mounted kanban cards + reducing the 44 overflow:scroll layers (research captured these). Known minor caveat: removing `backgroundColor` may cause a brief default-background flash before CSS paints on cold load — the dev loading shell covers most of it; if annoying, set an opaque NSWindow background in Rust setup (doesn't re-trigger non-opaque).
  - Intended commit message: `desktop perf: opaque window (transparent:false + drop backgroundColor) to fix WKWebView per-frame full-scene alpha-blend — the real click-lag root cause`

- 2026-07-04 02:55 +08 - Dev DX: `pnpm dev:ui` — iterate the dashboard in the browser (Chrome/Blink), not the slow Tauri WKWebView window
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `scripts/dev-ui.mjs` (reuses an already-running HivemindOS dev server on 5021/5020 and opens it in the default browser; otherwise starts a Tauri-free Next dev server on a free port ≥5030 — no Rust build, no WKWebView — and opens the browser when it answers), `package.json` (`dev:ui` script), `AGENTS.md` "Dev Server Ownership" (guidance: use the browser for UI dev; the Tauri window is macOS-WKWebView-only and much slower than Chrome, and `tauri:dev` recompiles the 23GB Rust shell on launch — reserve it for native-behavior testing).
  - Summary: Root cause of "the Tauri dev app is unusably laggy": on macOS a Tauri window can only use the system WebView (WKWebView via tao/WRY — no Blink option), which is dramatically slower than Chrome for the heavy dev-mode dashboard; the transparent window adds a WindowServer compositing tax and WKWebView starves the app's rAF/Lottie loops; and `pnpm tauri:dev` recompiles the native Rust shell every launch, spiking all cores. Loading the exact same dev server URL (localhost:5021) in Chrome is night-and-day faster. `pnpm dev:ui` makes that fast browser path one command and the blessed way to do UI work; the Tauri app is only needed for native-behavior testing.
  - Verification: `node --check scripts/dev-ui.mjs` clean; reuse path proven against the live server (5021 → HEAD 200 → opens browser, spawns nothing). Confirmed by direct measurement this session: dev server responds ~30ms and is idle, WKWebView WebContent + WindowServer (72% CPU) carry the cost, Cargo `tao` vendor confirms WKWebView-only on macOS, 12 rAF sites + Lottie in the dashboard. NOT changed: the Tauri app itself (WKWebView can't be swapped for Blink on macOS — this is a workflow fix, not an engine fix).
  - Intended commit message: `dev dx: add pnpm dev:ui (browser-based dashboard dev) + AGENTS.md guidance to avoid the slow Tauri WKWebView window for UI work`

- 2026-07-04 - Dev perf: the Work Board panel stops re-rendering on every background poll tick (memo() now bails when kanban data is unchanged)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-stable-handlers.ts` (`useStableHandlers` — mirrors a bag of handler functions through stable identities that always call the latest closure; the latest bag is captured during render, not in an effect, so it is correct for both event handlers and the render-time `kanbanTaskMenuItems` call, with no dependency arrays to churn or go stale), `src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx` (wraps the 39 `<KanbanPanel/>` action-handler props — sourced from six controller hooks — in `useStableHandlers` and overrides them onto the panel's prop spread; +12 lines, size-gate allowance ratcheted 4726→4738 in `scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs`), `src/features/dashboard/views/KanbanPanel.tsx` (TEMPORARY `console.count("[perf] KanbanPanel render")` instrumentation for profiler verification — REMOVE before commit).
  - Summary: `KanbanPanel` is `memo()`-wrapped, but its ~39 action handlers arrive from six controller hooks that each return a fresh function identity every render, so any background-poll `setState` in the ~4,700-line `DashboardApp` (293 `useState` hooks) defeated the memo and re-rendered the whole board and every card on every tick — a dev-only lag amplifier (production is fast: minified reconciler + only the active panel mounted via `activeView` gating). The panel's data/derived props are already memoized upstream (`use-dashboard-derived-state`), so the fix stabilizes only those churning handler identities; the memo now bails on any render where the kanban data itself did not change (e.g. unrelated notification/env/UI-state poll ticks). Renders that DO change kanban data — the board refresh, plus fleet-snapshot and chat-stream ticks that legitimately change `displayAgents` — still re-render, which is correct.
  - Verification: `pnpm typecheck` clean (0 errors tree-wide — the new typed helper checks out; no syntax errors in the `@ts-nocheck` edits). `pnpm test` gate 87/89, identical to the pre-existing baseline — the 2 reds are `check-sizes` on two concurrent-session files (`agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`, `kanban-board.module.css`) and the pre-existing `guard:hive-action-route-drift`; `DashboardApp.tsx` no longer contributes to the size red after the ratchet. Statically verified that all 39 stabilized handlers are exactly the churning props and that the other ~148 `KanbanPanel` props are already stable (refs / setters / state values / module consts / `useMemo`'d derived-state). NOT verified here — the actual render-count reduction: it needs the React DevTools Profiler (or the temporary `console.count`) in the running dashboard. This is a dev-only amplifier; confirm the win with the profiler, not by feel, then remove the instrumentation. Not addressed (larger structural follow-up): fleet-snapshot / chat-stream ticks still churn `displayAgents` and re-render the panel — eliminating those needs the polled-state-into-context refactor (option d), out of scope here.
  - Intended commit message: `dev perf: stabilize KanbanPanel handler props so memo() bails on background poll ticks`

  - Status: Pushed

Size-gate ratchet: +57 local-kanban-store (mutation uses store-private internals), +2 DashboardApp (hook wiring).

Pressing an option sends the answer back to the SAME agent that asked: instantly via live chat when the conversation modal has it reachable, otherwise durably — answer into the task body, card back to Ready with the assignee kept, and an immediate autonomous pickup scheduled when the card has a delegated machine.
