LIVE-verified 15/15 via project Playwright on the throwaway-auth 5041 server against three block()-produced cards on a disposable board (deleted after): decision card rendered chips + both options, clicking "Yes, send now" moved the card out of Needs Human with the answer in the body and the assignee kept, the conversation modal showed the OPENAI_API_KEY form prefilled with a disabled-until-filled save button, and the file card showed the attach hint (screenshots captured). NOT verified live: the actual API-key save submit (it writes to the REAL shared hive env via hive-env-add — path confirmed by trace against the existing /api/env route the settings UI uses) and the immediate-pickup scheduling against a live collector (delegation shape mirrors `redispatchReadyQueenBeeTasks` exactly; route test covers the no-target case).
  - Intended commit message: `work board: interactive needs-human asks (LINK/OPTIONS/NEEDS protocol, one-click answers, hive-env key saves) + answer action with same-agent instant pickup`

- 2026-07-04 02:10 +08 - Dev perf: `pnpm tauri:dev` reuses a running dev server instead of spawning one per session (+ Turbopack dev FS cache, opaque dev window, adaptive recovery poll, AGENTS.md reuse rule)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/tauri-next-dev.mjs` (new `identifyExistingProxy` — if the proxy port is already served by a HivemindOS dev proxy, ATTACH the window to it and spawn no second Next dev server / voice worker, instead of the old hard "port in use" exit; `HIVEMINDOS_DEV_NO_SHARE=1` forces a private server; the injected dev-recovery readiness poll now backs off 1s→5s once the backend is healthy instead of a fresh proxy→Next connect every second forever; on owner start it also drops a `.metadata_never_index` marker into `.next-tauri/` and `node_modules/` so Spotlight/mds stops indexing+eventing the multi-GB dev caches that flood fseventsd, and prunes orphaned `.next-tauri/dev-<port>` Turbopack caches whose port has no listener and are >30min stale — dead sessions were leaking these to 5.6GB), `scripts/tauri-dev-signed.mjs` (dev-only `--config` overlay sets `transparent:false` so `pnpm tauri:dev` runs the window opaque off WKWebView's slower alpha-compositing path — the committed config and all packaged builds keep transparency; `HIVEMINDOS_DEV_TRANSPARENT=1` restores it), `next.config.ts` (dev enables `experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForDev` so the ~278 routes' cold first-compile survives a dev-server restart; `HIVEMINDOS_DEV_FS_CACHE=0` disables), `AGENTS.md` "Dev Server Ownership" (new rule: reuse ONE dev server across sessions/windows — one Next dev backs unlimited Tauri windows — don't spawn one per session; how to check; prefer a git worktree for isolated editing).
  - Summary: The Tauri dev app was unusably laggy on the main Mac because ~9 concurrent agent sessions each ran their own Next dev server against the one shared working tree — load avg ~14.7 on 12 cores, 22.4GB in swap, and `fseventsd` pegged at 96% from the stacked file watchers each writing a sibling `.next-tauri/dev-<port>` cache the others also watch. The built app was fast because it is one compiled process with no watchers, which is why building felt fine and dev did not. Root fix: a second `pnpm tauri:dev` now attaches to the already-running dev server instead of erroring/spawning another, and a new AGENTS.md rule tells agents to reuse a single server. Secondary dev-only wins: Turbopack persistent FS cache (routes stop recompiling cold after every restart — the "routes take forever" symptom), an opaque dev window, and a 5x-slower steady-state recovery poll.
  - Verification: `node --check` clean on both launcher scripts; `identifyExistingProxy` proven against live ports (running proxy :5021 → hivemind/204, foreign `http.server` → foreign/404, free port → refused → private-server path); transparent overlay emits `transparent:false` with all 13 window keys preserved; `turbopackFileSystemCacheForDev` confirmed present as `boolean` in the installed Next 16.2.6 type (`node_modules/next/dist/server/config-shared.d.ts:483`) and type-checks; repo confirmed NOT a Syncthing folder (only the Obsidian vaults are) so a repo `.stignore` was deliberately NOT added — it would have been inert. `pnpm test` gate 86/88, identical to the pre-existing baseline: the only 2 reds are `check-sizes` (`scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` 8189/8187, `src/app/kanban-board.module.css` 4536/4528 — both mid-edit by concurrent sessions, untouched here) and `guard:hive-action-route-drift` (pre-existing, unrelated); `test:tauri-dev-resilience` green. NOT verified live: a second `pnpm tauri:dev` attaching in an actual Tauri window (avoided — it spawns a native window + more load on the saturated box; verified by the probe logic + control flow instead), and the opaque-window latency delta (a cheap A/B, not a confirmed fix — the authoritative Tauri thread blames a 60Hz WKWebView frame cap, not transparency).
  - Intended commit message: `dev perf: tauri:dev reuses a running dev server (attach-on-existing) + turbopack dev fs cache + opaque dev window + adaptive recovery poll + AGENTS.md reuse rule`

- 2026-07-03 - Notifications / alerts view restyled onto the Fleet-Hive dark-honey design system (Fleet / Trade / Brain)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/app/notifications.module.css` (full rebuild — CSS module only, no markup/logic change). The whole view now runs on the warm dark-honey token system the Fleet-Hive / Trade / Brain views use: scoped `--nf-*` tokens declared on `.notificationsPanel` with values lifted verbatim from `fleet-hive.css` `.fr-root` — warm-neutral dark panels (`--panel #14161c` / `--panel-2 #181b22` / `--panel-hi #1e222b`), cream-tinted hairlines (`rgba(238,232,220,0.07–0.20)`), warm text tiers (`--fg #f3f0e9` / `--fg-2` / `--fg-3`), one muted-honey accent (`#e7b45c`) used sparingly, teal (`--live #6fcdba`) reserved for the normal/resolved signal, warm rose (`--danger #e58e85`) for trouble, 14px radius + soft shadow. Priority is shown by the priority pill only — the old colored card left-border accent was removed (cards are now a uniform hairline); glyphs are honey-soft chips; inline `code`/links are honey. Buttons: the shared `<Button>` is re-skinned *scoped to this panel* to the elegant Fleet-Hive `.fr-act` idiom — medium-weight text (500, NOT the earlier bold 600 that stood out against every other view), a single honey-filled primary ("Mark read") with dark text, and quiet transparent secondaries with a hairline border that only fill on hover — replacing the jarring bright-teal primary and the heavy filled/bold gray secondaries; the app-wide Button component is untouched (targeted via `button[data-slot="button"]` / `[data-variant]`, and CSS-module rules are unlayered so they win over Tailwind's layered utilities). The whole view flips to the warm-paper light theme by re-declaring the token block once under `[data-theme="hive-light"]` (values from `.fr-root[data-fr-theme="light"]`) — no per-element light overrides. Also: the source/task pill now WRAPS instead of ellipsis-truncating (removes a pre-existing silent truncation of the task title, per the AGENTS.md no-truncation rule). `.navBadge` (rendered in the nav shelf outside the panel) intentionally keeps the global `--danger` token since the scoped `--nf-*` tokens don't reach it.
  - Summary: The notifications tab was still on the old cool blue-slate palette (`rgba(148,163,184,…)` lines, teal-slate surfaces) — an earlier restyle attempt only tightened the layout but kept that palette, so it still looked out of place next to the dark-honey Fleet, Trade and Brain views. It now shares their exact palette and surface treatment (warm panels, cream hairlines, sparing honey accent, honey primary button), in both dark and hive-light themes, with the card priority left-bar removed at the user's request. No behavior, handlers or data flow changed — purely visual.
  - Verification: eslint clean on `NotificationsPanel.tsx` (`--max-warnings=0`); confirmed every module class the panel references is still defined and that `createStyleClass` passes modifier strings through untouched (priority/state modifiers unaffected). Rendered a faithful static harness (exact class names + real markup incl. the real `<Button>` `data-slot`/`data-variant` attributes + real token values, backdrop set to the hive `--bg`) and screenshotted both themes at 1080px — warm panels, honey eyebrow/glyphs, honey primary + hairline secondary buttons, no card left-bar, tinted pills, honey inline-code and resolution notes all correct in dark; warm-paper mirror correct in light. NOT verified: the live authenticated dashboard route (localhost-auth + pulse-overlay + needs real notification data) — the isolated harness is a proxy for it; open the Notifications tab in both themes to confirm in situ.
  - Intended commit message: `notifications: rebuild the alerts view on the Fleet-Hive dark-honey token system (honey buttons, no priority left-bar)`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen voice: hive-brain + work-board context in conversation turns; ack cut on reply arrival
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/lib/services/queen-bee/voice-brain-context.ts` (shared-brain recall via `answerFromAgentMemory` — the established `buildSharedBrainMemoryContext` pattern incl. `untrustedContextMessage` wrapping — plus a compact open-Work-Board digest, each under a hard 900ms budget), `voice-turn.ts` (context rides the system preamble for ALL brain lanes; lazy import keeps node suites clean), `use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (the "On it" ack is now cuttable: the reply's first speech delta cancels an ack that has not started or only just began (<350ms), so it can no longer play after the reply text is already visible).
  - Summary: The conversation lanes are bare chat calls with no tools, so "look in my hive brain / what's on our to-do list" got "I can't access that" — now every spoken turn carries relevant shared-brain memories and the live open-task digest. LIVE-VERIFIED: asked "what's on our to-do list right now?" through converse-stream and she answered with the real board (Universal TTS keepalive [working · OpenClaw], email deliverability, USDC approval, loop evals). Ack-collision root cause: turns slower than the 4.5s ack delay (fleet lane) legitimately fired the ack right before speech arrived.
  - Verification: gate 87/87 (0 regressions), eslint clean. NOT verified: the ack cut by ear (needs a >4.5s live turn). Known context: TTS synth-stream opens occasionally spike to ~7.3s (NYC qwen sidecar instability — the voice-lab keepalive task on the board IS the fix); the Queen profile's model is still empty so the brain tag reads "auto" — set Calls → Chat brain → Custom → OpenAI (ChatGPT) → gpt-5.5 for the fast subscription lane.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: hive-brain + work-board context in spoken turns; cut stale ack on reply arrival`

- 2026-07-03 - Syncthing single-instance fix: legacy-unit cleanup on Linux, service-manager-first recovery, RestartSec (hel1-2 crash-loop)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/install-telemetry-collector.sh` (Linux branch now disables + removes the pre-rename `omni-agent-hivemind-syncthing.service` user unit before installing `hivemindos-syncthing.service` — mirroring the macOS legacy-LaunchAgent bootout it always had; unit template gains `RestartSec=5` so a future lock-contention loop respawns every 5s instead of ~1/s), `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` (new `startSyncthingViaServiceManager` — syncthing recovery in `waitForSyncthing` now starts the installer-managed unit via `launchctl kickstart` (macOS) / `systemctl --user start` (Linux, with an XDG_RUNTIME_DIR fallback) and only falls back to the old detached `run-syncthing.sh` spawn when no service-manager path works, so recovery can never mint a second instance that races the unit for the Syncthing DB lock), `scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs` (collector allowance 8152 → 8187, dated).
  - Summary: On hel1-2 the renamed `hivemindos-syncthing.service` crash-looped for ~3 weeks (restart counter 422,759) because the never-cleaned-up legacy `omni-agent-hivemind-syncthing.service` from the pre-rename install kept running since boot and held the Syncthing DB lock — the installer only cleaned up the legacy service name on macOS, never on Linux. Remediated live on the box (legacy unit disabled + stopped, deployed unit patched with RestartSec=5, sanctioned unit now the sole owner) and root-caused in the repo so the next installer/fleet rollout fixes any other Linux box in the same state. The `hivemindos-vault` "isn't making sync progress" stall was state in the 21-day-old legacy instance: after the handover the folder reports 0 needed items, both peers at 100%, and no stall warnings.
  - Verification: `bash -n` + `node --check` on the edited scripts; gate 86/87 (sole red: `check-sizes`, caused by this change's +35 lines) → 87/87 after the documented allowance bump. LIVE-verified on hel1-2 via linkd shell: legacy unit stopped cleanly (PID 1001 gone), `hivemindos-syncthing.service` active with `NRestarts=0`, same device identity (KAQZW), vault folder idle / 100% completion on both peers, zero "sync progress" warnings since restart. NOT verified live: the new collector recovery path (needs a fleet rollout plus a syncthing-down event to exercise), and the installer's legacy cleanup on a box that still has the legacy unit (hel1-2's was hand-disabled during remediation).
  - Intended commit message: `syncthing: never race the managed unit — Linux legacy-unit cleanup in installer, service-manager-first recovery in collector, RestartSec=5 (hel1-2 421k-restart crash-loop)`

- 2026-07-03 - Collector /chat: client disconnect now kills the spawned hermes CLI (zombie-worker fix)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` (`sendHermesChat` accepts an AbortSignal wired into `execFileAsync` — an abort SIGTERMs the hermes child and skips the empty-output fallback retry, returning a 499-shaped result; the `/chat` handler creates the controller and aborts it on response `close` with the response still unwritten; kill-switch `AGENT_TELEMETRY_CHAT_ABORT_KILL=0` restores the old detached behavior), `scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs` (collector allowance 8123 → 8152, dated), new `scripts/test-collector-chat-abort.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: When a /chat caller aborted (queen-bee delegates time out at 240s via `QUEEN_BEE_AUTONOMOUS_CHAT_TIMEOUT_MS`), the collector-side `hermes -z` process kept running up to the full 20-minute `AGENT_TELEMETRY_CHAT_TIMEOUT_MS` — during the 2026-07-03 hel1-2 pile-up every aborted delegate attempt left a full hermes CLI burning CPU/memory, amplifying the overload. The non-streaming path is now tied to the request lifecycle. The streaming paths already had this wiring and are unchanged: `streamHermesChat` kills its child on response close (or intentionally detaches once a pollable session exists) and `proxyHermesApiChat` aborts its upstream fetch on response close.
  - Verification: gate baseline 86/86 (26.2s) → 87/87 with the new suite, 0 regressions. The new hermetic test (sandbox HOME, free ports, `HERMES_BIN` faked with a pid-recording sleeper, 7.6s) proves both directions: a client abort kills the child within 5s, and `AGENT_TELEMETRY_CHAT_ABORT_KILL=0` preserves the old survive-the-abort behavior (negative control). NOT verified live: a real queen-bee 240s delegate abort against a fleet collector (needs the fleet rollout that ships this file).
  - Intended commit message: `collector: kill spawned hermes CLI when the /chat caller disconnects (zombie-worker fix, kill-switch AGENT_TELEMETRY_CHAT_ABORT_KILL=0)`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen Bee autonomous pickups: per-machine chat concurrency gate + in-flight dedupe + proxy-error diagnostics (WEBS crew-batch overload fix)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/queen-bee/autonomous-worker.ts` (per-machine semaphore around each delegation attempt — at most `QUEEN_BEE_MACHINE_CHAT_CONCURRENCY` (default 1) autonomous chats per target machine, waiters bounded by `QUEEN_BEE_MACHINE_SLOT_WAIT_MS` (default 10 min), `0` disables the gate; capacity-only misses return "skipped" leaving the task ready for the next dispatch sweep instead of escalating to needs-human; `scheduleQueenBeeAutonomousPickup` now dedupes on task id while a pickup is in flight so the driver's 5-min re-dispatch sweep can't double-run a slot-waiting task; `defaultFetchJson` surfaces non-JSON error bodies — a linkd 502 now reads "hivemind-linkd peer proxy error: <cause>" in the task record instead of bare "Bad Gateway"; new exported `pickupMachineKey` resolves peer-proxy URLs to the REMOTE peer identity), `scripts/test-queen-bee-autonomous-pickup.mjs` (4 new scenarios: same-machine serialization, capacity skip stays ready + never blocks, schedule dedupe + release, machine-key identity incl. `/peer/<host:port>` decoding).
  - Summary: the 2026-07-03 ~13:44Z Website Outreach Agency batch dispatch fired several pickups at once onto hivemindos-ubuntu-8gb-hel1-2 (4 GB-swapped vCPUs, 4 crew agents); concurrent `hermes -z` turns starved each other past the 240s `QUEEN_BEE_AUTONOMOUS_CHAT_TIMEOUT_MS`, every delegate aborted, and the reroute chain amplified the pile-up (client aborts don't kill the collector-side hermes process, which runs on a 20-min collector timeout). Confirmed from the box: 10+ hermes CLI boots in 13 min lining up to the second with the task's per-delegate failures, while the same box completed identical work solo. The "Bad Gateway" failure was linkd's 502 for a collector restarted mid-chat (a `systemctl` client inside the collector's own cgroup — i.e. a crew agent's shell — restarted `agent-telemetry.service` at 14:01:53Z), with the diagnostic body discarded by the pickup's fetch helper.
  - Verification: gate baseline 85/85 (two consecutive greens; one unattributed red on a first heavily-parallel run) → 85/85 after; `test:queen-bee:autonomous` green with the 4 new scenarios; eslint clean on touched files. NOT verified live: the next real batch re-dispatch onto hel1-2 exercising the gate end-to-end.
  - Intended commit message: `queen-bee: per-machine autonomous chat concurrency gate + pickup dedupe + surface proxy 502 bodies`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen voice: post-playback speech loss + slow commit fixed; chat-style thinking UI + brain tag
  - Status: Pushed

Also: an API probe transcript accidentally created a real delegated task ("sanity check on UI changes" → Ada Lovelace) on the Work Board.

- 2026-07-03 - Notifications: auto-resolved alerts now show "resolution in progress" / "resolved" on the card itself
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/types/agent-notifications.ts` (`AgentNotificationResolution` on the notification), `src/lib/services/obsidian/agent-notifications.ts` (resolution lives in a `state/resolution-state.json` sidecar like read receipts — notes stay immutable; `setAgentNotificationResolution` no-ops on identical stamps; `createAgentNotification` now returns the created id), `src/lib/services/messaging/escalation-notify.ts` (escalation state remembers key→card id (`notes`) plus a card-creation TTL gate (`carded`) separate from external-delivery dedupe; explicit per-send card ids FIX a pre-existing flaw where same-day escalations with the same title — two "Work is blocked on you" for different tasks — silently overwrote each other's card file; new `resolveEscalationNotification(key,…)` + pure `needsHumanResolutionFor`; the sweep now auto-stamps every needs-human card each tick: re-dispatched task → "resolution in progress", done/archived/gone → "resolved", bounced back to needs-human → stamp cleared), `src/lib/services/company-autonomy-driver.ts` (fleet recovery resolves the original "can't see the fleet" card in place, in addition to the recovery notice), `src/features/notifications/NotificationsPanel.tsx` + `notifications.module.css` (resolved/in-progress pill + note line with timestamp, dark + hive-light), `src/features/notifications/notification-actions.ts` (resolved cards stop offering "Send to board"), new `scripts/test-notification-resolution.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: An alert that self-healed used to look identical to a live one — the 2026-07-03 "driver can't see the fleet" alarm read as an open incident hours after the condition cleared. Escalation cards now track their condition's lifecycle automatically: while a remediation loop is actively retrying (a blocked task re-dispatched to the crew) the card shows "resolution in progress"; once the condition clears (task completed, fleet snapshot recovered) it shows "✓ resolved" with a note and timestamp; if the condition re-fires, the stamp clears and the card reads live again. Resolved cards keep their deep links and Discuss but stop offering "Send to board".
  - Verification: gate baseline 84/84 → 86/86 (+1 the new suite, +1 a concurrent session's suite; 0 regressions); the new hermetic test covers the sidecar service, the key→card mapping, the duplicate-card gate, the same-title-overwrite fix, one-key-never-touches-another, and the full needs-human sweep transitions (in-progress → cleared on bounce-back → resolved) against a real temp Work Board; LIVE-verified via the project Playwright recipe against the real vault feed — the actual 20:48 driver alarm (resolution backfilled with the honest 21:03 recovery facts) renders "✓ resolved" + note + suppressed board button (the Claude preview browser hung on the boot splash this round; Playwright per the recipe worked). NOT verified live: an end-to-end in-progress→resolved transition on a NEW escalation (covered hermetically; the next real needs-human cycle will exercise it), and pre-feature notifications have no key→card mapping so they never auto-resolve (only new escalations get lifecycle).
  - Intended commit message: `notifications: auto-stamped resolution lifecycle (in-progress/resolved) for escalation cards + per-send card ids fix same-title overwrite`

- 2026-07-03 - Notifications: every card now has tailored action buttons (deep links, Send to board, Discuss with the Queen)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/features/notifications/notification-actions.ts` (pure derivation: notification kind/tags/source/referenced task id → ordered action descriptors), `src/features/notifications/NotificationsPanel.tsx` (action row per card: navigate buttons via `DashboardRouteTarget`, Send-to-board POSTs `/api/kanban` with an idempotency key and flips to "On the board — open task", Discuss expands the persistent Queen chat bubble, auto-sends a context turn via `queenChat.sendText`, and focuses the input; `task:` tags hidden from the pill row), `src/features/dashboard/views/UtilityPanels.tsx` + `DashboardApp.tsx` (thread `navigateDashboardTarget` down as `onNavigateTarget`), `src/app/notifications.module.css` (action row + error styles, hive-light mirror), `src/lib/services/messaging/escalation-notify.ts` (needs-human escalations now stamp a structured `task:<id>` tag so the button deep-links straight to the blocked task), `src/lib/services/company-autonomy-driver.ts` (the empty-fleet alarm now sends a one-shot "can see the fleet again" recovery notice so a resolved alert can't read as live), new `scripts/test-notification-actions.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: Notifications used to say "needs attention" with no way to act. Every card now derives buttons from what it is ABOUT: a needs-human escalation gets "Open task"/"Open Work Board" (+ "Open Companies" when a company is named), the company-driver alarm gets "Open Companies"/"Open Fleet", approvals get "Open Wallet", env/credential problems get "Open Env setup", and every card gets "Send to board" (creates a Work Board task, deduped by notification id) and "Discuss" (expands the Queen chat pill, sends the notification as context asking for cause/resolution/next action, and focuses the input). Acting via board/discuss marks the notification read.
  - Verification: gate baseline 83/83 → 84/84 (+1 the new hermetic suite with fixtures mirroring the two real 2026-07-03 escalations; 0 regressions); eslint clean on touched files; LIVE-verified on the `hivemindos-verify-5041-throwaway-auth` server against the real vault feed — all three tailored button shapes rendered per kind, "Open Companies" on the driver alarm navigated to `?view=governance`, "Discuss" sent the context turn + expanded the bubble + focused the input (Queen replied live), "Send to board" created a real task, flipped the button, and the test task was archived afterwards (t_mr51dsst_f1130). NOT verified: hive-light rendering of the new action row (layout-only styles + shared Button component; low risk), and the recovery notice needs the next real empty-fleet episode to fire.
  - Intended commit message: `notifications: tailored action buttons (deep links, send-to-board, discuss with the Queen) + needs-human task tags + empty-fleet recovery notice`

- 2026-07-03 - Paid seller endpoints: external buyers reach x402/credit payment auth instead of the dashboard 401 (revenue-rail unblock)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/proxy.ts` (adds `/api/paid-agents` and `/api/official-paid-agents` to `SELF_AUTHENTICATING_API_PREFIXES` with rationale), `src/app/api/paid-agents/[slug]/chat/completions/route.ts` + `src/app/api/official-paid-agents/[slug]/chat/completions/route.ts` (`RouteContext.params` typed as `Promise<{slug}>` per the Next 16 contract, matching the sibling credits routes — the old `Promise|plain` union failed typecheck once dev-server route-type stubs for these routes were first generated), `scripts/test-dashboard-api-auth.mjs` (pins paid-agent env to deterministic network-free states; new tokenless seller-route assertions plus a counter-assertion that the buyer-side wallet-spending `/api/hivemindos/models/chat/completions` stays 401-gated).
  - Summary: since the dashboard auth gate moved to `src/proxy.ts` (2026-07-03), external buyers hitting the paid seller endpoints — `/api/paid-agents/<slug>/chat/completions` (self-hosted x402 seller gateway) and `/api/official-paid-agents/<slug>/{chat/completions,credits/top-up,credits/balance,credits/checkout}` (official hosted proxy) — got a proxy-level 401 before the x402 payment flow could start, dead-ending the revenue rails. Payment IS the auth on these routes: the x402 handshake requires the first request to arrive credential-less to receive the 402 challenge (x402 settlement or X-HivemindOS-Credit-Token upstream). The routes fail closed on their own: seller-mode/enabled/config checks and payment verification run before any runtime work, the dev bypass stays triple-gated to non-production, and the official-* routes only relay allowlisted payment headers to the hosted gateway. Rate limiting was considered and deferred: the repo's only limiter (`src/lib/arcjet/user-rate-limit.ts`) is a no-op stub and unpaid requests do no expensive work before payment verification.
  - Verification: live dev-server delta on a scratch port — tokenless POST `/api/paid-agents/default/chat/completions` went 401 → 402 with a real x402 v2 `Payment-Required` challenge header (self-hosted + testnet facilitator config), tokenless GET readiness 401 → 200, control `/api/env` stayed 401. `pnpm test:api-auth` passes with the new assertions (re-run after the params type fix). Gate: 83/83 recorded baseline (concurrent sessions' entries today) → 82/83 (typecheck red surfaced by the probe runs generating route-type stubs for the pre-existing params union type) → 83/83 after the params fix; eslint clean on all touched files. NOT verified: a real paid settlement end-to-end (needs a funded buyer wallet), and the official hosted path against the real worker base URL.
  - Intended commit message: `paid seller endpoints: exempt from dashboard auth gate so external buyers reach x402/credit payment auth (+ regression test)`

- 2026-07-03 - Hermes `/capture/<agent>` crash fixed: demo-fixture localDataDir scrubbed everywhere + voice agent budget raised to fit the codex lane
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/lib/utils/agent-local-data-dir.ts` (`isPlaceholderLocalDataDir`/`normalizeAgentLocalDataDir` — rejects the `/capture/<id>` placeholder, sibling of the `*.capture.invalid` telemetry scrub), `dashboard-storage.ts` (`normalizeAgentProfile` scrubs localDataDir on every profile load/save), `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` (`sanitizeLocalDataDir` backstop at all 8 localDataDir read sites, so a poisoned profile from a stale client can never become `HERMES_HOME`), `voice-turn.ts` (`AGENT_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` 10s → 20s). Data fix (not in git): the 4 poisoned profiles (hermes-lead, codex-engineer, brain-sync, research-scout) in `~/.hivemindos/dashboard-state.json` `hivemindos.agentProfiles.v1` reset to empty localDataDir via POST /api/dashboard/state (pre-fix backup in session scratchpad), then re-mirrored to the vault Agents/ profiles.
  - Summary: Every hermes-lead chat turn crashed with `FileNotFoundError: mkdir '/capture/hermes-lead'` — the path was NOT derived in hermes: the remotion demo capture script (`remotion/gitlawb-integration-video/scripts/capture-real-ux.mjs`) seeds fake agents with `localDataDir: "/capture/<id>"` and these leaked into the real dashboard state via the localStorage seeding path; the collector then exported that as `HERMES_HOME`, and hermes died in pathlib mkdir at the unwritable filesystem root. Separately, even with the crash fixed, the queen voice agent lane aborted at its 10s budget: a warm hermes CLI turn on openai-codex/gpt-5.5 takes 8-13s end-to-end through /api/chat/agent-runtime (telemetry: agentTurnMs 10002 = abort + gpt-4o-mini fallback), so the budget now matches the existing 20s OpenAI budget. The hermes gateway default model was never touched (model stays agent-scoped via `-m/--provider`).
  - Verification: reproduced pre-fix (CLI with poisoned HERMES_HOME dies: `[Errno 30] Read-only file system: '/capture'`; brain-status file held the original Errno 2 traceback). Post-fix live: collector /chat with a still-poisoned agent payload returns a real reply (backstop works); POST /api/chat/agent-runtime with the vault hermes-lead profile streams the reply in ~11-12s; queen voice converse-stream telemetry shows agentTurnMs 12605 with NO openAiTurnMs (real gpt-5.5 reply, no fallback) and `queen-voice-brain-status.json` flipped to degraded:false, consecutiveFailures 0. Gate: 82/83 — the 1 red is typecheck on generated `.next-tauri-build/dev/types` paid-agents route stubs, pre-existing/concurrent (0 of the 4 errors touch this change's files); eslint clean on touched files.
  - Intended commit message: `agents: scrub /capture demo-fixture localDataDir (hermes mkdir crash) + fit voice agent budget to the codex lane`

- 2026-07-03 - ChatGPT OAuth as a first-class, fleet-shared provider (in-app sign-in; preferred over the API key for spoken turns)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/lib/services/openai-oauth.ts` (PKCE sign-in flow on the Codex CLI's public OAuth client — ephemeral localhost:1455 callback listener, never kills a port owner; token exchange/refresh with rotation persisted; `runOpenAiOAuthChatTurn` over the ChatGPT backend Responses API with SSE text deltas), new `src/app/api/openai-oauth/route.ts` (GET status, POST start/login-state), `voice-turn.ts` (direct lane provider `openai-oauth`, lazy import), `api/queen-bee/voice/route.ts` (brain resolver prefers ChatGPT OAuth for OpenAI-family selections serving codex-era models — incl. openai-codex agents, replacing the hermes-runtime detour when connected; `OPENAI_PREFER_API_KEY` overrides back to the key), `AgentSettingsCallsPanel.tsx` ("OpenAI (ChatGPT)" brain provider + connect/status block with in-panel sign-in button).
  - Summary: OAuth tokens are stored in the SHARED HIVE ENV (`OPENAI_OAUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN`/`_REFRESH_TOKEN`/`_ACCOUNT_ID`/`_EXPIRES_AT`) — separate from `OPENAI_API_KEY`, auto-replicating to every machine via hive-env tailnet sync; deliberately NOT in the brain vault (standing rule: vault notes carry credential status only, never values). HivemindOS runs its OWN OAuth grant and never touches ~/.codex tokens (refresh rotation would race the Codex CLI). Preference order for spoken turns: ChatGPT OAuth when connected and the model is codex-era (gpt-5*/o*/codex*) > API key > agent runtime for OAuth providers when OAuth is not connected; failures stay loud via the existing degradation alerts.
  - Verification: gate 83/83 → 83/83 (0 regressions), eslint clean. LIVE-verified without completing a login: status endpoint; start action returns a correctly-shaped PKCE authorize URL and binds the 1455 callback listener (404s non-callback paths); the auth token endpoint accepts our exact request encoding (structured invalid_request error for a garbage code). NOT verified (requires the user's browser login): the authorize page render (curl gets bot-protection 403 regardless of UA — expected), the code→token exchange with a real code, a real Responses-API turn on OAuth credentials, cross-machine env replication of the tokens, and refresh rotation over time. The flow constants (client id, endpoints, Responses event shapes) mirror the Codex CLI's public client and are marked as such in code — an unofficial surface that may change; all failures raise the queen-voice degradation alert.
  - Intended commit message: `openai oauth: in-app ChatGPT sign-in, fleet-shared tokens in hive env, preferred voice brain over API key`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen voice: configurable voice modes + chat brain (agent's model by default, custom override, UI in Calls settings)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/types/agent-runtime.ts` (`VoiceChatBrainPreference` on AgentCallPreferences + normalization), `voice-settings.ts` (`readQueenBeeBrainDefaults` — Queen agent's own provider/model, same 15s-cached read), `voice-turn.ts` (`VoiceChatBrainPlan` with three lanes — `direct` provider call, `agent-runtime` (pinned to the configured agent, REQUIRED for OAuth-held providers like openai-codex/copilot/xai-oauth so voice always uses the agent's own credentials, never a key substitution), and legacy `fleet-agent`; `runProviderConversationTurn` — provider-catalog/shared-env endpoint resolution, reasoning-model body `max_completion_tokens` + `reasoning_effort: low` with legacy-param retry; OpenAI fallback now rides the same helper), `api/queen-bee/voice/route.ts` (`resolveVoiceChatBrainPlan` from Calls prefs → both converse actions; OAuth-held providers plan as agent-runtime, key-based catalog providers as direct; GET adds `pipelineSelected`; new `openai-tts` cloud-voice pipeline runtime), `QueenBeeVoiceOverlay.tsx` (mode = pipelineSelected, back-compat localTtsSelected), `AgentSettingsCallsPanel.tsx` (voice runtime relabeled as modes incl. "Cloud voice (pipeline)"; new "Chat brain" picker — Agent's model (default) / Custom provider+model via /api/providers/models / Fleet agent — shown for pipeline runtimes).
  - Summary: There was previously NO UI for the pipeline voice LLM: converse turns silently used the ranked fleet agent then env-var-or-gpt-4o-mini. Now the Calls settings expose the three voice modes (realtime hybrid, cloud-voice pipeline, local-voice pipeline) and, for pipeline modes, which brain answers: the agent's own selected model by default (resolved server-side and called directly over the provider's OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint), an explicit voice-only custom override, or the legacy fleet-agent runtime lane. Failures of the chosen brain surface through the existing degradation alert/status (never a silent substitution) before the OpenAI fallback answers.
  - Verification: gate 83/83 → 83/83 three times (0 regressions), eslint clean on all touched files. LIVE-verified on the dev server: GET returns pipelineSelected for the local-tts Queen; the direct lane was proven end-to-end with gpt-5.5 over the OpenAI API (3/3 turns, directTurnMs 1503-3069, no fallback, degradation status self-cleared) during an interim build that aliased codex→API key — that aliasing was then REMOVED as a silent credential substitution, and the final build routes the Queen (Hermes Lead, openai-codex/gpt-5.5) through the agent-runtime lane on its own OAuth credentials: verified live (agentTurnMs 1100, pinned agent ran) and immediately exposed a REAL pre-existing hermes bug — the runtime crashes with `FileNotFoundError: mkdir '/capture/hermes-lead'` (absolute /capture path built inside hermes' Python; not present in ~/.hermes config files; also the likely cause of the historical 10s voice timeouts) — captured by the degradation alert, fallback answered. NOT live-verified: the Calls panel UI render in a browser (lint/typecheck only), the custom-override and openai-tts mode paths end-to-end, and non-OpenAI providers (groq/gemini/venice) on the direct lane. KNOWN OPEN: hermes /capture crash blocks the codex-OAuth voice brain until fixed (chip spawned).
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: voice modes + configurable chat brain (agent's model default, custom override, openai-tts pipeline runtime)`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen voice: silent model fallback is now loud (auth fix + alert + settings indicator)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/lib/utils/internal-api-auth.ts` (node-safe `internalApiAuthHeaders()` + `DASHBOARD_AUTH_HEADER` single source; `server-auth.ts` re-exports), new `src/lib/services/queen-bee/voice-brain-status.ts` (degradation status file `~/.hivemindos/cache/queen-voice-brain-status.json` + escalation-bridge alert, 6h re-alert TTL), `voice-turn.ts` (both internal `/api/chat/agent-runtime` fetches send the server device token; conversation success/failure now records brain status; `voiceFallbackModelName()` single source), `pilot-turn.ts` (auth header), `runtime-integrations.ts` + `dashboard-types.ts` (`queenVoiceBrain` on RuntimeIntegrationStatus), `api/runtimes/[runtime]/integrations/route.ts` (attaches status + diagnostic on local and proxied paths), `AgentSettingsModal.tsx` (warning block under the model picker when voice turns bypass the selected model).
  - Summary: The proxy auth gate (moved to src/proxy.ts earlier on 2026-07-03) made the queen voice/pilot internal self-fetches 401 in ~8ms, so every voice turn silently fell back to gpt-4o-mini while the UI showed the configured agent/model as selected — the operator believed gpt-5.5 was answering for hours with zero indication (fleet view, settings modal, and notifications all quiet). Fix is two-part: (1) internal server→server self-fetches authenticate with the server's own device token (browser callers hold a session cookie the server can't mint); (2) the fallback can never be silent again — a failed runtime conversation turn writes a degradation status (agent, error, fallback model, consecutive failures) and raises a TTL-deduped escalation (vault notifications feed + configured messaging channels), the agent's settings modal shows the status next to the model picker, and a successful runtime turn clears it.
  - Verification: gate baseline 82/82 → 83/83 (+1 concurrent session's suite, 0 regressions); eslint clean on touched files (modal is @ts-nocheck). LIVE-verified against the running dev server: pre-fix telemetry showed agentTurnMs 7-10ms (instant 401) on every real turn; post-fix a live converse-stream turn ran the real agent attempt (agentTurnMs 10005 — Hermes agent timed out at the 10s voice budget, a REAL failure now visible), wrote the status file, created the vault notification ("Queen Bee is not using your selected model"), and the integrations route returns queenVoiceBrain + diagnostic for that agent. NOT live-verified: the modal's warning block render in a browser (route contract + lint only) and external-channel (Telegram) delivery.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: authenticate internal self-fetches; surface model-fallback degradation (alert + settings indicator)`

- 2026-07-03 - Zero-human companies: autonomy driver self-recovery + honest failure handling (WEBS 7h-stall fix)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/company-autonomy-driver.ts` (self-port/self-base memory `rememberCompanyDriverSelfPort`/`rememberCompanyDriverSelfBase` + `resolveCompanyDriverSelfBases`, globalThis-backed; fleet snapshot + dispatch origin try last-known-good door then both loopback families; consecutive-empty-fleet escalation after 6 ticks), new `src/lib/services/queen-bee/worker-output-failure.ts` (`classifyRuntimeFailureOutput`) wired into `src/lib/services/queen-bee/autonomous-worker.ts` (failure-shaped worker text throws into the existing reroute→block→needs-human machinery instead of completing), `src/instrumentation.ts` (driver autostart retries forever — 60s cadence after the boot burst — and logs when PORT is unset instead of silently skipping), `src/app/api/company-autonomy-driver/route.ts` + `src/app/api/companies/route.ts` (routes remember the request's loopback host; companies GET calls `ensureCompanyAutonomyDriver()` when an autonomous company exists and returns `driver` health in the payload), `src/features/dashboard/views/zero-human-companies/ZeroHumanCompaniesView.tsx` (visible warning when the driver is down while companies are launched), `scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` (`checkCompanyAutonomyDriver()` after every cycle: lease dead-pid/15-min-staleness check, revive via any local dashboard on BOTH 127.0.0.1 and [::1], per-candidate failure diagnostics, `company-driver-down` alert; APP_PORTS default now includes 5122; knobs `FLEET_WATCHDOG_COMPANY_DRIVER=0`, `FLEET_WATCHDOG_DRIVER_STALE_MS`), new `scripts/test-worker-output-failure.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs`, self-port/base cases added to `scripts/test-company-autonomy.mjs`.
  - Summary: The Website Outreach Agency sat "running" for ~7h dispatching nothing. Stacked causes, all fixed: the driver's boot autostart gave up after 5 attempts/20s (slow-binding dev server) and nothing ever retried; the only driver-hosting process died and no other server ran a standby (the Tauri-spawned dev server on :5122 skipped autostart — no PORT env); even a running driver self-fetched its fleet snapshot via `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` which a `[::1]`-only server refuses, so ticks silently saw an empty fleet and dispatched nothing; the self-fetch ALSO 401'd once the API auth gate moved to src/proxy.ts (the driver and the company goal-planner's `/api/chat/agent-runtime` call sent no credential — both now ride `internalApiAuthHeaders()`); and Hermes runners whose model API was down returned the transport error as chat text, which autonomous pickup recorded as DONE — six fake completions poisoned company memory and the planner's dedup context. Recovery is now layered: persistent boot retry, every `/api/companies` poll revives a dead driver (any open dashboard self-heals), and the fleet-health-watchdog is the headless backstop that probes the lease and revives via whichever local dashboard answers. Failure-shaped worker output now reroutes across delegates and lands in needs-human with an escalation + a BLOCKED company-memory entry (learning) instead of a fake DONE.
  - Verification: gate baseline 82/82 → 83/83 (+1 new suite, 0 regressions; re-run after the self-base fix pending below). LIVE-verified on the real dashboard/board: `/api/companies` GET revived the dead driver twice during the session (12:28 via the app's own poll after hot-reload, 12:43 via a manual poll); two deliberate kill drills — watchdog detected the dead lease and revived the driver via the Tauri proxy `127.0.0.1:5021` from the shell (12:48:34) AND from the real LaunchAgent (12:50:49); the stuck task `t_mr4hb1ee_rrv9o` (claim expired 7h earlier) was reclaimed to ready on the driver's first revived tick. One launchd watchdog walk at 12:44:29 failed to find any dashboard for an unconfirmed reason (likely a transient proxy 503 window) — per-candidate failure diagnostics now log exactly why. NOT verified live yet: a full new dispatch→real-completion round trip on the re-routed task (runner confirmed healthy end-to-end via a live /chat probe returning real model output).
  - Intended commit message: `companies: autonomy driver self-recovery (persistent autostart, route/watchdog revive, loopback-family-safe self-fetch) + never record runtime failures as done`

- 2026-07-03 - Queen voice: "On it" ack no longer plays on every turn
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-bee-voice.ts` (ACK_PLAY_DELAY_MS 1400→4500; first streamed speech delta now cancels the ack timer, not just the first completed sentence chunk).
  - Summary: The pre-synthesized "On it. Give me a moment." ack was armed on a bare 1.4s timer per converse turn, but first spoken audio needs a full sentence + synth first-byte and typically lands ~2-3s — so the ack fired on essentially every message, including ones that weren't requests (and became audible everywhere once TTS stabilized, since the cached ack clip previously often failed to play). The ack now only covers genuine dead air: it plays when no speech text has streamed in within 4.5s (pre-speech tool calls, delegation routing); the first speech delta cancels it. Measured context: qwen synth TTFB is ~0.2-0.34s on the NYC box and ~0.7-0.9s through the linkd→collector→UTTS chain from Makassar, so first speech is dominated by the model's first sentence, not the synth.
  - Verification: gate baseline 82/82 → 82/82 (0 regressions; includes test:queen-clap + test:queen-voice-working). NOT verified live: the audible behavior (no ack on ordinary turns; ack still fires on a >4.5s pre-speech stall) needs a real overlay voice turn — user-verifiable immediately in the dev overlay.
  - Intended commit message: `queen voice: gate the "On it" ack on genuine pre-speech dead air`

- 2026-07-03 18:25 +0800 - Work Board: merge-friendly shard storage ends silent Syncthing conflict loss on kanban.json
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/lib/services/kanban/board-shards.ts` (shard engine), `src/lib/services/kanban/local-kanban-store.ts` (`readBoard`/`writeBoard` delegate to the engine; `archiveBoard` invalidates the fold cache), new `scripts/test-kanban-shards.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: The shared Work Board was a single hot kanban.json rewritten wholesale by every machine; Syncthing can't merge JSON, so concurrent cross-machine mutations forked the board into `kanban.sync-conflict-*.json` files that nothing ever read — the losing side's task completions silently vanished (12 conflict files had accumulated). Boards now use the same merge-friendly granularity as shared env and Agent Memory: one file per task (`shards/tasks/<id>.json`, last-writer-wins by `updatedAt`, tombstones for deletes so they never resurrect), per-machine append-only logs for events/comments/runs/links (`shards/logs/<machineKey>.jsonl`), and per-machine freshness stamps. kanban.json remains as a deterministic materialized snapshot, so legacy readers (packaged Rust bridge, external scripts, not-yet-updated fleet machines) keep working; anything that still writes kanban.json directly — old-store machines, agent-session edits, and Syncthing conflict copies of the snapshot — is lifted back into the shards on the next read, and absorbed conflict files are deleted. Migration is lazy and one-shot on first read; kanban.json doubles as the rollback artifact (old code just keeps using it). Kill switch: `HIVEMINDOS_KANBAN_SHARDS=0` restores single-file behavior. Known limits (named, accepted): hard-deletes performed by an old-store machine don't propagate to sharded machines (a truncated fork must never tombstone live tasks), cross-machine last-writer-wins rides `updatedAt` so it trusts NTP-synced clocks, and same-millisecond divergent edits of one task keep the incumbent until the next real edit.
  - Verification: gate baseline 81/81 → 82/82 (+1 the new `test:kanban:shards`, 0 regressions); the new hermetic suite covers legacy migration, shard writes, external-snapshot lift, conflict-copy merge+delete, tombstone no-resurrect, cross-machine folds, byte-stable rematerialization, and the kill switch; existing `test:kanban:concurrency` + loop-store suites pass through the engine; REAL runtime verified — the running dev server hot-reloaded and lazily migrated the live vault Work Board (202 task shards match the 202 tasks; 10 real conflict files merged and deleted; the two remaining `sync-conflict*`-named entries are `.bak` backups of old conflicts, correctly ignored; snapshot parses with capped history; a subsequent store read is byte-stable). NOT verified live: other fleet machines still run the old store until they update (their whole-file writes are the lift path's job), and multi-machine convergence has only been exercised by the hermetic cross-machine fold test, not on the real fleet.
  - Intended commit message: `kanban: merge-friendly shard storage for Work Board (per-task files + per-machine logs, snapshot compat, conflict lift)`

- 2026-07-03 - hive-env-add: single-quote values round-trip (writer/reader quoting asymmetry fixed)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/hive-env-add` (`quote_env` now emits double-quoted backslash-escaped values when the value contains a single quote or newline instead of shlex concatenation; `parse_env_text` double-quote branch decodes escapes via an explicit decoder — python-dotenv's escape set plus `\xNN`/`\uNNNN` — replacing the `unicode_escape` round-trip that mangled non-ASCII and raises DeprecationWarning on unknown escapes under modern Python; single-quote branch now undoes legacy shlex concatenation via `shlex.split` so already-written values read back intact; `update_env_file` rewrites legacy-concatenated lines on any same-value touch so files heal; `--stdin` strips one trailing newline, matching the getpass branch), `src/lib/services/hive-env-write.ts` (`encodeEnvAssignments` emits the double-quoted escaped form for single-quote values; the loud rejection of single-quote values is removed now that they round-trip), new `scripts/test-hive-env-roundtrip.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: `quote_env` used `shlex.quote`, which encodes `don't` as shell concatenation (`'don'"'"'t'`), but `parse_env_text` only strips one outer quote pair — so every env value containing a single quote corrupted on read-back through every hive-env-add path (CLI, `--stdin`, `--import-stdin`, tailnet sync push/pull, dashboard `/api/env`), and live-tested hermes (python-dotenv) and `@next/env` could not parse the concatenated form at all. Values with a single quote (or newline) are now stored double-quoted with backslash escapes — the same format the collector's `JSON.stringify` peer-push payloads already used — and all other value classes keep their previous on-disk form byte-identical. Legacy concatenated lines decode correctly on read and are rewritten on the next touch of the key. Residual (named, not a regression): values combining a single quote with a backslash or double quote decode correctly through hive-env-add, python-dotenv, and shell, but the naive strip-one-quote-pair JS parsers and `@next/env` keep the backslashes (those values were entirely unreadable before); `${VAR}`/`$VAR` interpolation by python-dotenv and `@next/env` on values containing `$` is pre-existing dotenv semantics in every quote style, untouched here.
  - Verification: gate baseline 80/80 → 81/81 (+1 the new round-trip suite, 0 regressions); repro confirmed pre-fix (`K=don't` → on-disk `K='don'"'"'t'` → read back `don'"'"'t`); post-fix round-trip suite covers CLI/`--stdin`/`--import-stdin` writes, `--export-json` and `hive-env-run` read-back, on-disk format, and legacy-line healing for values with `'`, `"`, spaces, `#`, `$`, backslash, `=`, unicode, newline; new on-disk format live-parsed by the hermes venv's python-dotenv 1.2.2 and the repo's `@next/env` 16.2.6.
  - Intended commit message: `hive-env-add: single-quote values round-trip (double-quoted escapes replace shlex concatenation)`

- 2026-07-03 17:19 +0800 - Fleet apps feed: peer app URLs ride the peer's linkd tailnet door, and the watchdog verifies advertised TTS bases
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/app/api/fleet/apps-normalize.ts` (new `peerPortalToTailnetUrl` + `pinPeerAppProxyUrl` + `HIVEMIND_LINK_TAILNET_PORT`), `src/app/api/fleet/apps/route.ts` (`toHostedApp` composes `apiBaseUrl`/`healthUrl`/signature probes on the peer's own tailnet door instead of the localhost-scoped `/peer/<host:port>` portal — `openUrl` keeps the portal for browser HTML rewriting; new `repairCachedAppUrls` in the disk-cache read path heals stale bases), `scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` `discoverTtsApps` (new `ttsCatalogAlive` + `resolveTtsBase`: a dashboard-advertised TTS base is verified via `/v1/models` and, when dead, rebased to the same host's pinned :8787 linkd door or a same-host machines-list base; kept as-is when nothing answers so real outages still alert), new `scripts/test-fleet-apps-tailnet-url.mjs` registered in `package.json` + `test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: The apps feed advertised NYC's Universal TTS as `http://<nyc-ip>:8792/app-proxy/8799` — the peer's local COLLECTOR port, dead from the tailnet since the 2026-07-03 linkd audit pinned the fleet door to :8787 and left collectors localhost-scoped. Two mechanisms fed it: fresh peer apps were composed on whatever base answered the probe (including the localhost-only `/peer/…` portal, which the watchdog rightly filters out), and the disk cache kept resurrecting pre-audit `:8792` entries because the dedupe scoring (richer cached entries, shorter-URL tie-break, the "Hivenet app host" penalty) re-elected them over fresh ones and wrote them back — a self-renewing loop. The watchdog trusted the advertised base, cached it, and produced false "TTS down / restart FAILED" alerts against a healthy service. Now peer `apiBaseUrl`/`healthUrl` always carry the tailnet-reachable door, stale cached URLs heal on read, and the watchdog independently validates what the dashboard advertises before probing or remediating against it.
  - Verification: gate baseline 79/79 → 80/80 (+1 the new suite, 0 regressions; an earlier baseline run had 2 flaky reds that vanished on immediate re-run before any changes); live-confirmed the premise (`:8792/apps` connection-refused from the tailnet, `:8787/apps` + `:8787/app-proxy/8799/v1/models` both 200) and the fix (dev dashboard :5021 hot-reloaded, `/api/fleet/apps?fast=1` now advertises `http://<nyc-ip>:8787/app-proxy/{8766,8799,8802}` for all three NYC TTS apps from the previously poisoned cache); `resolveTtsBase` exercised as shipped code against the live fleet (stale `:8792` → rebased to live `:8787` with a log line; live base kept; dead host with no live rebase kept for alerting). NOT verified live: the running watchdog LaunchAgent still executes pre-fix code until restarted (left alone — the same file carries a concurrent session's uncommitted TTS-probe + linkd-staleness work); `~/.hivemindos/fleet-health-watchdog-tts.json` still holds the stale base until the restarted watchdog's first healthy discovery overwrites it; other machines' dashboards keep serving old URLs until they pick up this change.
  - Intended commit message: `fleet apps: peer app URLs ride the linkd tailnet door (:8787) + watchdog verifies advertised TTS bases`

- 2026-07-03 17:14 +0800 - Env hardening: shared-env writers routed through hive-env-add (lock + meta + sync); remaining exclusive-file writers atomic
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/hive-env-write.ts` (rewritten: batch `writeSharedHiveEnvValues` over `--import-stdin` — one spawn per store instead of one per key; `writeSharedHiveEnvValue` now a wrapper; new `ensureSharedHiveEnvPlaceholder`; values validated — no empties, control chars, or single quotes), `scripts/hive-env-add` (new `--ensure-placeholder KEY` mode: adds `KEY=` only if absent, under the store lock, deliberately never stamping updatedAt meta — a fresh timestamp on an empty value would win pull-reconcile on peers and delete their real token; plus `write_json` — openclaw stores + `env-sync-pending.json` — now `atomic_write_text` instead of `write_text`), `src/app/api/usepod/host/setup/route.ts` (`saveUsePodHostEnvValues` → `writeSharedHiveEnvValues`; local upsert/quote helpers deleted), `src/app/api/fleet/hetzner/env/open/route.ts` (`HCLOUD_TOKEN=` placeholder → `ensureSharedHiveEnvPlaceholder`; direct writes removed), `src/lib/services/miroshark/companion-client.ts` + new `src/lib/utils/atomic-env-file.ts` (`writeEnvFileAtomicSync` temp+rename for MiroShark's exclusively-owned env files, which hive-env-add does not manage).
  - Summary: Follow-up to the 2026-07-03 `.env.local` corruption audit, second pass. The usepod setup and hetzner env-open routes previously did unlocked in-place rewrites of `~/.hivemindos/.env` — racing hive-env-add and the collector's 10-minute env-sync (torn files, lost updates, and meta-less writes that env-sync could overrule with a peer's older value). They now route through `hive-env-add`, gaining the sidecar lock, atomic write, per-key updatedAt meta, encrypted backup, tailnet sync, and runtime mirrors. Fleet note: usepod host keys already replicated fleet-wide via pull-reconcile (absent-locally keys are pulled unconditionally), so sync-on-write is faster propagation with correct timestamps, not a new exposure. Pre-existing flaw found and named, NOT fixed here: `quote_env` (shlex-style) and `parse_env_text` (strip-one-quote-pair) disagree, so any value containing a single quote corrupts on read-back through every hive-env-add path; the TS writer now rejects such values loudly instead of corrupting.
  - Verification: gate 79/79 → 80/80, exit 0 (the +1 is a concurrent session's `test:fleet-apps-tailnet-url`; all baseline suites still pass); `test-env-sync-queue.mjs` + `test-hive-env-remove.mjs` pass; `py_compile` clean; hermetic functional smoke of the new paths against scratch stores (placeholder: creates file 0600 with `KEY=`, byte-identical no-op when key exists, preserves neighbors, rejects `KEY=value`/non-generic runtimes, stamps NO meta; `--import-stdin` with the exact TS payload: values with spaces/`#`/`$`/`"` round-trip exactly, meta stamped); eslint clean. NOT verified live: usepod/hetzner flows not exercised end-to-end (need live services); packaged desktop builds that don't ship `scripts/hive-env-add` next to the server cwd will now fail these writes loudly (same constraint as the existing clawbank/x-mcp writers) instead of writing unlocked.
  - Intended commit message: `env hardening: route shared-env writers through hive-env-add (batch import, locked placeholder mode); atomic writes for exclusive env files`

- 2026-07-03 16:08 +07 - Zero Human Companies: fleet-replicated definitions, config audit trail, governance proofs, and a company↔code-project link
  - Status: Pushed

To prevent duplicate fleet-wide dispatch, each company has a home machine (claimed automatically on migration/create/Launch); only that machine's autonomy driver re-dispatches it. An id missing from the registry stays selectable so opening + saving can't silently unlink it.

- 2026-07-03 - Fleet: stale-linkd alert and installer rebuild-skip share one criterion — did linkd SOURCES actually change
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `scripts/lib/linkd-staleness.mjs` (shared criterion: `git diff --quiet <binaryStamp> <checkoutCommit> -- cmd/hivemind-linkd go.mod go.sum scripts/build-hivemind-linkd.sh`; returns true/false/null-indeterminate; commit stamps regex-gated before becoming git argv), `scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` `checkLinkdBuild` (alerts only when the diff proves a rebuild would change the binary; indeterminate — a commit unknown to this clone — logs once a day instead of alerting; benign stamp-trails-HEAD logs once per commit pair), `scripts/install-telemetry-collector.sh` new `linkd_sources_unchanged_since` (bash reimplementation of the same criterion replacing the stamp==HEAD equality; unknown/pre-stamp/missing-commit stamps fail closed to rebuild), new `scripts/test-linkd-staleness.mjs` (hermetic temp-git-repo suite incl. the Ubuntu case, change+revert, go.sum bumps, argv-injection inputs, and a bash/mjs path-list drift guard), registered in `package.json` + `test-gate.mjs`.
  - Summary: The two halves of the 2026-07-03 stale-linkd feature disagreed. The watchdog alerted whenever the binary's stamp differed from the checkout HEAD — but after any commit that touches no linkd source, every healthy box legitimately keeps its old stamp, so the fleet false-alarmed daily (live ~08:23Z: hivemindos-ubuntu-8gb-hel1-2 alerted "built b535fb9, checkout 09dfaa9" though nothing between them touched linkd). Meanwhile the installer's b535fb9e skip (stamp==HEAD) meant every `update-hivemindos.sh` run paid a full Go rebuild + linkd restart (the ~5-10min :8787 gap) even when linkd hadn't changed. Both now key off the same question — did anything `go build ./cmd/hivemind-linkd` consumes change between the stamped commit and the checkout — so the alert fires exactly when a rebuild is needed and the installer rebuilds exactly then too. Premise correction vs the task report: the committed b535fb9e skip compared stamp==HEAD (it did NOT skip on unchanged sources — a rerun at 09dfaa9 would have rebuilt), so "rerun can never clear the alert" held only under the intended criterion; the daily false alarms between pushes and reruns were real either way.
  - Verification: gate baseline 77/77 → 78/78 (the +1 is the new suite); `node --check` + `bash -n` clean; real-history replay through the new code: b535fb9→09dfaa9 = current (no alert, installer skips), b8335c5a→09dfaa9 = stale (6a7ee9fa touched cmd/hivemind-linkd between; alert fires, installer rebuilds), unknown/empty/absent commits = indeterminate (no alert; installer rebuilds); the installer's actual bash function exercised on the same cases and agrees. NOT verified live: the running watchdog still executes the pre-fix code until restarted (left alone — the same file carries a concurrent session's uncommitted TTS-probe work); fleet boxes pick the fix up on their next update.
  - Intended commit message: `fleet: stale-linkd alert + installer skip share one source-diff criterion`

- 2026-07-03 15:40 +0800 - Installer: linkd rebuild-skip keys off the binary's commit stamp, not mtimes
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/install-telemetry-collector.sh` `build_hivemind_linkd_if_enabled` only.
  - Summary: The watchdog's new stale-linkd detection compares the binary's embedded commit stamp against the machine's checkout, but the installer skipped rebuilds by file mtimes — so a checkout that advanced without touching Go source kept an older-stamped binary forever and alerted daily (observed live on the VPS within an hour of shipping the detection: built 6a7ee9f vs checkout f0f7398). The skip check now uses `hivemind-linkd -version` vs `git rev-parse --short HEAD`; pre-stamp binaries always rebuild.
  - Verification: `bash -n` clean; the stamp-extraction sed verified against the real local binary; VPS + NYC reinstall run after push (rebuild fired, stamps match checkout, watchdog stale alert clears).
  - Intended commit message: `installer: skip linkd rebuild by commit stamp, not mtime`


- 2026-07-03 16:20 +0800 - Skills: kill the recurring aeon-* duplicate-mirror plague at the root (no doubled-prefix minting, all-roots guard, vault→aeon GC)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `brain-skills.ts` (`nextDestinationSlug` never re-prefixes an already provider-prefixed slug — collision counters the existing base; `pathLooksInsideProviderSkillRoot` matches any `/.aeon/` subtree instead of only `/.aeon/skills/`, closing the 3-of-4-roots hole; `syncSharedBrainSkillsToAeon` gains a GC pass removing MANAGED mirrors whose vault source is gone + refuses to project doubled-prefix `aeon-aeon-*` shelf entries; `removed` added to `BrainSkillAeonSyncResult`), `test-skill-mirror-loop-guard.mjs` (three new end-to-end cases incl. the June-14 generator reproduction: name-keyed dedup drops a slug from the shared maps while its dir remains on disk → import collision → old code minted `aeon-aeon-*`, new code counters `aeon-<x>-2`), `check-file-sizes.mjs` (+50 documented allowance).
  - Summary: The fleet has repeatedly regrown tens of thousands of `aeon-aeon-*`/`aeon-aeon-aeon-*` skill dirs (16k June 14; 28,210 on the NYC Mac 2026-07-03, where the collector's skills scan over them caused the fd-exhaustion `spawn EBADF` crash-loop that took down linkd and the Queen's voice). A 4-reader code hunt pinned the mechanism: the collector's skill auto-sync (fs.watch + 10s poll, autoImport ON since June 1) feeds runtime inventories to reconcile; `nextDestinationSlug` was the sole minter (blindly prepends `${provider}-` on collision); `syncSharedBrainSkillsToAeon` projected the whole shelf verbatim back into `~/.aeon/skills` with NO GC, re-firing the watcher seconds later — `-173/-199` counters = ~200 loop iterations. The June guards work (vault stayed clean) but ALL cleaners were vault-side only, so runtime dirs kept the residue forever and every cleanup looked like it "came back". Fixes: minting doubled prefixes is now impossible; prefixed slugs under ANY aeon root are recognized as recursive mirrors; the vault→aeon sync garbage-collects managed mirrors whose shelf source vanished (this organically cleans every machine's residue on its next sync) and never projects doubled-prefix entries. Manual quarantines tonight: NYC 28,210→2,442 and This Mac 1,548→411 (`~/aeon-skills-dupe-backup-2026-07-03` on each; rollback = move back). Not Syncthing (it only syncs the vaults); Liam's machine-to-machine spread instinct confirmed via collector-inventory merge → vault → per-machine mirror sync.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` 77/77 green (loop-guard suite grown 1→4 scenarios; each new case discriminates — old code mints `aeon-aeon-collide`/imports the plugins-root mirror/lacks `removed`); typecheck clean. Not verified live: the GC pass against a real machine's residue (next aeon sync on any machine exercises it — watch `removed[]` in the sync result); the fd-burst reduction on NYC is observational (28k→2.4k dirs) pending the collector crash-proof session's bounded-concurrency scan.
  - Intended commit message: `fix(skills): kill recursive aeon mirror plague — no doubled prefixes, all-roots guard, vault→aeon GC`

- 2026-07-03 13:42 +0800 - Security: dashboard API auth gate actually runs now (middleware.ts was silently ignored)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `src/proxy.ts` (the auth gate, moved verbatim from the repo-root `middleware.ts` and renamed to Next 16's proxy convention — function `middleware` → `proxy`, same self-authenticating allowlist and `/api/:path*` matcher), DELETED root `middleware.ts`, `scripts/test-dashboard-api-auth.mjs` (new anonymous probe of `GET /api/notifications` — a route with no in-route `requireAuth`, so the test now fails if the proxy file ever stops loading; every pre-existing probe hit routes that self-enforce auth, which is why the suite stayed green while the gate was dead), stale-reference cleanups in `scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` (comment) and `docs/for-users/architecture/generated-media-signing.md`.
  - Summary: The dashboard's API-wide auth check never executed — in any environment. `middleware.ts` sat at the repo root, but with a `src/` layout Next only discovers `src/middleware.ts`/`src/proxy.ts`; traced in Next 16.2.6's own source, dev (`setup-dev-bundler.js` watches `path.join(pagesDir||appDir, '..')`) and build (`build/index.js` scans the same dir, non-recursively) both resolve to `src/`, so the packaged desktop's embedded standalone server was equally ungated, not just dev. Every `/api` route without its own `requireAuth` call (e.g. `/api/notifications`) answered credential-less requests with 200. The gate now lives at `src/proxy.ts` (Next 16 deprecates the `middleware` filename; proxy always runs the Node.js runtime, which `verifyAuth`'s Web-Crypto-only code supports). Embedded builds ship it: `output: "standalone"` compiles the proxy into the server tree that `scripts/tauri-build.mjs` copies wholesale into resources. The static desktop build (`output: "export"`) tolerates the proxy file with a warning — it has no API routes by design.
  - Verification: baseline recorded pre-fix: `pnpm test:api-auth` green (proving its probes never covered the gate) while a fresh dev server served anonymous `GET /api/notifications?limit=1` → 200. After: `pnpm test:api-auth` green including the new probe; live verify dev server `127.0.0.1:5031` (real `.env.local` credentials): anonymous `GET /api/notifications?limit=1` → 401, device-token → 200, `POST /api/auth/session` (self-auth allowlist) → 200, `GET /` page → 200; server request logs explicitly time `proxy.ts` on `/api` requests; dev `middleware-manifest.json` regenerated with the entry. Full gate: baseline 77/77 (this morning's entry) → 77/77 green, `gate PASSED`. eslint clean on changed files. NOT verified by running: a full packaged desktop build (standalone staging confirmed by code trace of `tauri-build.mjs` `completeTurbopackStandalone` + `copyEmbeddedNextResources` only) and the static-export build (warn-not-throw confirmed in Next's `export/index.js` source only). Externally-reachable deployments of the old code remain ungated until rebuilt.
  - Intended commit message: `fix(security): move dashboard API auth gate to src/proxy.ts — root middleware.ts was never loaded by Next`

- 2026-07-03 13:28 +0800 - Fleet watchdog: escalates to a human when restarts stop working (dashboard urgent alert + Telegram)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `scripts/lib/fleet-watchdog-escalation.mjs` (pure escalation policy: per-target deep-failure streaks, remediation-attempt gating, time-gated repeats, deep-recovery reset, alert formatting), `scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` (v3: tracker wired through the probe loop; escalations go to the existing Telegram alert rail AND as an `urgent`/`alert` entry to the dashboard notifications feed via POST `/api/notifications` on the first responding local dashboard port; local dashboard calls now send `HIVEMINDOS_DASHBOARD_DEVICE_TOKEN` resolved env → `~/.hivemindos/.env` → checkout `.env.local`, same order as `scripts/hivemind-mcp`), new `scripts/test-fleet-watchdog-escalation.mjs` (12 checks: tracker behavior + source-contract wiring guards), `package.json` + `scripts/test-gate.mjs` (suite registered; gate 76 → 77).
  - Summary: During the 2026-07-03 NYC incident, the watchdog (and Universal TTS's own keepalive) kickstart-looped a machine-wide MLX synth deadlock for hours — health endpoints stayed green, every deep synth probe failed, and no human was told until the Queen voice overlay went mute. The watchdog now tracks, per target, consecutive confirmed deep-probe (severe) failures across remediation attempts — restarts do not reset the streak; only a passing deep probe does, because cheap probes stay green through a wedged backend. After `FLEET_WATCHDOG_ESCALATE_AFTER` (default 3) consecutive deep failures with at least one restart attempt, it raises a human-visible escalation naming the machine, the last probe error, and the consecutive-failure count; while the target stays wedged it re-escalates every `FLEET_WATCHDOG_ESCALATE_REPEAT_MS` (default 30 min), and announces recovery once a deep probe passes again. Applies to both TTS synth and collector chat deep probes (same failure shape). Escalation reaches the dashboard's notifications feed (bell + urgent badge, vault-persisted so it survives the dashboard being closed at the time) — the same feed the escalation bridge uses — plus the watchdog's existing Telegram rail and its log.
  - Flaw found, filed not fixed (out of scope): the dashboard's API auth middleware never executes — `middleware.ts` sits at the repo root while the app lives under `src/` (Next 16 only loads `src/middleware.ts`/`src/proxy.ts`); a fresh dev server served `/api/notifications` HTTP 200 with no credentials despite auth being configured, and `verifyAuth` 401s all credential-less requests, so the middleware provably never ran. Background task chip spawned. The watchdog sends the device token anyway — a no-op today, required the moment the middleware is fixed.
  - Verification: baseline `pnpm test` 76/76 green exit 0 (recorded before any edit) → after: 77/77 green exit 0 (delta: +test:fleet-watchdog-escalation, no regressions). `node --check scripts/fleet-health-watchdog.mjs` OK; `pnpm exec eslint` clean on all four changed/new script files; new suite green standalone (12/12). Real-path proof on isolated verify dev server `127.0.0.1:5031`: POSTed the exact watchdog escalation payload → `ok:true`, `urgentUnread` 0→1, entry listed in the feed with machine + error + counts; test entry's vault file then deleted and the feed confirmed back to `urgentUnread: 0`. NOT verified live: a real end-to-end escalation on a wedged fleet machine (needs the next real incident or a staged wedge), Telegram delivery (needs `FLEET_WATCHDOG_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` configured), and rollout — running watchdog LaunchAgents keep the old code until restarted.
  - Intended commit message: `fleet-watchdog: escalate persistent deep-probe failures to dashboard alerts + telegram after N failed remediations`
