- 2026-07-03 11:15 +0800 - Queen voice: a prefs-store hiccup can no longer swap her selected local voice for a cloud voice
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `dashboard-state.ts` (new `readDashboardStateStrict` — read/parse failure of an existing state file throws instead of masquerading as empty state; lenient `readDashboardState` now wraps it, behavior unchanged), `agent-profile-store.ts` (new `readStoredAgentProfilesStrict` — store outage or corrupt profiles value throws instead of reading as "no profiles"), `voice-settings.ts` (`readQueenBeeCallPreferences` uses the strict read; on failure serves last-known-good prefs from the process cache, or throws typed `QueenCallPreferencesUnavailableError` when no good read exists yet; failures no longer poison the 15s TTL cache; test hooks `resetQueenBeeCallPreferencesCache` + injectable `now`), voice route (`speak` returns the 503 `voiceUnavailable` outage envelope on prefs outage — overlay shows muted text + notice; `speak-stream` returns 409 fallback so the buffered path owns the decision; `speak-prewarm` skips with `call-prefs-unavailable`), `test-local-tts-robustness.mjs` (scratch-HOME isolation + 5-case call-prefs continuity section).
  - Summary: Closes the deferred chip from the 2026-07-03 sentence-streaming entry ("a transient prefs-read failure can serve the cloud voice to a local-voice user"). The root cause sat two layers below the reported symptom: `readDashboardState()` swallowed every read/parse failure of the ~26MB state file into empty state, so a transient store hiccup surfaced as "no queen profile" → "local TTS not selected" → the route silently voiced the reply in an OpenAI voice, and the failure-derived null was cached for 15s — the route-level `.catch(() => null)` the symptom was attributed to never even fired. Now an outage is distinguishable from "cloud voice selected" end to end: a failed read first serves the last-known-good prefs (stale beats a substituted voice; the store is retried on every later call), and only a cold-start outage with no good read yet reaches the caller — where `speak` reports the same voiceUnavailable envelope as a local-TTS outage (reply stays on screen as text) instead of guessing cloud. A genuinely missing state file (fresh install) still reads as "no prefs" → default cloud voice, unchanged. Trade-off accepted: during a cold-start store outage a cloud-voice user's reply is also muted rather than spoken — indistinguishable from a local-voice user without readable prefs, and muting is the continuity-safe side. Residual (pre-existing, named not fixed): the GET settings probe keeps its lenient fallback, so a cold-start outage when the overlay opens can still route the session to OpenAI Realtime; the per-turn speak gate above caps the blast radius.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` baseline 76/76 green → after 76/76 green (`gate PASSED`; `test:local-tts-robustness` grown with the continuity section: cold-start corrupt store → typed outage error; healthy read resolves local voice; post-TTL corrupt store → last-known-good served; corrupt profiles value → outage; missing file → null/fresh-install). Suite runs against a scratch `HOME`, never the real `~/.hivemindos`. Not verified live: the route-level 503 envelope under a real store outage (would require corrupting the shared dashboard-state file on a running server; the envelope shape and overlay muted-text handling are the same contract the 2026-07-02 voice-continuity work already exercised).
  - Intended commit message: `fix(queen-voice): treat call-prefs store outage as voiceUnavailable, never a substitute cloud voice`

- 2026-07-03 02:30 +0800 - Queen voice: sentence-streaming TTS — she starts speaking the first sentence while the rest of the reply is still being written
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: voice route (new `converse-stream` NDJSON action), `voice-turn.ts` (onSpeechDelta/onSpeechReset threading, streaming OpenAI fallback + `readOpenAiSseText`), `runtime-voice-turn.ts` (`readRuntimeResponseText` gains an `onTextDelta` sink + now REJECTS on runtime error frames instead of silently truncating — the internal throw was self-caught dead code; all call sites verified throw-tolerant), `voice-speech-stream.ts` (new `createVoiceSpeechEmitter`; first production wiring of the extractor/chunker; `minFirstChunkChars` default fixed 12→4), pipeline voice hook (NDJSON consume + `createChunkSpeaker` serial chunk playback with one turn-spanning barge-in watcher), new `converse-stream.ts` (orphan-safe shared-pump NDJSON reader), new `spoken-reply-playback.ts` (playback ladder extracted verbatim from the hook for the 1500-line size gate), `test-queen-voice-working.mjs` (emitter, NDJSON reader, OpenAI SSE reader, error-frame propagation suites).
  - Summary: The overlay used to wait for the ENTIRE model reply before any TTS started (converse resolved as one buffered JSON, then speak). The new `converse-stream` action extracts the `"speech"` JSON field while the model streams (runtime SSE deltas on the preferred path; the OpenAI fallback now streams too) and emits NDJSON `speech`/`reset`/`done` events; the client cuts them into TTS-sized sentence chunks (first sentence ships alone) and plays each through the existing local-stream→buffered→browser→muted ladder, so first audio tracks the first sentence instead of the full reply — on delegation turns that's multi-second perceived-latency savings. Contract: concatenated `speech` events = exactly what the buffered path would have spoken (task receipts arrive as a late suffix after submission; non-JSON replies speak whole at the end; a failed model attempt emits one `reset` that stops its audio mid-word and discards its queue). Barge-in mid-generation snaps back to listening immediately (pre-roll ring holds only ~5s) while a background drain finishes captions/history; the server keeps running to completion so an in-flight task delegation still lands. Chunk boundaries recalibrate the barge-in echo floor (the pinned underrun-regression shape); punctuation-only chunks are filtered client-side (they'd synthesize to zero bytes and trip the healthy-engine breaker); once a chunk goes muted the rest of the reply stays text (no mid-reply voice switch). Captions now fill word-by-word as she composes. Pre-existing flaw fixed: `createSentenceChunker`'s `minFirstChunkChars` default (12) contradicted its own gate test ("On it." = 7 chars ships alone) — the suite was RED on baseline; default is now 4 and the gate is green. Legacy `converse` is untouched (the client falls back to buffered handling if the response isn't NDJSON). A 21-agent adversarial review pass then hardened five paths: a dead stream (network drop / 75s timeout) now stops the chunk queue and barge-in watcher instead of playing over the error state and the next listening turn; a barge-in's queen history entry is reserved in order at detach time (a late background push used to land AFTER the next user turn, misattributing the reply for the rest of the session); the buffered-fallback branch clears the ack timer so "On it" can't talk over a fast reply; chunks cut by a mid-play attempt reset no longer over-claim audibility; and the voice-continuity rule now also covers the browser-synth branches (network hiccup / suspended context / decode failure mid-reply goes text-only for local voices, never a third voice). Accepted trade-off: a task-submission failure AFTER the model's spoken "kicking that off" surfaces as an error banner — the spoken claim cannot be unspoken. Deferred as follow-up chips (pre-existing): the runtime SSE reasoning filter can drop real tokens like "First," from displayed+spoken text; a transient prefs-read failure can serve the cloud voice to a local-voice user.
  - Verification: baseline `pnpm test` 75/76 (red: `test:queen-voice-working`, the pre-existing chunker default bug) → after: 76/76 green, exit 0 (suite grown with emitter, NDJSON-reader race-safety, and OpenAI-SSE cases; `test:queen-clap` source-regex pins intact). Live on isolated dev :5035 through the real route: `converse-stream` returned 15 incremental speech deltas before `done` (first delta +2694ms, done +2979ms), concatenation === final reply verbatim, telemetry line `stage:"converse-stream" firstSpeechMs:2660 totalMs:2950` landed — and the probe organically exercised the runtime-attempt-fails→OpenAI-streaming-fallback chain with no spurious reset. Overlay E2E (Playwright, fake mic) passed against :5035, re-run green after the review fixes and the playback-ladder extraction; the live probe re-run after the fixes also streamed deltas correctly, and the full gate stayed 76/76. Not verified live: a real runtime-brain streaming turn (this Mac's runtime attempt failed in 1.2s and is now in 5-min cooldown), a task-delegation receipt suffix spoken end-to-end, the reset path audibly, and everything by-ear (inter-chunk gaps, prosody discontinuity between per-sentence synths — the engine gets no `utterance_id` join smoothing on this path; if seams annoy, that's the next knob).
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice: sentence-streaming TTS — speak the first sentence while the reply writes`

- 2026-07-03 10:16 +0800 - Chat thinking bee no longer freezes mid-animation
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/components/ui/lottie-player.tsx` (rAF-liveness heartbeat + timer-driven frame fallback; TEMP-BEE-DIAG beacon removed).
  - Summary: The Lottie bee in the chat "thinking…" loader played briefly then sat frozen. The committed TEMP-BEE-DIAG beacon captured the root cause from the live desktop webview: during the idle wait the player reports `isPlaying: true` but `currentFrame` stays pinned (its `requestAnimationFrame` render loop never gets scheduled — WKWebView starves rAF while the page is otherwise idle, waking only when streaming DOM churn begins), while plain timers keep firing dead-on. The existing watchdog restarts could not help — a restarted player still depends on the same starved rAF. LottiePlayer now keeps a rAF heartbeat; when the loop watchdog sees no rAF callback for >450ms it pauses the core and drives frames itself from a ~30fps `setInterval` via `setFrame()` (which renders synchronously, no rAF needed), re-arming a rAF probe each tick and handing back to native playback the moment rAF comes alive. Applies to every looping autoplay LottiePlayer (fleet bees, loaders), not just the chat bee. The temporary diagnostics (localhost:8920 beacon) are removed now that they've served their purpose. Rollback: revert `lottie-player.tsx`.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green 76/76 (baseline 74/76; the two baseline reds were a concurrent session's mid-edit `use-queen-bee-voice.ts`, fixed by them — no step touched by this change regressed). Playwright against dev :5121 on a temporary `/lottie-smoke` page (removed after): normal rAF 6/6 distinct sampled canvas frames; `requestAnimationFrame` stubbed dead → 10/10 distinct frames over 5s (fallback carried the animation; 187 swallowed rAF requests prove both native chains were dead); rAF restored → 6/6 distinct (continued animation here can only be native playback, since the fallback self-terminates once rAF is alive — handback confirmed). Not verified in the packaged Tauri app itself — the harness reproduces the diagnosed condition (dead rAF, live timers) exactly, but a real-app thinking turn is the user-side confirmation.
  - Intended commit message: `fix(ui): timer-driven Lottie fallback when the webview starves rAF`

- 2026-07-03 01:35 +0800 - Queen voice: stop Bankr hijacking every spoken turn; continuous mic capture (no lost words at turn boundaries)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: agent-runtime `messages.ts` (`bareUserRequestText` unwraps the flattened voice prompt), `stream-openai-compatible.ts` (tool offers key on the bare utterance), `bankr-actions.ts` (word-anchored intent regexes), pipeline voice hook (session-long mic pump + PCM pre-roll ring buffer, barge-in pre-roll hand-off, stale prewarmed-socket guard), `test-bankr-actions.mjs`, `test-queen-voice-working.mjs`, `test-queen-clap-activation.mjs`.
  - Summary: Telemetry showed EVERY pipeline voice turn dispatching a real ~8s Bankr agent job within ~10ms of the request ("Uh nothing much" → "Using bankr_action"): the flattening fix made the persona+history the routed user text, and the persona sentence "(a job, build, fix, research, automation, reminder…)" matched the Bankr rail's `\bautomation` intent — the rail hijacked the turn before Hermes ever ran (also the ~8s "Let me check…" stall). `unwrapLatestUserRequest` (the FAB briefing unwrapper) now also strips the voice prompt down to the "User's latest spoken message:" line for ALL money rails, and tool offers test the same bare utterance, so casual turns fall through to the model while genuine "swap $5 usdc on bankr" utterances still rail (and their drafts now quote the bare utterance, not the persona blob). Adjacent pre-existing bug fixed: half-anchored regex alternations matched substrings — "broadcast" contains "dca", "alphabet" ends in "bet" — every intent alternative is now word-anchored (plurals kept). Separately, mic capture is now continuous: one session-long pump fills a 5s PCM pre-roll ring buffer through thinking/speaking; when listening resumes the frames captured while the STT socket was opening are flushed in first (no more "Your turn" delay swallowing opening words), a barge-in flushes the ~0.9s that triggered it (the interrupting words are transcribed instead of the user repeating themselves), a mid-listen socket drop recovers the last 1.5s, and a prewarmed socket that went stale during a long turn is replaced instead of adopted dead (previously wedged listening silently). Speech during the THINKING phase is still not consumed (deliberate — the turn is already committed); barge-in during playback is the interrupt path.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green 76/76 vs 76/76 baseline (bankr suite grown with anchored-regex + persona-sentence cases, queen-voice-working grown with a builder↔unwrapper round-trip contract, clap guard grown with the continuous-capture contract). Live on an isolated :5033 dev server through the real `/api/chat/agent-runtime` route: flattened "Uh nothing much" → `voice.fast_path.dispatch`, zero bankr telemetry events (was `bankr_action.started` in ≤15ms); flattened "swap $5 of usdc to eth on bankr" → `bankr_action.draft` only, card quotes the bare utterance, nothing executed. Not verified by ear: pre-roll flush timing on a real barge-in (worst case is today's behavior — the head of the utterance missing), and whether flushed audio around a barge-in picks up residual speaker bleed as ghost words (AEC should suppress; report if her words appear in your captions).
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice: unwrap spoken utterance for rails/tools; continuous mic capture with pre-roll`

- 2026-07-02 23:40:40 PST+0800 - Queen voice: stop stutter chopping and gap-triggered self-interruption
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Realtime PCM worklet (rebuffer-on-underrun + partial-stream flag), barge-in detector (gap recalibration), pipeline voice hook (playback-activity plumb, partial outcome + notice, broadened playback beacon), voice route beacon fields, detector regression tests.
  - Summary: A reply stuttered syllable-by-syllable ("I-m r-r-eady...") then stopped mid-sentence (reported 2026-07-02 ~23:31 during an NYC recovery window: prewarm 19.3s, upstream opens 1.2-1.5s — the box generated slower than realtime under load). Two mechanisms fixed. (1) The worklet used to resume the instant any chunk arrived, leaking a silence gap between every chunk of a slower-than-realtime feed; it now REBUFFERS after an underrun (240ms, growing to 720ms with repeats) so a slow feed produces a few clean pauses instead of machine-gun chopping. (2) Those same gaps broke the barge-in echo floor — calibrated on gap silence, her resumed voice read as sustained user speech and self-interrupted playback (this also explains the missing playback beacon: it skipped aborted turns). Playback gaps now feed a shared activity channel that re-enters echo-floor calibration for 1s around every underrun, covered by regression tests including one documenting that the un-recalibrated floor self-triggers. A stream that dies mid-reply after >=2s now surfaces "Her voice cut out mid-reply - the full reply is on screen" instead of stopping silently, and the speak-metrics beacon now reports aborted/partial turns too (those are the ones worth diagnosing).
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green 76/76 (detector suite grown to 8 scenarios incl. the gap-recalibration regression pair), `pnpm typecheck` exit 0, focused ESLint clean. Root cause grounded in the session's own telemetry (15:31Z: 19.3s prewarm, 1.2-1.5s upstream opens, absent playback beacon). Not verifiable from this session: the audible result (needs speakers/mic and the NYC box in its loaded state) — worth one voice turn during a busy period; the worklet rebuffer thresholds (0.24s/x3 growth) are the tunable if pauses feel wrong.
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice: rebuffer underruns, recalibrate barge-in around gaps`

  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Local TTS resolution/caching (new `local-tts-app-cache.ts`, `local-tts.ts`, `connected-apps.ts`), collector `/apps` caching (`agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` + new `scripts/lib/hosted-apps-cache.mjs`), Queen voice route stage-timing telemetry + `speak-metrics` beacon, call-prefs read cache (`voice-settings.ts`), pipeline hook start buffer + playback beacon, new adaptive barge-in detector (`barge-in-detector.ts`), size-ratchet allowance note, and regression coverage.
  - Summary: Stream opens measured 5.6-12.5s in real sessions while the TTS box served first bytes in ~0.3s; new per-stage telemetry (prefs/config/appResolve/upstreamOpen + a client playback beacon) pinpointed connected-app DISCOVERY as the cost (~10s fleet-wide sweep whenever the 5-minute app cache lapsed). Fixes measured live: every successful synth now slides the app-cache TTL (warm sessions never re-discover — warm stream opens dropped to 633-928ms, all app-chain stages 0ms); resolving a KNOWN app id now queries its own machine's collector directly (port variants raced, cached app list instead of a forced rescan) cutting cold resolution from ~10-12s to ~4.5s; resolved apps persist to `~/.hivemindos/cache/local-tts-apps.json` (7-day cap, evicted on synth failure) so restarted servers skip discovery entirely once seeded; the collector's `/apps` now serves a 60s-cached sweep (`?refresh=1` still rescans) — fleet collectors pick this up on their next update/restart; Queen call prefs are cached 15s (the ~26MB dashboard-state parse was costing ~75-90ms several times per turn); and the client jitter buffer dropped 220ms -> 140ms. Barge-in no longer self-interrupts: a pure adaptive echo-floor detector calibrates on Queen's own speaker bleed during a 600ms grace window and only treats sustained energy well above that floor (>=2.75x, 420ms) as the user interrupting; knobs in `BARGE_IN_TUNING`.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green — 76/76 including expanded `test-queen-voice-working` (six echo-floor detector scenarios: steady loud echo never triggers, speech over quiet and loud echo triggers after sustain, transients ignored, grace window inert, muted mic inert) and `test-local-tts-robustness` (persist path isolated to a scratch file), typecheck exit 0, `node --check` on the collector, size guard green after extracting the app cache to its own module (+documented +5 collector allowance). Live measurements against the NYC Universal TTS host on an isolated dev server: warm `speak-stream` opens 633-928ms (`appResolveMs 0`), cold-with-direct-host ~4.5s (was 10.7-11.9s), and during an NYC flap the failure path evicts the cached app + trips the breaker as designed. Not verified live: the persisted-cache cold start (needs one successful synth to seed — NYC was flapping at session end) and the retuned barge-in by ear.
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice: fast TTS resolution, cached /apps, echo-floor barge-in`

- 2026-07-02 23:02 +0800 - Zero Human Companies: cut autonomous task churn (dedup + progress-gated cadence + busy-but-blocked alert)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new `company-task-dedup.ts` (pure), `companies-orchestration.ts` (`dispatchCompanyGoal` dedup + `deduped` result), `company-autonomy-driver.ts` (progress-gated re-dispatch + stalled-progress escalation), new `scripts/test-company-task-dedup.mjs`.
  - Summary: The driver re-planned the broad apex goal every ~30 min once the board drained, and the planner re-proposed the same steps each cycle, so the board filled with redundant tasks. Three fixes: (1) DEDUP — the driver passes the company's recent (≤24h) + in-flight task titles into dispatch; `dedupeDrafts` drops planned tasks that substantially match (token Jaccard ≥ 0.6 or exact normalized title), precision-biased so it never drops genuinely-new work; an explicit manual Launch stays force-fresh. (2) PROGRESS-GATED CADENCE — if the last batch drained without completing anything, the re-dispatch interval widens ×4 (~2h) until something completes. (3) BUSY-BUT-BLOCKED — a company with ≥8 completed tasks but zero apex-metric progress raises a 12h-TTL escalation ("producing work but the goal isn't moving — clear the blocker"). Env knobs: `HIVEMINDOS_COMPANY_DEDUP_WINDOW_MS` (24h), `_DRIVER_NOPROGRESS_BACKOFF` (4), `_STALL_MIN_DONE` (8). Rollback: revert the three source files; behavior returns to blind 30-min re-dispatch. Note: the live :5031 dev driver is a boot-time background loop — it only picks up these changes on the next server restart.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` 76/76 (typecheck + `test:company-task-dedup`). On live company df5c0f4a: 126 done tasks / $0 of $5k → the busy-but-blocked escalation fires; feeding 3 re-proposed tasks (2 real repeats + 1 new) against the 126 board titles dropped both repeats (verbatim and reworded) and kept the new one. Not verified at runtime: the cadence backoff over multiple real ticks (deterministic filter/arithmetic, typecheck-clean).
  - Intended commit message: `fix(zhc): dedup re-dispatched tasks + progress-gate cadence + busy-but-blocked alert`

- 2026-07-02 21:47:14 PST+0800 - Queen voice continuity: never substitute a different voice for a selected local voice
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Queen voice API route (`speak` fallback), pipeline voice hook (muted outcome, outage notice + soft cue, voice-consistent ack cache), voice overlay + styles.
  - Summary: Replies no longer alternate between the selected local cloned voice and OpenAI `nova` when the Universal TTS server flaps (reported today; telemetry showed voice01 → HTTP 502 → nova for each ~45s breaker window). With a local voice selected, the `speak` fallback now returns a `voiceUnavailable` outage report instead of cloud TTS; the overlay keeps the session going with the reply as text, one soft descending cue at outage start, and a visible "voice server unreachable — replies show as text" notice that clears automatically on the next spoken reply (prewarm probes already re-close the breaker when the server recovers). Browser speech synthesis (a third, also-wrong voice) is skipped for this case too. The "On it" ack clip now only caches audio in the RIGHT voice — with a local voice selected it refuses a cloud-voice mp3 (which previously could get cached during a flap and ack in the wrong voice all session) and retries on later warm turns until a local WAV lands. Cloud fallback behavior is unchanged when no local voice is selected. Rollback: delete the voice-continuity early-return in `streamSpokenReply` to restore cloud fallback for local-voice users.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green (75/75 suites), `pnpm typecheck` exit 0, focused ESLint clean on the three touched files. Live smoke on an isolated dev server against the currently-flapping NYC Universal TTS host: first `speak` returned 503 `{voiceUnavailable:true}` after the real 10.9s local attempt (engine "none", skipped "voice-continuity" telemetry — no nova synth), an immediate second `speak` returned the same in 107ms and `speak-stream` 409 in 79ms (breaker fast-fail), confirming muted-not-substituted behavior end to end. Not verified by ear: the soft outage cue and notice rendering in the live overlay.
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice: voice continuity over cloud fallback for local voices`

- 2026-07-02 21:50 +07 - Loop eval gate verifies claimed-live URLs (no more passing with a dead/fabricated link)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: src/lib/services/loops/loop-runner.ts (live-URL integrity check + injected `probeUrl`; reserved URLs no longer count as artifact evidence; run integrity check even with no/optional gates); loop-engine.ts (`loopCompletionBlock` blocks on a hard-fail receipt regardless of `required`); loops/index.ts (exports); queen-bee/autonomous-worker.ts (real bounded prober wired into pickup); extended scripts/test-loop-runner.mjs + scripts/test-loop-completion-blocking.mjs.
  - Summary: A company task could be marked done claiming "deployed a live payment page" with a link that never resolves — the operating-unit loop's outcome/receipt gates are self-report (`required:false`) and never probed a claimed-live URL. Two live examples that passed: `https://demo.sarasota-sites.example/paid?session_id=mock_…` (RFC-2606 `.example` domain, never resolves) and `https://cal.com/sarasota-sites/website-kickoff` (real host, nonexistent username → 404). Fix: `runLoopGates` now finds URLs the worker presents as live customer deliverables (payment/booking/preview/deploy-host shapes, or any URL near live-claim language; third-party docs excluded) and verifies them — reserved/example/mock/non-public hosts are rejected with NO network (option b, always on), and real deliverable URLs are probed via an injected fetcher, treating only a definitive not-found (404/410/NXDOMAIN) as fabrication so slow/gated/rate-limited hosts (timeout, 401/403/429/5xx) never block (option a). A violation writes a stable-id hard-fail receipt that blocks completion → needs-human even though the company loop's gates are all optional; a clean retry overwrites it and completes. Generic across companies, bounded (≤6 URLs/run, 5s/probe), and hermetic (the runner never touches the network — the prober is injected; kill switch `QUEEN_BEE_LIVE_URL_PROBE=0`).
  - Decisions made without asking: (1) the check blocks even no-loop / all-optional-gate tasks — a MISSING receipt stays non-blocking (the deliberate company-loop design), but affirmatively-FALSE evidence (a dead claimed URL) blocks; (2) the gate's reserved-host check is intentionally stricter than the UI/extractor siblings — it also flags `*.example.com` subdomains and private/loopback/link-local/metadata hosts (which also closes an SSRF path: those are never probed). The two sibling copies (`isPlaceholderUrl`, `isNonRoutableDeliverableUrl`) were left as-is — one is being edited by a concurrent session — so the reserved-host logic now exists in three near-identical places; converging them onto the new canonical `isReservedOrMockUrl` is a follow-up.
  - Verification: full `pnpm test` gate 74/74 PASSED (typecheck + guards + suites) in 19.1s; `tsc --noEmit` clean. New hermetic cases (injected fake prober, no real network): pure `isReservedOrMockUrl` matrix incl. private/metadata hosts; reserved claimed-URL → hard-fail with no prober; cal.com-style 404 → hard-fail; 200 → pass; timeout/403/429/503 → NOT blocked; NXDOMAIN → blocked; artifact gate rejects a reserved-only URL; stable-id retry-overwrite; docs/reference URL not treated as a live claim; and an end-to-end company-loop (all-optional gates) task that blocks to needs-human on a dead URL then completes on a fixed retry. Not verified at runtime: the real `fetch`-based prober against a live host (the production path) — only the injected fake was exercised in tests; the DNS `ENOTFOUND` → `dnsFailed` mapping relies on undici's `error.cause.code` and is inferred, not observed here.
  - Intended commit message: fix(loops): verify claimed-live URLs in the eval gate so dead/fabricated links can't pass

The pending-task re-router (added with the deadlock fix) ranked a stranded `company:{id}:` task against the WHOLE fleet instead of the company's members, so when the crew was momentarily busy the task got picked up by the globally-best available agent — including a Venice-provider agent, which then failed on Venice's API rate limit and leaked a Venice docs link into the company's deliverables. Live: re-verified all 6 members are openai-codex/gpt-5.5 via the fleet (HermesMain+Ada on This Mac, Emerson/Grace Hopper/Ida/Octavia on the Ubuntu box); the 5 mis-routed tasks are all `done` (historical). - 2026-07-02 20:20 +07 - Company-autonomy driver: exactly one active driver per machine (lease election)
  - Status: Pushed

  - Summary: instrumentation.ts auto-starts the autonomy driver in EVERY Next server process, and a dev box routinely runs several at once (pnpm dev, tauri dev, verify servers) — so N drivers independently ticked the same shared Work Board every 5 minutes: duplicate fleet scans, duplicate reclaim/redispatch passes, and potentially duplicate real agent dispatches (an 8.6s agent-runtime call was observed firing on a dev-server boot). Now a machine-wide lease file (~/.hivemindos/company-autonomy-driver.lease.json, same wx-create + stale-takeover shape as the veil transfer lock) elects exactly one active driver: standby instances poll the lease (tiny file read, 60s) instead of ticking, take over within ~1 minute when the holder dies (pid-liveness ESRCH check), within 15 minutes if it wedges while alive (renewal staleness), and immediately on graceful stop (release). The packaged app (one embedded server) behaves as before. Knobs: HIVEMINDOS_COMPANY_DRIVER_LEASE=0 disables (pre-lease behavior), _LEASE_FILE, _LEASE_STALE_MS, _STANDBY_POLL_MS. Deliberately per-machine: multiple MACHINES' app servers still each run a driver against the vault-synced board (pre-existing, mitigated by board state transitions) — cross-machine election would need a shared-store lease and is out of scope.
  - Verification: extended hermetic suite covers acquire/renew (startedAt preserved, renewedAt advances), live-foreign-holder blocking, refusing to release another's lease, dead-pid takeover, stale takeover, corrupt-file self-heal, own release, and the kill switch — test:company-autonomy PASSED. Live two-server proof on this box: servers on 5041+5042 (second via the verify-server HIVEMINDOS_TAURI_NEXT_DIST_DIR pattern; Next refuses a second dev server on the same distDir) → A acquired and ticked (tickCount 1), B logged "standby — active driver is pid 64059 (port 5041)" with tickCount 0 (previously BOTH would tick on boot); killed A (no release, lease left with a dead pid) → B logged "lease acquired" and flipped to held:true/tickCount 1 in 18 seconds. Status API on both servers showed the lease field throughout. Full `pnpm test` gate 73/73 PASSED after (baseline earlier today also 73/73).
  - Intended commit message: feat(companies): machine-wide lease elects one active autonomy driver across concurrent servers

Queen Bee is now interruptible in pipeline mode: a barge-in watcher runs during playback (sustained speech ~2x the VAD floor for 350ms after a 450ms grace, echo-cancelled capture) and aborts HER playback only — the reply stops and the session snaps back to listening; interruption was previously impossible (nothing monitored the mic while speaking). Live "you" captions keep animated finalizing dots while the trailing transcription words land (they arrive seconds late from the STT model; `delay: minimal` is already set server-side — the dots make the lag read as work, not a dropped word).

Live smoke on an isolated dev server (`127.0.0.1:5033`): a real converse turn with the re-greeting repro history returned "I'm ready to assist you with whatever you need..." (no re-greeting), while concurrent `turn-progress` polls streamed live stages ("Thinking with Hermes" running → done → "Thinking with OpenAI"). Today's real-session telemetry located the 10s dead-air anatomy (10s empty Hermes attempt + 8.4s OpenAI + 3s fleet scan on the delegation turn). Not verified by ear/mic in this session: ack clip playback, barge-in behavior against real speaker echo, and chip rendering in the live overlay — needs one real voice session.

- 2026-07-02 13:42:15 PST+0800 - Make Queen voice local TTS play immediately and fail fast
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Queen voice overlay local TTS playback (`use-queen-bee-voice.ts`), shared realtime PCM worklet player, Local TTS synth/stream helpers, new Local TTS health/breaker/prewarm module, Queen voice API route, and a new hermetic regression suite.
  - Summary: The overlay's local TTS streaming now plays through the same jitter-buffered AudioWorklet player the local TTS calls use (220ms lead-in, underrun-tolerant, no per-chunk seams) instead of ad-hoc buffer scheduling, and reuses the session's already-unlocked AudioContext. Turns can no longer hang or go silently unplayed: a 15s first-audio watchdog abandons a wedged stream for the buffered path, a suspended AudioContext now falls back to audible browser synthesis instead of hanging (previous behavior) or playing silence, browser synthesis reports whether speech actually started, and a reply that every engine failed to voice surfaces an on-screen error instead of pretending it spoke. Failure cascades are cut by a 45s per-app circuit breaker shared by all Local TTS consumers — a stream/synth/discovery failure makes the immediate buffered fallback (and following turns) skip local TTS and go straight to the cloud voice instead of re-paying 10-30s of doomed timeouts (telemetry showed up to ~24s of silence). New `speak-prewarm` action + overlay pings (on open and per reply, 45s-throttled, server-deduped) warm the selected model during the think phase so cold model loads (measured 5-30s) stop delaying first audio; a prewarm success also re-closes the breaker as the recovery probe. A stream that dies after >=2s of audio finishes what arrived instead of re-speaking the reply from the top.
  - Verification: `pnpm test` gate green — 71/71 suites including the new `test:local-tts-robustness` (breaker trip/expiry/reset, synth wiring, prewarm dedupe/skip/failure), `pnpm typecheck` exit 0, focused ESLint clean on all six touched files, size guard exit 0. Live round-trips on an isolated dev server (`127.0.0.1:5033`) against the real NYC Universal TTS host: `speak-prewarm` performed a genuine 21.5s cold-load warm-up (`warmed:true` telemetry), warm `speak-stream` calls then returned real 24kHz PCM with server first-byte at ~4-5s, and telemetry logs the new `speak-prewarm` stage. Also re-confirmed live that `voice01` on `qwen3-tts-0.6b-custom` returns ~5-6s of audio for the input "Hi." — the voice-clone reference-clip leak is a Universal TTS server bug (documented in CHANGELOG_ARCHIVE), and that clone path emits no audio until generation ends, so warm first-audio stays ~4-5s until the TTS server fixes clone streaming. In-overlay audible playback (worklet path, watchdog, browser-synth fallback) type/lint-verified but not exercised by ear in this session.
  - Intended commit message: `Queen voice local TTS: worklet playback, breaker, prewarm`

- 2026-07-01 02:15:57 +0800 - Setup wizard: stop the renderer pegging a CPU core on GPU-less Windows (freeze / no progress / late modal)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/features/native/NativeFirstRunOnboarding.module.css`, `src/features/native/NativeFirstRunOnboarding.tsx`, `src-tauri/src/setup.rs`
  - Summary: On a GPU-less Windows host (cloud VPS / RDP / VM, where WebView2 falls back to CPU software rendering) the first-run setup wizard froze the window into "Not Responding", never showed live progress, and opened after the daily/weekly popups. Root cause, traced through the actual render path: the setup modal's own CSS was the only continuous renderer work — a full-screen `backdrop-filter: blur()` scrim (re-rasters the whole screen every frame) plus two infinite `box-shadow` glow animations (`cellPulse`, `ping`, each a per-frame blurred-shadow repaint). The dashboard behind it has no idle animation (queen-voice RAF is gated on an active call, the bee-flight RAF self-terminates when settled), so those modal effects were the entire cost that pegged one core. Fixes: (1) drop the backdrop blur for a near-opaque dim gradient; (2) convert the two glow animations from animated `box-shadow` to animated `opacity` (composite-only — same breathing/lit look, ~free); (3) open the wizard inline in its effect instead of via `setTimeout(…, 0)`, which on a busy main thread yielded the open behind the other popups; (4) the hidden-setup stdout throttle now always flushes the final line (it could drop the last/most-important activity line if it landed inside the 150 ms window). Helps every low-end / remote-desktop user, not just the test VPS; negligible visual change on GPU machines.
  - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean on the changed TSX (0 errors in the file; the repo's other baseline type errors are unrelated and `next.config` sets `ignoreBuildErrors: true`). Freeze traced through real call chains and CPU-sampled on the VPS earlier (renderer at 100% of one core, node server idle). NOT yet confirmed on-device after the fix — the decisive test is installing the v0.2.27 prerelease on the GPU-less Windows box; the Rust throttle change compiles in CI.
  - Intended commit message: `Setup wizard: stop renderer CPU peg on GPU-less Windows (freeze/progress/late-open)`

- 2026-07-01 16:05:43 +0800 - Windows setup: collector crashed on a -SkipDeps box (missing bonjour-service) so /health never came up and the wizard hung on "Working…"
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `scripts/agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`
  - Summary: After the UTF-8 fix let setup stream + finish, the wizard still sat on "Setting things up… / Working…" forever. Root cause found on the live VPS: the agent telemetry collector (a node script the setup registers + starts) crashed immediately with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package 'bonjour-service'`. The app-driven setup runs with `-SkipDeps`, so app-source has NO node_modules, and the collector's one third-party dep (`bonjour-service`, used only for LAN mDNS advertising) was imported at the TOP LEVEL — so the whole collector died at module load, `/health` never answered, and the wizard's "wait for collector ready" gate never resolved. Fix: import `bonjour-service` LAZILY inside `advertiseHubMdns()` (its only use, already wrapped in try/catch) instead of at the top of the file. A missing package now just disables mDNS advertising; the collector boots normally and `/health` responds. Every other collector import is a `node:` builtin or a local `.mjs`, so this was the only blocker. Ships via the app-source `main` pull — no app release.
  - Verification: On a live Windows Server 2022 VPS with no node_modules — BEFORE: `node agent-telemetry-collector.mjs` exited 1 with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND 'bonjour-service'`; AFTER: collector runs (no crash) and `GET /health` returns 200. This is the last blocker in the Windows first-run chain (streams → no hang → Node installed → collector installs → collector reachable → wizard completes).
  - Intended commit message: `Windows collector: lazy-load bonjour-service so a -SkipDeps install doesn't crash the collector`

- 2026-07-01 15:06:56 +0800 - Windows setup: force UTF-8 output so the app's progress reader doesn't die on ✓/✗/↑ glyphs (the real "freeze + no updates")
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `setup.ps1`
  - Summary: THE root cause of the Windows first-run "freezes, no status updates, Not Responding at the last step" that survived the earlier fixes — found by reproducing the app's exact reader on a live VPS. The app streams setup progress by reading the hidden run's stdout one line at a time and decoding each line as UTF-8 (`spawn_hidden_setup` in src-tauri/src/setup.rs used `.lines()`, which yields an Err and STOPS at the first non-UTF-8 byte). Under the default console code page, setup.ps1's status glyphs (✓ ✗ ↑ from Ok/Fail/brain-sync) are written as non-UTF-8 bytes (e.g. 0xFB). So the reader died on the very first ✗/✓ line → no more progress emitted, and the still-running setup process then failed with "The process tried to write to a nonexistent pipe" (the read end was gone), stalling the wizard. Entirely GPU-independent; the earlier CSS/renderer theory was wrong. Fix: setup.ps1 now sets `[Console]::OutputEncoding = UTF8` (and `$OutputEncoding`) at the very top, so every line is valid UTF-8 and the reader drains cleanly to completion. Ships via the app-source `main` pull — fixes the installed v0.2.27 with no rebuild. (The durable belt — a lenient `from_utf8_lossy` byte reader on the app side — is queued for the next app release.)
  - Verification: Reproduced + fixed on a live Windows Server 2022 VPS. A strict UTF-8 line reader (byte-identical to the Rust `.lines()` reader) STOPPED after 0 lines on the raw output (`Unable to translate bytes [FB]`); with the encoding fix it read ALL 91 lines of a full real setup with 0 choke, 0 "nonexistent pipe", exit 0, and the collector scheduled task registered.
  - Intended commit message: `Windows setup: force UTF-8 stdout so the app progress reader doesn't choke on glyphs`

- 2026-07-01 12:05:00 +0800 - Windows setup: stop the hidden run hanging on prompts + auto-install Node so the collector installs
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `setup.ps1`
  - Summary: Two real bugs found + fixed on a live Windows Server VPS while testing the app-driven first-run setup (the app runs setup.ps1 HIDDEN from `~/.hivemindos/app-source` via the launcher, re-downloading it from `main` each run — so this ships with NO app release). (1) HANG AT LAST STEP: the launcher runs `setup.ps1` without `-NonInteractive`, so `Ask-YesNo` falls through to `Read-Host`, which BLOCKS FOREVER on the hidden run's console-less stdin — setup was observed stuck ~24 min at a prompt (a live hung `pwsh` PID), surfacing in the app as a "Not Responding" freeze at the last step with no progress. Fix: setup.ps1 now auto-enables `$NonInteractive` when it's app-driven (script path under `.hivemindos\app-source`) or has no interactive console (`[Console]::IsInputRedirected` / `-not [Environment]::UserInteractive`), so every prompt takes its safe default instead of blocking. A human running setup.ps1 in a real terminal is unaffected. (2) COLLECTOR NEVER INSTALLED ON A FRESH BOX: the agent telemetry collector is a node script (`scripts\agent-telemetry-collector.mjs`), but `Ensure-Node` only offered a winget install; on a winget-less box (Windows Server) with the prompt now auto-declined, Node stayed missing → `$Missing` → setup exited at the required-deps check BEFORE the collector install. Added `Install-NodeWindows` (mirrors the existing `Install-PythonWindows`): winget first, else the official nodejs.org LTS MSI (22.11.0), silent, adds to PATH. This node exit was previously MASKED by the hang.
  - Verification: Validated END-TO-END on a real Windows Server 2022 VPS (no winget), running setup.ps1 exactly as the app does (hidden `cmd`->`pwsh`, no stdin, `-SkipDashboard -SkipBuild -SkipDeps`). BEFORE: unpatched run hung 24 min at the first `Ask-YesNo`. AFTER: streamed past every prompt live, auto-installed Node 22.11.0, ran brain-sync + MCP registration, registered the "HivemindOS Telemetry Collector" scheduled task (State: Ready), and exited 0. (Collector `/health` is only up after next logon — pre-existing logon-task behavior, unchanged.)
  - Intended commit message: `Windows setup: auto-non-interactive + Node auto-install so the hidden app-driven run completes`
